## [Implicit Neural Representation Boosts Hyperspectral Video Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31435) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31435) proposes extending an existing RGB video compression model with implicit neural representation (INR) for hyperspectral video, achieving Bjøntegaard Delta PSNR gains of +4.99 dB and BD-rate reduction of -88.88% versus traditional frame-by-frame hyperspectral image compression. It also improves downstream object tracking on the HOT2026 dataset, raising tracking AUC by up to 23.42% and distance precision by up to 35.56% in low-data regimes compared to PCA and JPEG2000 baselines. Hyperspectral video is becoming more available through snapshot cameras, but compression remains immature, so this work addresses a growing bottleneck for large-scale datasets. By showing that better compression also improves downstream tracking, it suggests compression should be evaluated not only by reconstruction quality but also by task performance, which could influence future codec design in specialized imaging fields. The method is a novel extension of an existing RGB video compression model rather than a from-scratch architecture, and the reported gains are measured against traditional hyperspectral image compression applied frame-by-frame. Downstream evaluation uses object tracking success on the HOT2026 dataset, with the largest improvements observed in low-data regimes relative to PCA- and JPEG2000-based compression.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Implicit neural representations (INRs), also called neural fields, are neural networks that map continuous inputs such as spatial coordinates and time to continuous outputs, representing signals like images, video, or 3D scenes directly in network weights rather than as discrete pixels or frames. Hyperspectral video captures many spectral bands per pixel over time, producing very large data volumes that are challenging to store and transmit. Bjøntegaard Delta metrics (BD-PSNR and BD-rate) are standard ways to compare rate-distortion curves between two compression methods, summarizing average quality or bitrate differences across operating points.

## [ViSTA Adapter Bridges Clinical Time-Series into Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31448) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced ViSTA, a compact adapter that injects irregular numerical clinical measurements into a pretrained vision-language model's chart representations by learning corrections to visual tokens while keeping all pretrained parameters frozen. On MIMIC-IV, the 2-billion-parameter model with only 0.516 million trainable parameters achieved an AUROC of 0.7376 for acute kidney injury, nearly matching GPT-5.6 Sol's 0.7380, and reached 69.27% accuracy on temporal question answering at 4 billion parameters. This work shows that clinical risk prediction and flexible temporal question answering can be unified within a single multimodal LLM using a very small number of trainable parameters, potentially lowering the cost of deploying medical AI. It suggests that pretrained vision-language models can be efficiently extended to structured numerical prediction tasks, which could benefit hospitals and researchers working with electronic health records. ViSTA trains only 0.516 million parameters, over 90% fewer than low-rank adaptation (LoRA) using charts or numerical text, with a 2.82-4.88 percentage-point accuracy gap on temporal question answering. The approach was evaluated across models with 2-9 billion parameters on MIMIC-IV, though the abstract is truncated and full experimental details remain to be verified.

## [Medical VLM Post-Training Audit: Accuracy Gains Can Hurt Visual Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31450) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled study on Qwen2.5-VL-3B using PMC-VQA found that language-model LoRA supervised fine-tuning improved correct-image accuracy by only +1.10 percentage points (95% paired bootstrap CI: -0.85 to +3.05), while visual-benefit events dropped by 2.40 points and image sensitivity fell by 5.60 points. The audit also compared broader multimodal adaptation, standard answer-only GRPO, and a counterfactual evidence objective, finding that broader adaptation underperformed and that GRPO's clean-test accuracy change remained uncertain. This work shows that answer accuracy alone can mask degraded image-conditioned decision-making in medical VLMs, meaning models may appear better while relying less on the actual medical image. It argues for visual utility metrics beyond accuracy, which could affect how medical AI systems are evaluated and deployed in clinical settings. On 2,000 clean-test questions, paired records revealed 155 acquired and 203 lost visual-benefit events, and a generation audit showed that canonical option scores can follow a different token path from generated answers. When scores were taken along the greedy generation path, the evidence target improved on the training set, but its gains over standard GRPO remained inconsistent on validation data at matched training doses.

## [First Federated Learning Framework for Infant Movement Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31463) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present the first federated learning framework for automated infant movement analysis and General Movement Assessment (GMA) using video-derived skeletal motion data, evaluated on fidgety movement classification. They also propose Uncertainty-Aware Federated Averaging (UA-FedAvg), which uses Monte Carlo (MC) Dropout predictive entropy to weight client contributions during aggregation. Most existing deep learning approaches for infant movement analysis rely on centralized training, which is often impractical in clinical settings due to privacy, governance, and data-sharing constraints. This framework enables privacy-preserving multi-institutional collaboration, potentially making automated early identification of neurodevelopmental disorders like cerebral palsy more feasible across hospitals. Experiments used a cross-subject evaluation protocol under a three-client federated learning setting, showing that federated learning substantially improves classification performance over independently trained local models and approaches centralized training performance. UA-FedAvg and its variant incorporating validation loss generally outperformed conventional FedAvg across the evaluated data-split configurations.

**Background**: Federated learning is a distributed machine learning technique where multiple clients collaboratively train a model while keeping data decentralized, motivated by privacy and data-access concerns. General Movement Assessment (GMA) is a clinical method, developed by Heinz Prechtl in the 1990s, that evaluates spontaneous movements in infants under four months to identify risk of cerebral palsy; the presence or absence of 'fidgety movements' around three months post-term is a key indicator. Skeletal motion data represents the body as a topological graph of joints and bones, typically extracted from video, and serves as the input for automated movement analysis.

## [ClearGS: Reliability-Aware Gaussian Splatting from Handheld Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31509) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ClearGS introduces Reliability-aware View Allocation (RVA), which assigns graded supervision weights to frames based on appearance reliability, degradation risk, and geometric utility instead of binary frame selection, while weakly reactivating suppressed frames to preserve trajectory coverage. It also adds Render-Guided In-Video Restoration (RIVR), using the current 3DGS render as a pose-aligned structural candidate and a frozen no-reference restoration expert to restore raw observations, then selecting among candidates via no-reference perceptual scores, plus Full-Trajectory Repair Consolidation. On GS2E and GSOTM benchmarks, ClearGS achieves state-of-the-art overall performance with consistent CLIP-IQA and MUSIQ gains and LPIPS reductions in most degradation settings, without paired sharp supervision or matched clean references. Handheld video is one of the most practical and accessible capture modes for 3D Gaussian Splatting, but uneven viewpoint coverage and mixed frame quality (blur, distortion) routinely break existing pipelines that assume clean, well-distributed input. By handling degraded frames without paired sharp supervision or clean references, ClearGS could make high-quality 3D reconstruction feasible for casual phone captures, benefiting researchers and practitioners in 3D reconstruction, neural rendering, and AR/VR content creation. The method combines three components: graded reliability-aware view allocation with weak reactivation of suppressed frames, render-guided in-video restoration using a frozen no-reference restoration expert and no-reference perceptual selection, and full-trajectory repair consolidation that revisits accepted repairs to preserve early-introduced details. Evaluation is on the GS2E and GSOTM benchmarks, with gains measured by CLIP-IQA, MUSIQ, and LPIPS; the approach explicitly avoids requiring paired sharp supervision or matched clean references.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is a volume rendering technique, revitalized in 2023 by a group at Inria, that represents a scene as many 3D Gaussians and renders novel views in real time, competing with NeRF-style radiance fields. It typically relies on many input images with good coverage and consistent quality, so handheld footage with motion blur, distortion, or sparse angles degrades reconstruction. No-reference image quality assessment (IQA) estimates image quality without an original reference image, which is what makes restoration and candidate selection possible when no clean ground truth exists.

## [Checkability Criterion Decides When Local SLMs Can Handle Network Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31540) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces 'checkability' as a criterion for deciding which network-automation tasks are safe for local small language model (SLM) inference, and instantiates it in Touchstone, a local-first pipeline using seven off-the-shelf SLMs (1-8B parameters). Touchstone achieves 98.6% accuracy on conflict detection and 93.8% on intent translation while escalating only 16% and 17% of inputs to a frontier LLM, but fails to match the frontier baseline on TeleQnA, a knowledge-only control with no task-specific intrinsic checks. The work addresses a real privacy-versus-performance trade-off: sending every network-automation input to a third-party frontier LLM exports sensitive production configurations, topologies, and logs, while purely local SLM outputs can be error-prone. By giving practitioners a concrete rule—keep inference local when task semantics support precise, low-cost checks, and escalate the rest—it could reduce data egress and cost in network automation deployments. The intrinsic check is a cheap, deterministic test that rejects outputs violating a necessary correctness condition, and the paper's key caveat is that checkability is task-dependent: on TeleQnA, a knowledge-only control lacking such checks, Touchstone cannot match the frontier baseline. The pipeline uses seven off-the-shelf SLMs in the 1-8B parameter range, so the approach depends on the availability of task-specific verification logic rather than on model scale alone.

**Background**: Small language models (SLMs) are compact models, often in the 1-8B parameter range, that can run locally on modest hardware, unlike frontier LLMs such as those from OpenAI or Anthropic that are accessed as third-party services. Network automation involves tasks like detecting configuration conflicts and translating operator intent into device commands, where inputs often contain sensitive production data. The paper's core idea is that some tasks expose a cheap deterministic test—an intrinsic check—that can catch incorrect SLM outputs before they are used, making local inference viable for those tasks.

## [OC-GS Refines Camera Angles for Irregular Turntable 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31572) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OC-GS introduces an object-centric Gaussian splatting method that jointly refines each image's rotation angle while maintaining a shared camera, rotation axis, and pivot, enabling reconstruction from sparse, irregular turntable captures. On rendered objects with 12, 8, and 6 irregularly spaced views, it achieves mean foreground PSNR scores of 21.26, 19.36, and 15.83 dB, exceeding all four evaluated pose-free Gaussian splatting baselines in every condition. Turntable capture is a common and low-cost way to digitize objects, but uneven rotation and dropped frames break the equal-angle assumption that most reconstruction pipelines rely on. By making Gaussian splatting robust to such irregular captures, OC-GS could lower the hardware and capture-quality bar for practical 3D scanning, benefiting researchers and practitioners in 3D vision, graphics, and content creation. Under a shared trainer, refining image-estimated angles improves mean foreground PSNR by 7.88 dB over keeping those estimates fixed, and an ablation study confirms that both image-derived angle initialization and the shared motion model contribute to the gain. On real captures, the refinement yields a more modest 0.70 dB improvement in mean foreground PSNR.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Gaussian splatting is a volume rendering technique that represents 3D scenes as collections of anisotropic 3D Gaussians, enabling high-quality, real-time novel view synthesis; it was revitalized in 2023 by a seminal Inria paper. Traditional turntable reconstruction assumes images are captured at equal angular intervals around a fixed axis, an assumption that fails when rotation is uneven or frames are dropped. Pose-free Gaussian splatting methods aim to reconstruct scenes without known camera poses, but they typically do not exploit the shared orbital motion structure of turntable setups.

## [Better Docs Don't Help Coding Agents Fix Bugs, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31587) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.31587) introduces a roundtrip benchmark that scores natural-language code descriptions by whether code regenerated from them passes the original tests, showing that completeness rather than length drives fidelity. Using this benchmark as an optimization signal, the authors discover a description-writing prompt that reaches full fidelity and generalizes to unseen files, but then find across two model families and ten repositories that improved documentation does not help coding agents resolve real repository issues. This rigorously tested negative result challenges the widely held assumption that better documentation automatically improves AI coding agents, suggesting that when source code is available, documentation adds little beyond the issue description itself. It matters for AI/ML and software engineering researchers and practitioners who invest in documentation pipelines for agentic coding workflows. The study spans two model families and ten repositories and includes a positive control confirming the evaluation can detect genuine improvements; when the source is present, neither static compact documentation nor retrieved context beats the issue alone. The authors also characterize the boundary conditions under which documentation does help.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents are LLM-based systems that autonomously resolve software issues in real repositories, often evaluated on benchmarks like SWE-bench. Documentation here means natural-language descriptions of code, and the roundtrip benchmark treats a description as the source artifact: regenerate the code from it, then check whether the regenerated code passes the original tests. A negative result is a study reporting that a hypothesized effect was not observed, which is valuable because it prevents the community from pursuing ineffective directions.

## [Post-processing algorithms align black-box generative AI attribute distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31607) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31607) develops post-processing algorithms that, given repeated black-box queries to a generative AI model, return m≥1 outputs whose joint attribute distribution closely matches a user-specified target. The authors prove their algorithms minimize the expected number of queries for both exact and approximate alignment, and show optimality as m→∞, with experiments on text-to-image generation and geocoded persona generation. This work addresses fairness and synthetic data representativeness by aligning protected attributes like gender, race, or age to target distributions without needing model internals. It offers a query-efficient, black-box complement to prompting-based interventions, which matters for practitioners deploying third-party generative models where only API access is available. The algorithms are designed for the black-box access setting where a user can only repeatedly query the generator, and they minimize expected query complexity while proving asymptotic optimality as the number of requested outputs grows. Experiments show improved statistical attribute alignment on text-to-image and geocoded persona tasks, complementing rather than replacing prompting-based methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI models such as text-to-image systems are often accessed only through APIs, making their internal weights and sampling mechanisms unavailable. Aligning output attribute distributions is important for fairness (e.g., ensuring balanced gender or race representation) and for synthetic data generation (e.g., producing representative datasets). Prior approaches often rely on prompt engineering or model fine-tuning, but these can be unreliable or infeasible when the model is a black box.

## [SkillFlow: Multi-Stage Retrieval System for Agent Skill Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06188) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released SkillFlow, described as the first open multi-stage retrieval system for agent skill discovery, which indexes roughly 35,000 community-contributed SKILL.md definitions from GitHub and narrows candidates through four stages: dense retrieval, two rounds of cross-encoder reranking, and LLM-based selection. On the SkillsBench coding benchmark, SkillFlow-retrieved skills raised Pass@1 from 9.2% to 16.4% (+78.3%, p_adj = 3.64e-2), reaching 84.1% of the oracle ceiling. As community skill repositories grow, loading too many or irrelevant skills degrades agent performance, so selective retrieval becomes a practical scaling requirement for skill-augmented agents. SkillFlow shows that framing skill acquisition as an information retrieval problem works, while also revealing that real gains depend on corpus coverage and the density of runnable, high-quality skills. The pipeline balances recall and precision across its four stages, and evaluation used two coding benchmarks: SkillsBench with 87 tasks and 229 matched skills, and Terminal-Bench with 89 tasks but no matched skills. On Terminal-Bench agents used retrieved skills at a 70.1% rate yet showed no performance gain, indicating retrieval alone is insufficient when the corpus lacks high-quality executable skills for the target domain.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents can extend their capabilities at inference time by loading reusable skills into context, where a skill is typically a folder containing a SKILL.md file with YAML frontmatter and instructions. Dense retrieval encodes queries and documents into vectors for semantic search, while cross-encoder rerankers jointly score query-document pairs for higher precision. SkillFlow applies these information retrieval techniques to a large corpus of community-contributed skills indexed from GitHub.

## [NarrativeAttack: Three-Act Jailbreak for Unified Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26473) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced NarrativeAttack, a semantic-preserving jailbreak framework that uses a three-act narrative structure in which a unified multimodal model's own image generator produces setup and resolution images, hiding the malicious event as a hidden climax. The attack ends with an image-based guessing game that embeds the original malicious query among benign candidates, achieving up to 88.25% attack success rate on Gemini-2.5-Flash. This work exposes an underexplored safety risk in unified multimodal models, where generated visual content becomes later reasoning context, enabling cross-turn visual grounding attacks that bypass text-based safety filters. It highlights the urgent need for safety alignment in UMMs and could influence how future multimodal systems are designed and evaluated. The attack is self-contained because the UMM's own generator creates the images, and a dynamic difficulty mechanism improves stability. Experiments show it consistently surpasses prior approaches, though the paper is a preprint and results are primarily on Gemini-2.5-Flash.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Unified Multimodal Understanding and Generation Models (UMMs) combine visual understanding and image generation in a single interactive workflow, so generated images can serve as context for later reasoning. Jailbreak attacks aim to bypass safety mechanisms in AI models, and prior multimodal jailbreak research has mostly focused on text rewriting or isolated visual prompts. Visual grounding is the ability of a model to identify image regions matching a textual description, and cross-turn visual grounding means using images generated in earlier turns to influence later responses.

## [LLM Pipeline Auto-Generates Personalized Therapy Networks From Session Transcripts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05836) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers developed an end-to-end LLM pipeline that automatically generates personalized client networks from therapy session transcripts, annotating 8,028 utterances across 77 transcripts from 6 clients. The pipeline identifies clinically relevant processes via binary classification, assigns dimensions through multi-label classification, clusters processes into meaningful groups with generated labels, and finally generates connections between clusters. This addresses a real scalability bottleneck in mental health care, since personalized networks typically require intensive longitudinal data that is often infeasible to collect. If validated, LLM-generated networks could make network-driven treatment personalization accessible to far more clinicians and clients. Evaluation generally supported model utility, but interrater agreement on performance metrics ranged from poor to substantial, and the authors caution that more research is needed to test whether these networks actually improve treatment outcomes relative to statistically estimated networks.

Large language models have recently shown promise in clinical NLP tasks like extracting structured information from clinical text, motivating this proof-of-concept application to psychotherapy transcripts.

## [Stepwise Marginal Information Gain: Annotation-Free Process Rewards for LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01034) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.01034v2) proposes Stepwise Marginal Information Gain (MIG), an intrinsic process reward computed directly from the policy itself, which measures how each structured reasoning prefix changes the length-normalized, teacher-forced log-likelihood of the reference answer. Combined with outcome and format rewards plus a gated self-distillation objective, the method outperforms outcome-only GRPO across eight benchmarks, improving average accuracy by up to 4.8 points and gaining 12.6 points on MathVerse, while at 7B it beats an external PRM-BoN@16 baseline by 12.9 points on vision-language transfer without inference-time reranking. This work offers an annotation-free alternative to process reward models (PRMs), which typically require costly step-level human labels, auxiliary models, or inference-time search. If validated, policy-derived stepwise credit could make RL-based reasoning training cheaper and more scalable for both LLMs and VLMs, and its real-versus-blank likelihood gate specifically addresses the problem of multimodal answers being driven by linguistic priors rather than visual evidence. MIG uses a monotonic historical watermark that rewards only new likelihood maxima, avoiding duplicate credit after sub-record detours, and for VLMs a real-versus-blank likelihood gate down-weights rewards when answers remain predictable without the image. The full method exceeds outcome-only GRPO in every single-run comparison across eight task-specific benchmarks, though results are reported as single-run comparisons rather than multi-seed averages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning is now a common way to improve reasoning in large language models and vision-language models, but the standard sparse binary outcome reward only scores final correctness and cannot tell which intermediate steps mattered. Process reward models (PRMs) address this by scoring each reasoning step, but they usually need process annotations, auxiliary models, or inference-time search. Teacher-forced log-likelihood refers to computing the probability of a reference answer token by token while feeding the model the correct preceding tokens, and GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a popular RL algorithm used for such reasoning training.

Researchers introduced FlyAOC, a benchmark that evaluates AI agents on end-to-end ontology curation from scientific literature. Given a gene symbol and a FlyBase gene description, agents must search a 16,898-paper corpus and recover structured annotations, with 7,397 expert-curated annotations across 100 genes as ground truth. The benchmark includes four baseline agent harnesses—memorization, fixed pipeline, single-agent, and multi-agent—and is sensitive to harness design, model family, and tool-use reliability. Outputs span standardized function terms, expression patterns, and historical synonyms linking decades of nomenclature.

## [AMOR: Entropy-Gated Attention for Hybrid Recurrent Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13215) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced AMOR (Adaptive Metacognitive Output Router), a post-hoc hybrid architecture that selectively invokes attention only when a recurrent backbone's output entropy exceeds a dynamic threshold derived from a running batch median and scaled standard deviation. Pretrained from scratch on FineWeb-Edu with attention activated on only about 40% of positions, an AMOR variant built on Mamba2 or Gated DeltaNet backbones achieved the highest eight-task common-sense reasoning average at each scale compared to pure recurrent, pure attention, and fixed-schedule hybrid models. This work shows that when attention is applied matters as much as how much, offering a parameter-free alternative to uniform or fixed routing strategies in hybrid models. It could improve the accuracy, robustness, and efficiency of long-context language models, benefiting researchers and engineers building efficient deep learning systems. The binary gate requires no learned routing parameters, and AMOR also improves retrieval performance over pure recurrent models while remaining competitive against fixed-schedule hybrids. It retains the long-context robustness of its recurrent backbones, whereas Transformer and other hybrid architectures degrade under distribution shift.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Recurrent-attention hybrids aim to combine the efficiency of recurrence with the contextual recall of attention, but existing approaches typically apply attention uniformly across all positions, even when the recurrent state alone is sufficient. Mamba2 is a state space model architecture that offers linear-time sequence modeling, while Gated DeltaNet is a linear attention variant that augments the delta rule with a data-dependent gate for fast memory erasure. AMOR builds on these backbones by using predictive uncertainty, measured via output entropy, to decide when attention is needed.

## [GEARS: Agentic Reasoning Framework for Large-Scale Ranking Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18640) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers (apparently at Meta) released GEARS (Generative Engine for Agentic Ranking Systems), a framework that reframes ranking optimization as an autonomous discovery process inside a programmable experimentation environment. Instead of static model selection, GEARS uses Specialized Agent Skills to encapsulate ranking-expert knowledge into reusable reasoning capabilities, letting operators steer systems through high-level 'vibe personalization' intent. The paper argues that progress in large-scale ranking is increasingly bottlenecked not by modeling techniques but by the 'engineering context constraint' — the hard work of translating ambiguous product intent into executable, verifiable hypotheses. If GEARS works as claimed, it could shift ranking optimization from manual, expert-driven experimentation toward agentic, intent-driven pipelines, affecting how recommendation and search teams at large platforms build and govern their systems. GEARS decomposes the end-to-end ranking optimization workflow into three coordinated stages orchestrated by an LLM-based controller, and adds validation hooks that enforce statistical robustness and filter out brittle policies overfitting short-term signals. Experiments across diverse product surfaces reportedly yield near-Pareto-efficient policies that combine algorithmic signals with deep ranking context while maintaining deployment stability, though the work is an arXiv preprint without peer review or public community discussion.

Agentic AI refers to LLM-driven systems that can plan, use tools, and act autonomously; 'Agent Skills' are a lightweight format for packaging specialized knowledge and workflows so agents can invoke them reliably. ## [Jagarin: Three-Layer Architecture for Hibernating Mobile AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05069) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2603.05069v3) presents Jagarin, a three-layer architecture that lets personal AI agents hibernate on mobile devices and wake on demand. It combines DAWN (an on-device scoring engine), ARIA (a commercial email identity proxy), and ACE (a machine-readable institution-to-agent protocol), demonstrated in a working Flutter prototype on Android. Personal AI agents on mobile face a deployment paradox: always-on background execution drains batteries and violates platform limits, while purely reactive agents miss time-sensitive obligations. Jagarin's structured hibernation and demand-driven wake could let assistants stay timely without sacrificing battery life, shaping how future mobile agent platforms are designed. DAWN scores four signals — duty-typed optimal action windows, predicted user engagement, cost of delay, and cross-duty batching — with per-duty adaptive thresholds to decide whether to stay silent, nudge the user, or offer escalation. Behavioural signals, thresholds, and scoring never leave the device, and ARIA seals every extracted record to the device's public key so the relay only holds unreadable ciphertext; cloud exposure is limited to parsing commercial email and user-initiated escalation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Mobile operating systems impose strict background execution limits to protect battery life, which makes it hard to run persistent personal AI assistants. Jagarin addresses this by letting agents sleep and only waking them when an on-device scoring engine judges that a pending obligation is urgent enough to act on. The paper is an arXiv preprint, and DAWN and ACE are specified and evaluated in companion papers, so this work focuses on how the three layers fit together.

## [Combee Scales Parallel Prompt Learning for Self-Improving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04247) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Combee is a new framework that scales parallel prompt learning for self-improving language model agents, enabling many agents to run in parallel while learning from their aggregate traces without quality degradation. Evaluations on AppWorld, Terminal-Bench, Formula, and FiNER show up to 17x speedup over previous methods with comparable or better accuracy and equivalent cost. Existing prompt learning methods like ACE and GEPA focus on single-agent or low-parallelism settings, which limits their ability to learn efficiently from large sets of agentic traces. Combee addresses this bottleneck, making it practical to learn from many parallel agent executions as agentic workloads continue to grow. Combee leverages parallel scans and an augmented shuffle mechanism, and introduces a dynamic batch size controller to balance quality and delay. It integrates plug-and-play with existing methods such as ACE and GEPA between the reflection and update stages, preserving most or all of sequential learning quality while reducing training time.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt learning allows LLM agents to acquire task-relevant knowledge from inference-time context without changing model parameters; methods like ACE and GEPA learn system prompts to improve accuracy based on previous agent runs. GEPA, for instance, uses LLMs to read full execution traces—error messages, profiling data, reasoning logs—to diagnose failures and propose targeted fixes, rather than collapsing traces into a single scalar reward like RL. Scaling such learning to many parallel traces is challenging because naive aggregation causes quality degradation at high parallelism.

## [AttriBench Reveals Demographic Bias in LLM Quote Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05224) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces AttriBench, the first fame- and demographically-balanced benchmark for quote attribution in LLMs, and evaluates 11 widely used models across different prompt settings. The study finds large, systematic disparities in attribution accuracy across race, gender, and intersectional groups, and identifies a novel failure mode called suppression, where models omit attribution entirely even when authorship information is available. As LLMs are increasingly used for search and information retrieval, biased or missing attribution can misrepresent who said what, amplifying representational harms for already marginalized groups. AttriBench provides a controlled way to measure fairness in attribution, extending AI bias evaluation beyond standard accuracy metrics. The benchmark explicitly balances author fame and demographics to separate general knowledge gaps from demographic bias, and it evaluates models across multiple prompt settings. Suppression is shown to be widespread and unevenly distributed across demographic groups, revealing biases that standard accuracy metrics fail to capture.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Quote attribution is the task of correctly identifying the original author of a quoted statement, a core capability for search engines, chatbots, and retrieval systems that cite sources. Prior attribution benchmarks did not control for the fame or demographic profile of quoted authors, making it hard to tell whether a model's error reflects missing knowledge or bias. AttriBench fills this gap by balancing these factors, and the paper also introduces suppression as a distinct failure mode where a model gives no attribution at all despite having access to authorship information.

## [ReasonAudio Benchmark Tests Logical Reasoning in Text-Audio Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03361) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced ReasonAudio, a new benchmark for reasoning-intensive text-audio retrieval that evaluates four abilities: negation, temporal order, sound co-occurrence, and sound duration. It contains five synthetic subtasks with 1,000 queries over 10,000 composite audio clips plus a natural subtask with 100 queries over 1,000 real-world clips, and evaluation of 11 state-of-the-art systems shows the best model, OmniEmbed-7B, scoring only 20.7 overall. This benchmark exposes a major blind spot in current audio retrieval research, which has largely focused on semantic matching rather than logical reasoning. The large gap between model performance and human accuracy (95.6%) suggests that multimodal retrieval systems need fundamentally new approaches to handle complex, reasoning-heavy queries. In a controlled experiment designed to reduce the influence of sound-event matching, OmniEmbed-7B reached 53.8% average accuracy, compared with 70.6% for its generative backbone Qwen2.5-Omni-7B-Thinker, revealing a notable performance gap between the retrieval model and the generative model it is built on.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Text-audio retrieval is the task of finding audio content that matches a text query, or vice versa, and most existing benchmarks measure only whether a model can match semantically similar sounds and descriptions. Reasoning-intensive retrieval instead requires models to understand logical operations such as negation ("a sound that is not a dog barking") or temporal order ("a door opens before footsteps"). OmniEmbed-7B is a multimodal embedding model, while Qwen2.5-Omni-7B-Thinker is the generative reasoning component extracted from the Qwen2.5-Omni-7B model.

## [Implicit Deductive Reasoning Scales in Deep Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04330) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.04330v3) shows that depth-bounded Transformers can perform implicit deductive reasoning over Horn clauses, approaching explicit chain-of-thought (CoT) performance when using counterfactual data augmentation, shared reasoning primitives, and a bidirectional prefix mask. The authors release code and models to reproduce the work. This work suggests that sufficiently deep Transformers can internalize deductive reasoning without generating explicit CoT steps, which could reduce inference cost and improve compositional reasoning. It also clarifies when CoT remains necessary, such as for depth extrapolation, informing future model design and scaling strategies. The study uses counterfactual data augmentation to discourage statistical shortcuts and promotes shared reasoning primitives across direct and CoT modes. Implicit reasoning matches CoT across graph topologies and problem widths, but CoT remains necessary for depth extrapolation, and the approach requires a bidirectional prefix mask and sufficient depth.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Horn clauses are a restricted form of logical formulas that are central to logic programming and deductive reasoning. Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting asks a model to produce intermediate reasoning steps explicitly, which improves accuracy but increases inference cost. This paper explores whether Transformers can instead perform such reasoning implicitly, without emitting those steps, and how that ability scales with model depth.

## [EBM-RL: Decoupled RL Framework for Immersive Video Role-Playing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced EBM-RL (Eye-Brain-Mouth Reinforcement Learning), a decoupled GRPO-based framework that separates observation, reasoning, and utterance generation for video-grounded role-playing dialogue. The framework integrates complementary rewards for scene-text alignment, perceptual-cognitive utility, answer faithfulness, and format consistency, and the team also released an open-source dataset for video-grounded role-playing dialogue. This work addresses a gap in immersive applications such as VR games and interactive narratives, where text-only role-playing models fail to capture scene atmosphere and evolving tension. By grounding dialogue in visual perception, EBM-RL could improve character authenticity and visual-atmosphere consistency, with strong zero-shot transfer to out-of-domain VideoQA benchmarks. EBM-RL mimics the human See-Think-Speak process and uses stage-specific GRPO optimization for each of the three stages. It substantially outperforms text-only role-playing baselines and larger-scale vision-language models on the authors' immersive role-playing benchmark, though it remains an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm proposed by DeepSeek for aligning large language models, notable for cutting compute requirements roughly in half compared to prior RL methods. Video-grounded role-playing dialogue extends text-based role-playing by requiring models to perceive visual scenes and generate character-consistent utterances, which is important for VR and interactive narrative applications.

## [SAGE: Multi-Agent LLM Framework Detects and Explains Time-Series Anomalies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05725) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SAGE (Specialized Analyzer Group for Expert-like Detection), a multi-agent LLM framework for evidence-grounded diagnosis of univariate time series. It uses four specialized Analyzers (point, structural, seasonal, pattern) plus a Detector and Supervisor, achieving an average Point-F1 of 66.26 across Yahoo S5, KPI, and WSD benchmarks — the highest among evaluated methods. Traditional anomaly detection returns only scores or intervals, leaving analysts without explanations; SAGE addresses this explainability gap by producing evidence-grounded diagnoses and analyst-facing reports. This could benefit AIOps, cloud KPI monitoring, and any domain where operators need to understand why an anomaly occurred, not just that it did. SAGE constructs synthetic in-context references from normal-reference training segments, reducing dependence on real anomalous demonstrations. A controlled synthetic evaluation tested localization and type diagnosis, component ablations confirmed the value of specialized evidence generation, and a method-blind human study rated SAGE's outputs as more useful than compared methods.

## [HaM-World Combines Soft-Hamiltonian Dynamics with Selective Memory for World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05951) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HaM-World introduces a structured world model that integrates history-conditioned selective memory (via Mamba state-space memory) with a Soft-Hamiltonian latent dynamics prior, decomposing the latent state into a canonical (q,p) subspace and a context subspace c. On six DeepMind Control Suite tasks, it ranks first on four and second on two, achieving the highest average AUC of 117.9 on the four-task core suite, 9.5% above TD-MPC2. This work advances model-based reinforcement learning by showing that combining energy-based Hamiltonian structure with selective memory can improve planning stability and out-of-distribution robustness, potentially benefiting robotics and control applications where reliable long-horizon prediction is critical. The strong empirical results on standard benchmarks suggest a promising direction for hybrid structured world models. Within short-to-medium planning horizons, HaM-World reduces imagined-rollout error to 45% of a strong baseline and wins 11 of 12 rollout-MSE cells for horizons k in {3,5,7}, but at longer open-loop horizons its error grows faster and is overtaken between k=7 and k=20, so the authors do not claim uniform long-horizon stability. Ablations show memory accounts for the larger share of gains, while Soft-Hamiltonian geometry provides smaller but consistent complementary improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: World models learn latent dynamics that allow agents to plan by imagining future trajectories rather than interacting with the real environment, a core approach in model-based reinforcement learning. Hamiltonian dynamics describe physical systems through conserved energy, providing a structured prior that can stabilize learned latent transitions, while Mamba is a selective state-space model that efficiently summarizes long sequences with input-dependent parameters. The DeepMind Control Suite is a widely used benchmark of continuous control tasks built on the MuJoCo physics simulator.

## [AcuityBench: Benchmarking Clinical Acuity and Uncertainty in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced AcuityBench, a new benchmark that harmonizes five public datasets into a shared four-level acuity framework spanning home monitoring to immediate emergency care, containing 914 cases (697 consensus and 217 physician-confirmed ambiguous). It evaluates 12 frontier proprietary and open-weight models on both explicit four-way classification and free-form conversational responses judged by a rubric-based evaluator. Acuity identification is a distinct safety-critical capability that existing health benchmarks largely overlook, and AcuityBench provides a systematic way to compare how well models guide users to the right level of care. Its findings on over-triage versus under-triage tradeoffs and poor uncertainty alignment could shape how healthcare AI systems are evaluated and deployed in real-world settings. Across the 12 models, clear-case acuity accuracy and error direction varied substantially, and conversational responses reduced over-triage but increased under-triage relative to QA, especially in higher-acuity cases. In ambiguous cases, no model closely matched the distribution of physician judgments, and model predictions were more concentrated than expert clinical uncertainty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Clinical acuity refers to how urgently a patient needs care, ranging from self-monitoring at home to immediate emergency intervention. Large language models are increasingly used for health-related questions, but existing benchmarks focus on medical question answering or narrow triage tasks rather than unified acuity identification. Uncertainty-aware evaluation is important because ambiguous presentations are common in real clinical settings, and models that appear confident can mislead users about the appropriate level of care.

## [SORT: Selective Off-Policy Reference Tuning with Plan Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11505) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SORT, a repair update for GRPO-style reinforcement learning that targets hard prompts where all sampled rollouts fail. SORT derives a plan from the reference solution, compares token probabilities with and without the plan, and upweights tokens that become more predictable under plan conditioning, turning all-wrong prompts into selective, structure-aware learning signals. GRPO-style methods stall on hard prompts where all rollouts fail, wasting compute and limiting reasoning improvement. SORT addresses this limitation and, across three backbones and eight reasoning benchmarks, outperforms GRPO and guidance baselines, with the largest gains on weaker models, suggesting it could make RL for reasoning more sample-efficient and effective. SORT adds a repair update without changing rollout generation, and it selectively weights tokens based on how much more predictable they become under plan conditioning rather than uniformly imitating the reference solution. The method was evaluated across three backbones and eight reasoning benchmarks, with the largest improvements observed on weaker models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm proposed by DeepSeek for training large language models, which reduces compute requirements by comparing groups of sampled outputs instead of using a separate value network. In reasoning tasks, RL with verifiable rewards is commonly used, but when all sampled rollouts for a prompt fail, GRPO-style methods receive no useful learning signal and stall. Off-policy learning refers to using data generated by a different policy than the one being optimized, and plan guidance involves providing a high-level plan or structure to guide reasoning.

## [BET Framework Cuts Reasoning Tokens 54% via Investment-Aware Budget Allocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11625) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Budget-Efficient Thinking (BET), a two-stage framework combining behavioral cold-start with GRPO under an investment-cost-aware reward, which learns to allocate test-time compute based on the expected return of reasoning rather than perceived difficulty. Across seven benchmarks and three base models, BET reduces reasoning tokens by 54% while improving accuracy by up to 3.2%, and transfers zero-shot to scientific QA and logical reasoning. This work addresses a key inefficiency in large reasoning models, which often overspend compute on unsolvable queries while under-investing in hard-but-solvable ones. By formalizing adaptive reasoning as a computational investment under uncertainty, it offers a principled path to more efficient inference with potential impact on deployment cost and latency. BET learns three distinct behaviors: short solve for easy queries, nice fold to abstain early when continued reasoning has near-zero expected return, and hero call to preserve compute for hard-but-solvable queries. The framework explicitly models the zero-return regime that prior methods leave unmodeled, and code is available at https://github.com/houqiii/BET.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Large reasoning models (LRMs) improve problem solving by generating extended reasoning traces, but they often misallocate test-time compute. Existing efficiency methods either compress reasoning traces or condition budget on perceived difficulty, treating pass rate as a difficulty score and leaving the zero-return regime unmodeled. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm designed for LLM alignment that reduces compute requirements compared to traditional RL methods.

## [CODESKILL: RL-Trained Skill Management for Self-Evolving Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25430) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CODESKILL is an LLM-based framework that reformulates skill extraction and skill-bank maintenance for coding agents as a learnable management policy, trained with reinforcement learning using a hybrid reward that combines dense rubric-based skill-quality feedback with sparse verifiable execution feedback from a frozen downstream agent. Experiments on EnvBench, SWE-Bench Verified, and Terminal-Bench 2 show an average pass-rate improvement of 11.03 points over a no-skill baseline and 5.10 points over the strongest prompt-based or memory baseline, while keeping the skill bank compact. Existing skill-construction methods for coding agents rely on fixed prompts and heuristic update rules, leaving it unclear how experience should be selected, abstracted, and maintained; by making this a learned policy, CODESKILL points toward agents that genuinely self-evolve from their own trajectories. If the gains hold up, this could change how AI/ML and software-engineering teams build long-lived coding agents that accumulate reusable expertise instead of starting from scratch each task. CODESKILL extracts multi-granularity procedural skills from coding-agent trajectories, evolves them with new experience, and maintains a compact skill bank for future task solving, with the downstream agent kept frozen so that only the management policy is trained. The reported numbers are averages across three benchmarks, and as an arXiv preprint the results have not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents such as those evaluated on SWE-Bench produce long trajectories while solving software-engineering tasks, and these trajectories can in principle be distilled into reusable procedural skills that compactly encode experience. Reinforcement learning commonly combines dense rewards, which give frequent graded feedback, with sparse rewards, which are only granted on verifiable success such as passing unit tests. CODESKILL applies this hybrid-reward idea to the meta-problem of deciding which skills to keep, abstract, and update in an agent's skill bank.

## [SciR: A Controllable Benchmark for Scientific Reasoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13020) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SciR, a benchmark that evaluates LLMs on three forms of scientific inference—deduction, induction, and causal abduction—by generating tasks from formal objects (deduction trees, inductive rule hypotheses, causal graphs) and rendering them into multi-document scientific discourse. It is the first multi-paradigm scientific-reasoning benchmark with parametric control over both extraction difficulty and inference difficulty. Scientific benchmarks built on human annotations are costly and lack mechanistic ground truth, while synthetic logical-reasoning benchmarks do not resemble real scientific documents, so SciR fills a critical gap by combining verifiable formal answers with realistic scientific discourse. This gives the AI/ML research community a controlled tool for diagnosing whether model failures stem from extracting key information or from the principled inference itself. Testing six models showed that both difficulty axes hurt every model and their effects compound, with rendering even degrading neurosymbolic pipelines that delegate inference to a verified solver. The two axes yield a per-model extraction-vs-inference profile, for example reasoning models like deepseek-r1 mostly surpass non-reasoning instruct models on the inference axis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Deduction, induction, and causal abduction are three paradigmatic forms of inference that recur across scientific reasoning: deduction derives conclusions from premises, induction forms general rules from observations, and causal abduction infers the best causal explanation for observed effects. A deduction tree is a tree-structured representation of a proof, while a causal graph encodes causal relationships among variables. Evaluating LLMs on these forms in realistic scientific settings has been difficult because human-annotated benchmarks lack mechanistic ground truth and synthetic benchmarks lack scientific realism.

## [Neural Networks Preserve Recoverable Case Structure, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11347) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2607.11347v2) establishes a theoretical connection between trained neural networks and Case-Based Decision Theory (CBDT), showing that neural networks can preserve a recoverable case structure through their learned representations. This structure allows fitted decision margins to be decomposed into individual training case contributions, and the authors identify conditions under which this recovered structure admits a CBDT interpretation with decision consistency. This work offers a new lens for interpretability and decision consistency, potentially influencing how we understand and audit neural network decisions in high-stakes applications. It connects model choices back to supporting and opposing training cases, providing a basis for assessing reliability. The paper validates the approach through experiments on a controlled CBDT setting and three decision tasks based on real-world data. It establishes decision consistency between the CBDT interpretation and the original neural network's decisions, though it remains an arXiv preprint without accompanying community discussion or immediate practical tooling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Case-Based Decision Theory (CBDT) is a decision-making framework where choices are made by comparing a current situation to similar past cases and their outcomes. Neural networks are powerful function approximators but are often criticized as black boxes because their internal mechanisms provide little evidence of how decisions relate to training data. This paper bridges the two by showing that neural networks can implicitly learn a case-based structure that can be recovered and interpreted.

## [GPU Power Benchmark of 18 Local LLMs on Consumer Hardware](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00008) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents a reproducible, hardware-level energy benchmark of 18 open-source LLMs ranging from 0.5B to 7B parameters, all run on a single consumer GPU (RTX 4060 Ti 16GB) via the Ollama inference engine. Using nvidia-smi to sample GPU power draw at 2 Hz, the study found that qwen2.5:0.5b and tinyllama:1.1b achieve the lowest energy cost at 0.2747 J/tok and 0.3234 J/tok respectively, while the 7B-Mistral model consumes up to 8.6x more energy per token than the most efficient model. As local LLM deployment grows due to privacy and on-premise inference needs, this benchmark fills a gap by quantifying energy costs that most evaluations ignore in favor of accuracy alone. The finding that architecture and quantization strategy—not just parameter count—drive energy efficiency gives practitioners concrete guidance for selecting models for power-constrained consumer setups. The benchmark measures mean/peak power, total energy per prompt (J/prompt), energy per output token (J/tok), and throughput (tok/s) across a fixed prompt set. Notably, qwen3.5:0.8b(on) shows anomalously high per-prompt energy due to extended internal reasoning, highlighting the need to distinguish between token generation modes in efficiency metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are typically evaluated on accuracy benchmarks, but running them locally on consumer GPUs involves real electricity costs that remain poorly characterized. Ollama is a single-binary inference engine with a built-in model registry and automatic GPU detection that simplifies local LLM deployment, while quantization reduces model weight precision to lower memory and compute demands. nvidia-smi is NVIDIA's standard system management tool that reports real-time GPU power draw, enabling hardware-level energy measurement.

## [Complex State Propagator Achieves Zero-Attenuation State Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03425) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Complex State Propagator (CSP), a minimalist recurrent model that operates purely on a complex phase manifold using an atan2-based Coordinate-to-Phase transformation, with no intermediate output projections, amplitude modulations, or per-step nonlinearities. A 3-layer CSP trained on sequences of length 16 generalizes to a 4x longer out-of-distribution length of 64 on the Mod-3 tracking task, reaching nearly 100% validation accuracy and F1-score. Transformers and Mamba-style state-space models suffer catastrophic out-of-distribution collapse when generalizing deterministic state tracking to longer sequences, so a compact recurrent paradigm that avoids this failure could reshape how sequence models handle tasks like parity checking, modular counting, and parenthesis matching. If the result holds beyond the Mod-3 benchmark, it suggests that pure-phase complex propagation can outperform much larger networks at a fraction of their size. The phase signal reportedly survives extreme depth with absolute zero informational attenuation, and the exact four-quadrant Coordinate-to-Phase (C-to-2) transformation governed by atan2(y, x) aligns the continuous optimization landscape with discrete cyclic groups. The abstract is truncated and the evaluation is limited to the canonical Mod-3 tracking task, so broader generalization claims remain to be verified.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: State-space models such as Mamba and recurrent architectures maintain a hidden state updated step by step, which makes them natural candidates for deterministic state tracking but prone to drift over long sequences. Complex-valued neural networks represent activations as numbers with magnitude and phase, and atan2 is the standard two-argument arctangent function that returns the angle of a point in the plane across all four quadrants. Out-of-distribution collapse refers to the sharp accuracy drop that occurs when a model trained on short sequences is tested on longer ones.

## [ADMITOR Certifies LLM Optimization Models Without Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ADMITOR, a label-free admission gate that generates optimization models from three model families, runs them on the stated problem and on instances with resampled parameters, and keeps the largest group whose optimal values agree across every instance and family. A threshold fitted on solver-verified problems then accepts, abstains, or escalates, with a finite-sample bound on the false-discovery rate among accepted values. Experience-learning agents for optimization modeling can poison their skill libraries with wrong trajectories that get retrieved repeatedly, and existing label-free substitutes such as execution success or single-instance agreement can admit wrong models. ADMITOR raises candidate-level admission precision to 0.927 versus 0.871 for majority vote and 0.726 for execution success, and its smaller library reaches the highest macro accuracy of 58.4 across five public benchmarks. An ablation shows the gain comes from the accepted value being external to the learner and unanimous across families, while resampling never changed an accepted value on this stream and only reduced coverage. The false-discovery bound holds on the calibration set but not on the benchmark stream; auditing false certificates traces most of them to benchmark texts that omit or round the numbers needed to reproduce the labeled answer, and a label-free check of extracted numbers against the text flags most of these cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based optimization modeling means using large language models to translate a natural-language problem into a formal optimization formulation, such as a linear or integer program. Agents that learn from experience store solved trajectories as reusable skills, but admitting a wrong trajectory into the library is dangerous because it can be retrieved again and again, and on a stream of new problems there is no ground-truth answer to check against. Existing learners admit trajectories by matching known optima or labels, while label-free substitutes like execution success or agreement at a single instance can still admit wrong models.

## [Verifier-Selected Self-Training Boosts Plan Validity on PlanBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18324) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.18324v2) shows that verifier-admitted outputs from Qwen3-14B thinking can supervise the same checkpoint for non-thinking execution, raising VAL acceptance on fresh PlanBench replanning cases from 1/80 to 57/80. A prospective replication across eight LoRA seeds and three inference realizations per seed produced a clear lift for every seed (45/80 to 70/80), with an exact seed-level sign-flip test giving p=0.0078125. The result suggests that machine-verifiable governance records can consolidate occasional or expensive model capability into reliable one-shot execution without oracle targets or a stronger teacher, which matters for deploying bounded models in structured workflow repair. It also draws a careful boundary: only one fixed, machine-checkable capability was robustly consolidated, not arbitrary planning or unrestricted self-improvement. Target-selection performance was less stable: an initial matched seed gave 102/160 accepted plans after VAL selection versus 69/160 after blinded model self-selection, but across eight prospective seeds the contrast was seed-dependent, included one clear reverse seed, and did not replicate (p=0.3672). VAL also failed its preregistered seed-level reliability gate over schema-only selection (p=0.0703), and a complementary Phi arm supports stronger-teacher distillation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: PlanBench is a benchmark that evaluates large language models on formal PDDL planning and reasoning about change, including replanning tasks. VAL is an independent plan-validation tool originally developed for the International Planning Competition that checks whether generated plans are correct. Self-training typically uses a model's own outputs as supervision, and verifier-selected self-training filters those outputs through an external verifier before training.

## [PhysElite: A New 11,586-Problem Olympiad Physics Benchmark for Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25097) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released PhysElite, a large-scale bilingual (Chinese-English) multimodal benchmark containing 11,586 Olympiad-tier physics problems, each with visual diagrams, step-by-step solution derivations, and final answers. Benchmarking 18 open-source and closed-source multimodal LLMs revealed that even the strongest model achieves only 33.7% answer accuracy, with step-level process evaluation diagnosing where reasoning chains break down. This benchmark exposes a substantial capability gap in multimodal LLMs for expert-level physics reasoning, showing that current models are far from solving Olympiad problems despite strong performance on easier benchmarks. It provides a rigorous, high-difficulty evaluation resource that could guide future research in multimodal reasoning and STEM education applications. PhysElite addresses two key limitations of existing physics benchmarks: the shortage of high-difficulty datasets and the lack of comprehensive coverage of visual forms, knowledge points, and step-by-step solution processes. The dataset is publicly released on Hugging Face, and the step-level process evaluation goes beyond final-answer accuracy to pinpoint specific failure points in the reasoning chain.

Olympiad-level physics problems are considered extremely challenging because they demand appropriate modeling, application of physical principles, and precise calculation within long reasoning processes. Existing benchmarks like PhoPile and P1 have begun exploring this area, but PhysElite claims to be the largest with bilingual support and detailed process-level annotations.

## [T-LoopFormer gives each token its own recursion depth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15160) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces T-LoopFormer, a looped Transformer variant that replaces the uniform fixed recursion depth with dynamic token-choice routing, letting each token decide how many loop iterations it needs based on its hidden state. It also proposes a recursion-wise KV cache that keeps a separate key-value cache per loop, so tokens at different depths only attend to their matching cached states, enabling faster autoregressive decoding. Fixed recursion depth wastes compute on easy tokens and under-processes hard ones, so token-level elastic depth could improve both accuracy and inference efficiency for latent reasoning models. The recursion-wise KV cache addresses a practical bottleneck in looped architectures, making them more viable for real-time autoregressive generation. The router is token-choice, meaning depth is decided per token from its hidden state rather than by a global schedule, and the recursion-wise KV cache ensures cross-depth attention does not mix states from different loop iterations. Experiments report robust language modeling and zero-shot reasoning results with the lowest decoding latency, and code is released at the project's GitHub repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Looped Transformers reuse a shared set of parameters across multiple iterations instead of stacking many distinct layers, achieving parameter efficiency while retaining representational power. Latent reasoning performs inference directly in hidden-state space rather than emitting explicit chain-of-thought tokens, reducing the number of tokens consumed during inference. However, prior looped models apply the same recursion depth to every token, which is suboptimal because token difficulty varies widely.

## [RLDS Decomposes Trajectory Rewards into Subtask Advantages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27035) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.27035v2) introduces Reinforcement Learning with Decomposed Subtasks (RLDS), whose core method, Subtask-Decomposed Advantage Estimation (SDAE), replaces the scalar GRPO advantage by splitting trajectory reward into per-subtask shares on a fixed taxonomy and computing a group-relative advantage for each subtask. Evaluated on four agentic benchmarks, RLDS yields the largest gains on high-heterogeneity tasks: +11.5 points on ScienceWorld and +9.8 points on FrozenLake, while results on HotpotQA and DeepResearch are within noise. This work targets a known weakness of GRPO-style policy-gradient training for multi-turn language model agents: collapsing an entire rollout into one scalar reward is lossy when tasks compose distinct skills under sparse, delayed feedback. If the approach generalizes, it could improve training efficiency and performance for agentic LLM systems, and its heterogeneity diagnostics offer a way to predict when decomposition will actually help. SDAE distributes per-token credit by weighting each subtask's advantage by its importance, concentrating credit around the reflection step that marks a subtask's execution as consequential. On ScienceWorld, RLDS is also more compute-efficient than scalar GRPO, reducing wall-clock time per step by 10.9% because long rollouts amortize the fixed reflect-and-grade overhead; the paper reports paired-bootstrap 95% confidence intervals for its main gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization), popularized by DeepSeek, is a reinforcement learning algorithm that samples a group of responses per prompt and uses the group's mean reward as a baseline for computing advantages, avoiding a separate critic network. Advantage estimation in RL measures how much better an action is than expected, providing localized credit assignment, but sparse and delayed rewards make this hard because the number of possible trajectories grows exponentially with the time horizon. RLDS builds on this line of work by decomposing the trajectory reward along subtasks before it enters the policy update.

## [PrivDrift Benchmark Audits Secret Leakage After Topic Drift in LLM Chats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30094) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced PrivDrift, a benchmark of 1,000 controlled multi-turn dialogues with seeded secrets, content-dense drift turns, and standardized extraction probes, to audit whether user-disclosed secrets remain recoverable after topic drift and persuasion-based probing. Across three LLMs with extended context windows, dialogue-level hybrid leakage ranged from 38.7% to 54.6%, varying strongly by model, secret type, and persuasion intensity. The findings suggest privacy risk in active LLM conversations should be treated as a persistent behavioral failure mode rather than only as training-data memorization or immediate jailbreak behavior, which matters for anyone deploying persistent assistants, shared-session tools, or tool-augmented agents. It gives researchers and practitioners a controlled methodology to quantify and compare leakage across models. Within the tested drift window, additional topic drift did not reliably reduce leakage, and the standardized probes were grouped into three persuasion levels, starting with a simple direct neutral query such as asking for a phone number mentioned earlier. Leakage also varied strongly by secret type and persuasion intensity, indicating that model behavior alone is not a sufficient audit signal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models increasingly act as persistent assistants in user-facing, shared-session, and tool-augmented settings, and extended context windows let them retain far more of a conversation. When users disclose sensitive information mid-conversation, that information may remain behaviorally recoverable through later prompts even after the dialogue shifts to unrelated topics. PrivDrift is designed to measure this recoverability under controlled topic drift and persuasion-based probing, rather than only testing training-data memorization or immediate jailbreaks.

## [AD-WM: Action-Discriminative World Models for Counterfactual MPC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30264) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce AD-WM, an action-discriminative joint-embedding world model that combines residual latent dynamics with predictor-level action-recovery regularization using inverse dynamics and a normalized recovery objective motivated by conditional mutual information. On OGBench-Cube, AD-WM raises hard-start success from 3.7% to 52.0% over a matched LeWM baseline, improves mean success in four of five simulation environments, and boosts zero-shot Franka pick-and-place success from 42.2% to 71.1% with a frozen V-JEPA 2 encoder. The work challenges the common practice of training latent world models purely for factual prediction accuracy, showing that planning quality depends on preserving action-dependent differences needed for counterfactual comparison. This has direct implications for model-based reinforcement learning and robotic control, where MPC must rank alternative actions from the same state rather than merely predict what happens next. The auxiliary inverse-dynamics and recovery heads are discarded at test time, leaving the MPC procedure unchanged, and diagnostics show that factual prediction error and whole-bank action ranking do not track closed-loop success, whereas CEM-aligned elite regret does. The zero-shot Franka transfer uses a frozen V-JEPA 2 encoder with matched DROID post-training and no lab-specific adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: World models learn a compact latent representation of an environment so an agent can predict future states and plan without interacting with the real system at every step. Joint-embedding predictive architectures (JEPA), such as V-JEPA 2, encode observations into embeddings and use a predictor to estimate future embeddings conditioned on actions. Model predictive control (MPC) uses such a model to simulate candidate action sequences and select the best one, so the model must distinguish between actions that lead to different outcomes. OGBench is a benchmark for offline goal-conditioned reinforcement learning that includes dexterous cube manipulation tasks.

It offers researchers and funders a clear map of gaps in resources, annotation, and model development, helping guide future work in African language technology. The survey highlights that language-specific challenges such as morphological analysis and effective representation of diacritics remain under-explored, and it calls for more collaborative efforts in resource enrichment, comprehensive annotation, and increased community support. ## [Tokenadapt Enables Tokenizer Flexibility in Pretrained Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09738) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Tokenadapt, a model-agnostic tokenizer transplantation method that initializes new token embeddings via a hybrid heuristic combining local subword decomposition and global top-k semantic similarity. It also proposes supertoken learning for multi-word units, achieving at least a 2-fold improvement in zero-shot perplexity ratio compared to ReTok and outperforming TransTokenizer baselines. This work addresses tokenizer lock-in, a significant practical problem that limits efficiency and performance in multilingual and specialized applications, by reducing the computational burden of tokenizer replacement. The model-agnostic approach could make it easier for practitioners to adapt pretrained LLMs to new languages or domains without prohibitive retraining costs. Tokenadapt uses a hybrid heuristic that combines a local estimate based on subword decomposition using the old tokenizer with a global estimate utilizing top-k semantically similar tokens from the original vocabulary. Empirical results show consistently lower perplexity ratios than ReTok and TransTokenizer across different base models and newly trained target tokenizers, though the paper is a preprint without peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Pretrained language models are typically tied to a fixed tokenizer, meaning the vocabulary and segmentation rules cannot be easily changed after training. This tokenizer lock-in causes inefficiencies, especially for multilingual or domain-specific use cases where a different tokenizer would be more suitable. Standard methods to replace the tokenizer require extensive fine-tuning, which is computationally expensive. Tokenadapt aims to reduce this burden by initializing new token embeddings heuristically so that minimal retraining is needed.

Researchers introduced LapDDPM, a conditional graph diffusion probabilistic model that generates high-fidelity single-cell RNA-seq data by combining graph-based inductive biases with score-based generative modeling and a novel spectral adversarial perturbation mechanism. Experiments on PBMC3K, Dentate Gyrus, HLCA, Visium, and 10x Multiome datasets show it outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in distribution matching, manifold preservation, and downstream utility. Generating biologically plausible synthetic scRNA-seq data is a critical challenge in computational biology, and LapDDPM's robustness to technical noise and structural variability could improve data augmentation, rare cell-type modeling, and downstream analyses. Its extension to spatial transcriptomics and multi-modal data suggests broad applicability across single-cell research. LapDDPM perturbs graph edge weights along principal spectral modes during training, acting as a Distributionally Robust Optimization (DRO) framework that enforces invariance to structural noise. It also extends to spatial transcriptomics and multi-modal data by treating generation as a robust inverse problem on cellular graphs.

Diffusion models are generative models that learn to denoise data through a gradual process, while graph neural networks capture relationships between cells. LapDDPM combines these ideas, using graph representations of cellular manifolds and spectral perturbations to generate realistic synthetic cells.
