## [Neural Bridge Processes Improve Stochastic Function Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07220) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Neural Bridge Processes (NBPs) replace the unconditional forward kernel of Neural Diffusion Processes (NDPs) with an input-anchored bridge trajectory, injecting input information directly into noisy training states. When input and output dimensions differ, NBP learns an output-space anchor a_ψ(x)=P_ψ(x), allowing coordinates or other inputs to guide the generative path without altering the denoising backbone. This work addresses a key limitation of NDPs by making the forward process input-dependent, which enhances expressivity and uncertainty-aware learning of stochastic functions. The theoretical analysis and consistent improvements across synthetic regression, EEG, CylinderFlow, and image regression suggest broad impact for probabilistic modeling and generative AI. The paper shows theoretically that process-level anchoring induces pathwise input distinguishability, injects information about x into noisy states, and creates a direct gradient pathway unavailable to NDPs. Ablations confirm that gains come from the full bridge construction with learned alignment, and the same principle transfers to Flow Matching Neural Processes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Neural Diffusion Processes (NDPs) use denoising diffusion to model distributions over functions, but their forward process is input-independent, meaning noisy training states do not encode conditioning inputs. Diffusion models typically learn to reverse a gradual noising process, and in NDPs inputs only enter the reverse denoiser. NBPs introduce a bridge trajectory—similar to a Brownian bridge that is pinned at both ends—to anchor the forward process to the input.

A new arXiv paper (2510.05191v3) develops a formal framework for speech attribute conversion, proving sufficient conditions for exact and consistent transfer under a deterministic autoencoder with a latent independence constraint. The authors also propose a practical voice conversion method implementing these principles, achieving competitive results on voice and pitch conversion tasks. This work addresses a theoretical gap in disentangled representation learning for speech, where most existing style transfer methods rely on heuristic objectives without guarantees. It could guide researchers in speech processing and generative models toward more principled, reliable attribute control. The analysis relies on explicit population-level assumptions about the data-generating process and links reconstruction, independence, and attribute manipulation feasibility. The guarantees are established for a deterministic autoencoder setting, and the practical method is validated on voice and pitch conversion tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Disentangled representation learning aims to learn latent representations where each dimension corresponds to a single interpretable factor of variation, which is important for controlled generation. A deterministic autoencoder maps inputs to unique latent codes while minimizing reconstruction loss, unlike variational autoencoders that use probabilistic encodings. Latent independence constraints encourage the learned latent representation to be statistically independent of controllable attributes, enabling manipulation without affecting task-relevant content.

## [Zero Cost Proxies Identify Top Wearable HAR Models Without Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06157) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2511.06157v3) evaluates eight Zero Cost Proxies (ZCPs) across six benchmark wearable Human Activity Recognition (HAR) datasets, showing that the top-predicted architectures achieve performance within 7% of full-scale training of 2,000 randomly sampled architectures. Training only the top-10 predicted architectures narrows the gap to within 2%, yielding substantial computational savings. This work introduces ZCPs to sensor-based HAR for the first time, offering a way to cut the enormous compute cost of Neural Architecture Search for wearable applications. It could significantly speed up model development for wearable computing and benefit both the HAR and AutoML communities. ZCPs are computed via a single forward/backward pass on a randomly sampled batch of data, making them extremely cheap compared to full training. The study is an incremental but technically solid advance, demonstrating ZCPs' suitability as an addition to NAS pipelines in practical scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Human Activity Recognition (HAR) with wearable sensors aims to automatically detect activities from sensor data, but the diversity of sensor locations, devices, and activities makes it hard to find architectures that generalize. Neural Architecture Search (NAS) automates architecture design but is computationally expensive because it typically requires training many candidate networks. Zero Cost Proxies (ZCPs) are scoring functions that correlate with trained performance yet can be computed without any training, offering a cheap alternative for ranking architectures.

## [SLMFix Uses Small Language Models and RL to Fix DSL Code Errors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.19422) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SLMFix is a new code generation pipeline that finetunes a small language model (SLM) with reinforcement learning to fix syntactic errors in LLM-generated programs for domain-specific languages (DSLs), using interpreter feedback as the reward signal. Experiments across multiple DSLs show a 40% improvement in validator pass rates on low-resource programming languages (LRPLs) and elimination of over 50% of syntactic errors for high-resource DSLs. This matters because state-of-the-art LLMs still produce syntactically broken code for low-resource and domain-specific languages, and finetuning large models is prohibitively expensive for many teams. By showing that a small, cheaply trainable model can outperform supervised finetuning even against 7B models on LRPLs like Ansible and Lean, SLMFix points toward a more accessible path for improving LLM code generation in niche ecosystems. SLMFix relies on interpreter feedback rather than human preference data, making the reinforcement learning loop automatic and domain-agnostic, and it generalizes across multiple DSLs. The paper reports that the approach substantially improves the base model and beats supervised finetuning for 7B models on LRPLs including Ansible and Lean, though it is an arXiv preprint (2511.19422v2) without peer review or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Domain-specific languages (DSLs) are programming languages specialized to a particular application domain, such as SQL for databases or Ansible for configuration management, as opposed to general-purpose languages like Python. Small language models (SLMs) are compact models, typically under forty billion parameters, that can be trained or hosted on modest hardware, unlike large language models that require massive compute. Reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) is a technique that trains a reward model from preference data and then optimizes a policy against it; SLMFix adapts this idea by using interpreter feedback as the reward instead of human preferences.

## [BabelCoder: Agentic LLM Framework for Code Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06902) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BabelCoder is a new agentic framework that decomposes code translation into specialized LLM agents for translation, testing, and refinement, and it outperforms four state-of-the-art baselines by 0.5%–13.5% in 94% of cases across four benchmark datasets, reaching an average accuracy of 94.16%. This work addresses the long-standing challenge of accurate cross-language code migration, which is increasingly important as software systems evolve and developers work across multiple programming languages. By structuring translation as a collaborative multi-agent process, it could improve reliability and reduce manual effort in real-world code migration tasks. The framework uses NL-Specification Validation to check whether translated code's behavior matches a language-agnostic summary of the source logic, enabling semantic error detection beyond syntactic fixes. Evaluation covers four benchmark datasets and four state-of-the-art baselines, though the abstract is truncated and full limitations are not fully detailed.

## [GPERT Decouples Geometry and Radiance for Event-based 3D Gaussian Ray Tracing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.18640) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GPERT introduces a dual-branch ray-tracing framework for event-based 3D Gaussian Splatting that separates event-by-event depth rendering from snapshot-based intensity rendering. It achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on real-world datasets without COLMAP initialization or pretrained models, with fast training and sharp edge reconstruction. This addresses a key trade-off between accuracy and temporal resolution in event-based 3D reconstruction, potentially enabling more robust and efficient neural rendering for high-speed motion scenarios. It could benefit researchers in 3D vision, robotics, and AR/VR who rely on event cameras for fine-grained temporal information. The method uses ray tracing and the image of warped events to decouple rendering, and it is more flexible in the number of events selected. It achieves competitive performance on synthetic datasets and works without prior information such as pretrained image reconstruction models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Event cameras are neuromorphic sensors that asynchronously report per-pixel brightness changes, offering high temporal resolution and low latency compared to traditional frame-based cameras. 3D Gaussian Splatting is a volume rendering technique that represents scenes as 3D Gaussians and enables real-time novel view synthesis. COLMAP is a standard structure-from-motion pipeline often used to initialize such reconstructions, but it can be a bottleneck for event-based data.

## [RAPTOR: Ridge-Adaptive Logistic Probes for LLM Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00158) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RAPTOR (Ridge-Adaptive Logistic Probe) is a new L2-regularized logistic probe whose validation-tuned ridge strength produces concept vectors from normalized weights, matching or exceeding strong baselines in accuracy while achieving competitive directional stability and substantially lower training cost. The authors also provide a mechanistic characterization of ridge logistic regression using the Convex Gaussian Min-max Theorem (CGMT) in an idealized Gaussian teacher-student model in the high-dimensional few-shot regime. This work addresses key desiderata for probe-then-steer pipelines in LLM interpretability and control—accuracy, directional stability under ablation, and computational efficiency—making it valuable for researchers and practitioners working on activation steering and representation engineering. The theoretical analysis also offers structural predictions that qualitatively align with trends observed on real LLM embeddings, bridging theory and practice. RAPTOR is a simple L2-regularized logistic probe that extracts concept vectors from normalized weights, with ridge strength tuned on a validation set; experiments span instruction-tuned LLMs and human-written concept datasets. The CGMT-based analysis explains how penalty strength mediates probe accuracy and concept-vector stability in the high-dimensional few-shot regime, yielding structural predictions that qualitatively match real LLM embedding trends.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Probing studies what information is encoded in a frozen LLM's layer representations by training a lightweight predictor on top of them. Beyond analysis, probes are often used operationally in probe-then-steer pipelines: a learned concept vector is extracted from a probe and injected via additive activation steering by adding it to a layer representation during the forward pass. Activation steering is a method for modifying LLM internal activations to reliably alter model outputs without retraining, using steering vectors injected at defined model layers to modulate properties such as sentiment, topic, and truthfulness. Ridge regression (L2 regularization) adds a penalty term equal to the sum of squared coefficients multiplied by a regularization parameter, which helps prevent overfitting by keeping coefficients small.

## [Auto-Comp: Automated Photorealistic Benchmark Diagnoses VLM Binding Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02043) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Auto-Comp, a fully automated, concept-driven pipeline that generates photorealistic compositional benchmarks at scale, using a parallel A/B construction that emits a Minimal sample (template caption, isolated objects on white background) and a Contextual sample (LLM-rewritten caption, objects in a realistic scene) for each concept. It instantiates four task families—Color and Shape-Color (attribute binding), and Position and Relative Size (relational binding)—and evaluates over 25 VLMs spanning CLIP, SigLIP, hard-negative-trained, and frontier generative models. This work addresses a key limitation in existing benchmarks by isolating core binding ability from visio-linguistic complexity, providing the VLM community with a scalable diagnostic tool. The findings—consistent across architectures and scales—reveal a large Swap-vs-Confusion gap and a task-dependent trade-off where context aids relational reasoning but hinders attribute binding, deepening understanding of compositional reasoning failures in multimodal AI. The pipeline's parallel A/B construction isolates binding ability by contrasting Minimal and Contextual samples, and low-entropy distractors (e.g., repeated objects or colors) expose failures beyond the known bag-of-words limitations. The study covers four task families and over 25 VLMs, and the pipeline and benchmarks are publicly released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP and SigLIP are trained to match images with text, but they often struggle with compositional reasoning—correctly binding attributes to objects (e.g., "red cube") or relations to referents (e.g., "left of"). Existing benchmarks either use noisy real images that conflate confounding visual variables with reasoning failure, or simplistic synthetic scenes lacking the realism modern VLMs are tuned for. Auto-Comp bridges this gap by generating photorealistic compositional benchmarks at scale.

## [AFlow and AFPO Bring Process-Level Alignment to Emotional Support Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08826) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Affective Flow Language Model (AFlow), which models multi-turn emotional support conversation as an affective utility flow along dialogue trajectories, and Affective Flow Preference Optimization (AFPO), a subpath-level flow-balance objective that propagates downstream preference signals to intermediate dialogue states. Experiments on the ExTES and ESConv benchmarks show consistent improvements in strategy alignment, response diversity, and generation quality, with code released at github.com/chz2025/AffectiveFlow. Existing LLM alignment methods for emotional support rely on sparse response-level preferences or dialogue-level outcomes, offering little supervision for the sequential strategy decisions that make multi-turn support effective. By deriving process signals from overall outcomes, AFlow and AFPO could improve the empathy and coherence of AI mental-health and support chatbots, benefiting both affective computing research and deployed dialogue systems. AFlow searches diverse support trajectories and estimates the utility of intermediate dialogue states and candidate strategies, then AFPO uses a flow-balance objective over dialogue subpaths to learn strategy transitions consistent with full-dialogue support outcomes. The approach is evaluated across different model environments and evaluation settings, though the abstract does not specify model sizes or the exact magnitude of the reported gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Emotional Support Conversation (ESC) is a dialogue task in which a system helps a user work through distress using strategies such as questioning, reflecting feelings, and offering suggestions. Large language models are increasingly used for this task, but aligning them typically relies on reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) or direct preference optimization (DPO), which judge whole responses or whole dialogues rather than the step-by-step strategy choices within a conversation. Benchmarks like ESConv and ExTES provide multi-turn emotional support dialogues for training and evaluation.

## [GVCC Turns Pretrained Video Generators into Zero-Shot Codecs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26571) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GVCC (Generative Video Codebook Codec) is a zero-shot video compression framework that repurposes a pretrained rectified-flow video generative model as the decoder, transmitting the bitstream by encoding the per-step stochastic innovations of a converted, marginal-preserving stochastic sampler. The authors instantiate it in three modes — Text-to-Video, autoregressive Image-to-Video with tail latent correction, and First-Last-Frame-to-Video with boundary-sharing GOP chaining — and characterize rate-quality behavior on the seven-sequence UVG dataset. At ultra-low bitrates, regressing to oversmoothed conditional means produces blurry results, so sampling plausible videos from the posterior becomes essential; GVCC shows that an existing generative video model can be reused as a decoder without any task-specific retraining. This could lower the barrier to building neural video codecs and open a new interface between generative modeling and compression research. Modern rectified-flow video models are normally sampled with deterministic ODE solvers, which leave no per-step stochastic channel for transmitting information; GVCC converts the deterministic flow sampler into an equivalent marginal-preserving stochastic process so bits can be carried by the per-step innovations. The paper reports full-dataset perceptual and fidelity metrics plus temporal diagnostics on UVG, but explicitly avoids claiming matched-rate or global rate-distortion improvements from its limited local atom-count sweeps.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Rectified flow (also called flow matching) is a generative modeling approach based on ordinary differential equations that learns a transport map between noise and data, and is widely used in modern video generation models. Video compression normally relies on hand-designed or learned codecs that encode residuals between frames; zero-shot here means the generative decoder is used as-is, without fine-tuning on the compression task. Diffusion and flow-based models are latent-variable generative models that can sample diverse outputs rather than a single averaged prediction.

## [Scepsy Schedules Multi-LLM Agentic Workflows on GPU Clusters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15186) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers at Imperial College London have introduced Scepsy, a serving system that schedules arbitrary multi-LLM agentic workflows onto GPU clusters by profiling each LLM under different parallelism degrees and combining these profiles into an Aggregate LLM Pipeline. On realistic agentic workflows, Scepsy achieves up to 2.5x higher throughput before saturation and 1.0-3.3x lower latency than systems that optimize LLMs independently or rely on user-specified allocations. Agentic workflows that orchestrate multiple LLMs and tools are increasingly common, but their unpredictable, data-dependent execution makes efficient GPU serving difficult, especially when the number of LLMs exceeds available GPUs. Scepsy's approach could significantly improve throughput and latency for production LLM infrastructure, benefiting both service providers and end users of agent-based applications. Scepsy exploits the insight that while end-to-end latency of an agentic workflow is unpredictable, each LLM's fraction of execution time remains relatively stable across requests. It uses the Aggregate LLM Pipeline to search over fractional GPU shares, tensor parallelism degrees, and replica counts, then employs a hierarchical heuristic to place the allocation onto the cluster while minimizing fragmentation and respecting network topology.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic workflows are AI systems that coordinate multiple large language models and external tools to complete complex tasks, often branching, fanning out, or recursing in data-dependent ways. Serving such workflows efficiently is challenging because their execution times are unpredictable and they often oversubscribe GPUs, meaning there are more LLMs than available GPUs. Traditional serving systems optimize each LLM independently, which can lead to poor resource utilization and high latency for the overall workflow.

## [AI Agents Aggregate Information in Prediction Markets but Hit Reasoning Ceiling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20050) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2604.20050v4) reports a controlled experiment where LLM-based AI agents trade in prediction markets after receiving private signals across four information structures of increasing complexity. The study finds that markets aggregate information well in easy structures but deteriorate in harder ones, suggesting AI agents struggle when more than two levels of interactive reasoning are required — a ceiling close to that documented in human subjects. This work provides experimental evidence that LLM-based agents inherit human-like limits in strategic reasoning, which matters for anyone deploying AI agents in economic or decision-making settings. It also shows that capability-frontier models improve aggregation in easier structures but not the hardest one, where they replace confidently wrong markets with markets that hedge near 0.5. The experiment varied cheap talk communication, market duration, strategic prompting, initial price, and feedback about past performance; none of these improved market accuracy, consistent with theoretical predictions. 'Smarter' agents performed better and were more profitable, but feedback did not improve aggregation, and a later wave with capability-frontier models still failed in the hardest structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Prediction markets are designed to aggregate dispersed private information through trading, giving participants an incentive to reveal what they know via prices. Interactive reasoning refers to the levels of strategic thinking agents use to infer others' knowledge from price movements, a concept studied in game theory and experimental economics. This paper applies that framework to LLM-based AI agents to test whether they can perform the same aggregation and reasoning tasks as humans.

## [VLAA-GUI: A Modular Framework for GUI Automation That Knows When to Stop, Recover, and Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21375) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VLAA-GUI is a modular GUI agentic framework that integrates three components — a mandatory Completeness Verifier, a mandatory Loop Breaker, and an on-demand Search Agent — to address early stopping and repetitive loops in autonomous GUI automation. Evaluated across five top-tier backbones including Opus 4.5, 4.6, and Gemini 3.1 Pro on Linux and Windows tasks, it achieves 77.5% on OSWorld and 61.0% on WindowsAgentArena, with three of the five backbones surpassing human performance (72.4%) on OSWorld in a single pass. This work tackles two fundamental failure modes that have limited the reliability of autonomous GUI agents, and its modular design allows the improvements to be applied to a range of backbone models. The strong benchmark results, including surpassing human performance on OSWorld, suggest that GUI automation agents are becoming viable for real-world deployment. The Completeness Verifier enforces UI-observable success criteria at every finish step, with an agent-level verifier that cross-examines completion claims using decision rules and rejects those lacking direct visual evidence. The Loop Breaker provides multi-tier filtering — switching interaction mode after repeated failures, forcing strategy changes after persistent screen-state recurrence, and binding reflection signals to strategy shifts — and ablation studies show it nearly halves wasted steps for loop-prone models, while a Coding Agent and Grounding Agent are invoked on demand for code-intensive actions and precise action grounding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous GUI agents are AI systems that interact with graphical user interfaces — clicking buttons, typing text, navigating menus — to complete tasks on a computer without human intervention. Two persistent problems have hindered their reliability: agents often declare success prematurely without verifiable evidence (early stopping), and they can get stuck repeating the same failing actions in a loop. VLAA-GUI is an arXiv preprint (arXiv:2604.21375v3) that proposes a modular architecture to address both issues, evaluated on OSWorld and WindowsAgentArena, two benchmarks for GUI automation tasks on Linux and Windows.

## [Human-1: First Open Full-Duplex Hindi Dialogue Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23295) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers at Josh Talks introduced Human-1, the first open and reproducible full-duplex spoken dialogue system for Hindi, built by adapting the Moshi duplex speech architecture with a custom Hindi tokenizer. The model was trained on 26,000 hours of real spontaneous conversations from 14,695 speakers with separate speaker channels, using a two-stage recipe of large-scale pre-training followed by fine-tuning on 1,000 hours of conversational data. Full-duplex dialogue systems have so far been explored almost exclusively for English and a few high-resource languages, so this work fills an important gap for Hindi and other Indian languages. It provides an open, reproducible baseline that speech and dialogue researchers can build on for low-resource, real-time conversational AI. To support Hindi text generation, the team replaced Moshi's original English tokenizer and reinitialized the text-vocabulary-dependent parameters while retaining the pre-trained audio components. Evaluation via the prompted dialogue continuation paradigm, using both automatic metrics and human judgments, showed the model produces natural and meaningful full-duplex conversational behavior in Hindi.

**Background**: Full-duplex spoken dialogue models listen and speak at the same time, enabling natural turn-taking, interruptions, overlaps, and backchannels with low latency, unlike traditional cascaded pipelines of voice activity detection, speech recognition, text dialogue, and text-to-speech. Moshi, released by Kyutai, is a speech-text foundation model that models two audio streams (the system's own speech and the user's) plus text tokens as an inner monologue, and Human-1 adapts this architecture to Hindi.

## [StraTA Adds Trajectory-Level Strategies to Agentic RL for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06642) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Strategic Trajectory Abstraction (StraTA), a framework that samples a compact trajectory-level strategy from the initial task state and conditions subsequent actions on it, trained jointly with a hierarchical GRPO-style rollout design. Experiments on ALFWorld, WebShop, and SciWorld show consistent gains, reaching 93.1% success on ALFWorld, 84.2% on WebShop, and a 63.5% overall score on SciWorld. Current LLM agent methods are largely reactive, which weakens exploration and credit assignment over long horizons; StraTA's explicit strategy layer addresses this bottleneck and could improve sample efficiency for agentic RL. Its reported outperformance of frontier closed-source models on SciWorld suggests hierarchical strategy abstraction is a promising direction for long-horizon decision-making agents. StraTA combines a hierarchical GRPO-style rollout with diverse strategy rollout and critical self-judgment, jointly training strategy generation and action execution. As a preprint without community discussion, its results are not yet independently validated, and the abstract does not detail computational overhead or failure cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly used for interactive tasks like embodied navigation and web shopping, but optimizing them for long-horizon decision making is hard because purely reactive policies struggle with exploration and credit assignment. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that simplifies training by removing the need for a separate critic model, and hierarchical variants extend it with multi-level decomposition. Benchmarks such as ALFWorld (text-based embodied tasks), WebShop (simulated e-commerce), and SciWorld (scientific reasoning environments) are standard testbeds for evaluating such agents.

## [Transformers Implement Preconditioned Richardson Iteration for Gaussian Kernel Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08475) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.08475v3) proves both theoretically and empirically that a standard softmax-attention transformer can approximate kernel ridge regression (KRR) with Gaussian kernels during its forward pass. The authors construct a single-head transformer whose forward pass approximately implements preconditioned Richardson iteration on the kernel system, using O(log(1/ε)) blocks and MLP width O(√(N/ε)) to achieve ε-accurate prediction for prompts of length N. This work offers a concrete mechanistic explanation for how transformers perform in-context learning, decomposing the roles of attention and MLP layers into cross-token and intra-token operations. It strengthens the theoretical foundation linking transformer architectures to classical kernel methods, which could inform future model design and interpretability research. The construction reveals a functional decomposition: softmax attention produces a row-normalized Gaussian-kernel operator for cross-token interactions, while MLP layers approximate the intra-token scalar arithmetic required by the update. Empirically, GPT-2-style transformers trained on Gaussian-process regression tasks progressively align with the exact Gaussian KRR estimator across depth in both prediction error and induced weights, and deeper layers align with later solver iterates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Kernel ridge regression (KRR) is a classical regression method that uses a kernel function to map data into a high-dimensional feature space, with Gaussian kernels being a common choice. Preconditioned Richardson iteration is an iterative numerical method for solving linear systems, where a preconditioner transforms the problem into a form more suitable for iterative solving. In-context learning refers to a transformer's ability to adapt its behavior based on examples provided in the input prompt without retraining.

## [CaC: Hierarchical Spatiotemporal Video Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11723) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CaC (Concentrate and Concentrate), a coarse-to-fine anomaly reward model built on Vision-Language Models that performs a global temporal scan, then fine-grained spatial grounding, and finally structured spatiotemporal Chain-of-Thought reasoning. It also releases the first large-scale generated video anomaly dataset with per-frame bounding boxes, temporal windows, and attribution labels, and uses a three-stage training paradigm including two-turn GRPO reinforcement learning. CaC reports a 25.7% accuracy improvement on fine-grained anomaly benchmarks and, when used as a reward signal, reduces generated-video anomalies by 11.7% while improving overall video quality, which could advance both video understanding and the reliability of video generation models. This matters for researchers and practitioners working on video anomaly detection, multimodal reasoning, and reward modeling for generative video. The training pipeline first learns spatial and temporal anchoring via single- and multi-frame supervised fine-tuning, then optimizes with a two-turn Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) strategy. Beyond conventional accuracy rewards, the authors introduce Temporal and Spatial IoU rewards to supervise intermediate localization, encouraging more grounded and interpretable spatiotemporal reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) are AI systems that jointly interpret images and text, extending large language models beyond text-only inputs. Chain-of-Thought reasoning is a prompting technique that guides models to produce intermediate reasoning steps, improving complex decision-making. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement-learning algorithm, originally developed for improving reasoning in large language models, that samples a group of responses per prompt and uses the group's mean reward as a baseline instead of a separate critic model.

## [Grid-Orch Uses LLMs and MCP to Automate Distribution Grid Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12728) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Grid-Orch, an LLM-powered orchestrator that connects large language models to power system simulation through the Model Context Protocol (MCP), using OpenDSS as its reference implementation. The framework provides 36 domain-specific tools across eleven categories, including power flow, voltage analysis, quasi-static time series (QSTS) simulation, and automated optimization, and supports both cloud-hosted models (Gemini, Claude) and locally deployed models (Ollama, llama-cpp) for air-gapped operation. The power distribution engineering workforce faces a projected shortage of up to 1.5 million engineers by 2030, so tools that let engineers perform complex analyses through natural language could significantly lower the barrier to entry. Grid-Orch's provider-agnostic design and air-gapped operation make it practical for security-sensitive utility environments, and its workflow demonstrations show that analyses formerly requiring hours of scripting, such as distributed energy resource (DER) interconnection screening, can complete in under two minutes. Grid-Orch is delivered as an interactive web platform with chat-based interaction, a QSTS dashboard, and feeder topology visualization, and it renders simulation results inline. Three optimization skills—capacitor placement, voltage violation analysis, and overvoltage mitigation—extend the platform beyond single-tool queries to multi-step engineering workflows, and the framework produces numerically identical results to direct OpenDSS scripting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard introduced by Anthropic in November 2024 that standardizes how AI systems like large language models integrate and share data with external tools, systems, and data sources; it has since been adopted by major AI providers including OpenAI and Google DeepMind. OpenDSS is a free, open-source electric power Distribution System Simulator used for distributed resource integration and grid modernization. Quasi-static time series (QSTS) simulation is a method for evaluating how distributed energy resources such as solar and batteries affect a grid over time, and it is widely used for hosting capacity analysis.

## [OASIS Stabilizes Attention Residuals by Routing Null Tokens to Depth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17887) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OASIS, an outlier- and sink-aware method that stabilizes dual-normalized attention-residual architectures (AttnResidual) via explicit Softmax1-based null routing at both token and depth levels, coupling token-level null evidence to downweight depth branches with stronger null behavior. Experiments across LLaMA-3.2-1B, Qwen3-0.6B, and Phi-4 show OASIS reduces maximum infinity norm by 81.75%, average kurtosis by 95.90%, lowers W8A8 perplexity by 82.00%, and improves W4A4 GSM8K Pass@1 by 42.11% on average compared to Vanilla AttnResidual. Attention sinks and activation outliers are major obstacles to low-bit quantization of large language models, and this work provides a principled, theoretically grounded solution that significantly improves post-quantization performance without sacrificing model quality. The method could enable more efficient deployment of transformer models on edge devices and reduce memory/compute costs, benefiting researchers and practitioners working on model compression and efficient inference. OASIS requires the Softmax1 attention variant (from OutEffHop) to be enabled; with vanilla softmax the null posterior is identically zero and the model reduces to plain Attention Residuals. The paper also provides a theoretical characterization of a conditional mechanism for sink-like attention concentration under dual normalization, explaining the low-bit sensitivity observed in AttnResidual.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Attention Residuals (AttnRes) replace fixed uniform residual connections in deep transformers with learned, content-dependent softmax-weighted depth retrieval, improving inter-layer routing flexibility but also amplifying attention sinks (tokens that attract disproportionate attention) and activation outliers. Softmax1 adds a null slot to the softmax denominator, allowing attention to be routed to a 'null' destination instead of overloading existing tokens, which helps mitigate sinks. OASIS builds on this by coupling token-level and depth-level null routing, addressing the decoupling that left depth branches weighting uninformative layers.

## [DyMoS Rebalances Reference-Frame Attention to Add Motion to Image-to-Video Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.19398v4) identifies "reference-frame dominance" as the cause of overly static image-to-video (I2V) outputs, showing that non-reference frames allocate excessive self-attention to reference-frame key tokens, which over-propagates the conditioning image across time and suppresses inter-frame dynamics. The authors propose DyMoS (Dynamic Motion Slider), a training-free, model-agnostic method that rebalances the attention pathway from generated frames to the reference frame during the initial denoising steps, leaving the input image and model weights untouched and exposing a single scalar for continuous motion control. Overly static videos are a well-known weakness of I2V generation relative to text-to-video, and prior fixes typically required extra training or degraded fidelity to the reference image. Because DyMoS is training-free, model-agnostic, and preserves both visual quality and reference fidelity, it can be dropped into existing state-of-the-art I2V backbones as a practical post-hoc control knob, potentially improving motion dynamics across the ecosystem without retraining. The method operates only during the initial denoising steps, introduces a single scalar parameter for continuous motion-strength control, and requires no changes to the input image or model weights. Experiments across multiple state-of-the-art I2V backbones reportedly show consistent improvements in motion dynamics while maintaining visual quality and fidelity to the reference image.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Image-to-video (I2V) models generate a video clip conditioned on a single still image, whereas text-to-video models start from text alone; I2V outputs tend to move less because the model leans heavily on the conditioning image. Diffusion-based video generators denoise a noisy latent over many steps, and self-attention lets tokens across frames exchange information, so how much attention generated frames pay to the reference frame strongly shapes the result. Prior work addressed static outputs by weakening or modifying the image-conditioning signal, but such approaches often need additional training or reduce fidelity to the reference image.

## [Cut-DeepONet Handles Discontinuities in Neural Operators via Domain Partitioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19823) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Cut-DeepONet, a two-stage training framework that explicitly models discontinuities in neural operators by partitioning the domain into smooth subregions and representing discontinuities as boundaries in a higher-dimensional space. An additional network predicts input-dependent discontinuity locations for unseen inputs, guiding the operator to generate smooth components within each region. Neural operators like DeepONet have struggled to capture discontinuities and sharp transitions in PDE solutions, typically requiring larger models and high-resolution data. Cut-DeepONet outperforms state-of-the-art methods on benchmark PDEs even with low-resolution training data and fewer trainable parameters, suggesting a representation-level fix rather than brute-force scaling. The method uses a lifting strategy that separates the operator learning task into smooth subregion modeling and discontinuity boundary prediction, aligning with the inductive bias of neural networks. Experiments show it excels on problems with discontinuities and sharp transitions while using fewer trainable parameters than competing approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Neural operators, such as DeepONet introduced by Lu et al. in 2020, are architectures that learn mappings between infinite-dimensional function spaces, enabling fast approximate solutions to partial differential equations (PDEs). DeepONet uses a branch network to encode input functions and a trunk network to encode query locations, combining them to produce the output function value. Because these architectures rely on continuous representations, they inherently struggle to represent discontinuities or sharp gradients in PDE solutions without significantly increasing model capacity and data resolution.

## [Hide-and-Seek Detects VLA Robot Failures from Trajectory Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30834) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Hide-and-Seek, a framework that formulates Vision-Language-Action (VLA) failure detection as a coarsely supervised learning problem, using inter-trajectory and intra-trajectory contrastive objectives to localize failure-indicative actions from trajectory-level supervision alone, without any step-level annotation. It is evaluated on LIBERO, VLABench, and a real-world robotic platform across OpenVLA, π0, and π0.5 policies, achieving state-of-the-art multi-task failure detection performance with a practical accuracy–timeliness trade-off under conformal prediction. Reliable failure detection during execution is critical for deploying embodied VLA systems in the real world, and existing methods either rely on expensive action resampling or external models, or propagate trajectory-level labels uniformly across timesteps, obscuring localized failure signals. Hide-and-Seek addresses this limitation and could have immediate impact on robotics and AI safety by enabling more robust runtime monitoring without costly step-level annotation. The method combines inter-trajectory and intra-trajectory contrastive objectives to induce temporally structured failure signals, and it generalizes well to both seen and unseen tasks. Evaluation spans three representative VLA policies (OpenVLA, π0, π0.5) and includes a conformal prediction component that provides a practical accuracy–timeliness trade-off.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that take an image or video of the robot's surroundings plus a natural language instruction and directly output low-level robot actions, typically built by fine-tuning a vision-language model on large-scale robot trajectory data. Contrastive learning is a machine learning approach that learns representations by distinguishing positive (similar) pairs from negative (dissimilar) pairs, and it underlies many modern representation-learning systems. Runtime monitoring for robot learning aims to detect errors or anomalies during task execution, which is essential for safe deployment of learning-enabled robotic systems.

## [INFUSER Uses Influence-Guided Self-Evolution to Boost LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09052) ⭐️ 7.0/10

INFUSER introduces an iterative co-training framework in which a Generator drafts questions and golden answers from unstructured documents while a Solver trains on them, with the Generator rewarded by an optimizer-aware influence score rather than a difficulty heuristic. To handle the continuous, noisy influence signal, the authors propose DuGRPO, a dual-normalized GRPO variant, and report over 20% relative improvement on Olympiad and SuperGPQA benchmarks with Qwen3-8B-Base. This addresses a key limitation of unsupervised self-evolution, where rewarding generators for difficulty alone does not guarantee solver improvement, potentially making self-improving LLM pipelines more data-efficient and less dependent on curated or teacher-generated datasets. The result that an 8B co-evolving generator outperforms a frozen 32B thinking generator suggests smaller models can drive curriculum generation effectively. The influence score is optimizer-aware and measures whether a proposed question would actually improve the solver on the target distribution, but it is continuous and noisy, which standard GRPO handles poorly. Ablations confirm each design choice is necessary, and extensions to an instruction-finetuned anchor and rule-verifiable RLVR data demonstrate flexibility; code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Self-evolution refers to a pretrained language model improving itself with minimal external supervision, often by generating its own training questions. Influence functions, originally from robust statistics and popularized in machine learning in 2017, estimate how much a given training sample affects a model's predictions or objective. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm used to train language models, and INFUSER modifies it to better handle continuous reward signals.

## [Genetic Algorithms with Optimization-Guided Operators Analyzed via Query Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12279) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a general model of genetic algorithms where mutation and recombination operators are guided by machine learning optimization rather than randomness, and formulates the optimization process as a query complexity problem using reinforcement learning language. The authors prove three fundamental phenomena: diversity in the solution pool can be necessary (for parity learning, optimal query complexity is Θ(w+2^{n-w})), generation, mutation, and recombination can all be simultaneously necessary, and a phase transition for Gaussian distributions yields exponential speedup when operators have positive drift. This work bridges classical genetic algorithm theory with modern machine learning, providing a rigorous theoretical foundation for the increasingly popular practice of applying genetic algorithms at inference time to iteratively improve solutions. It could influence the design of ML-guided optimization systems and deepen our understanding of when and why diversity and different operators matter. The paper shows that for parity learning, with pool size w and vectors of length n, the optimal query complexity is Θ(w+2^{n-w}), and that Θ(n^2) bits of memory are necessary for efficient success under general memory constraints. It also provides a phase transition for Gaussian distributions, demonstrating exponential speedup when operators have positive drift.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Genetic algorithms are search and optimization techniques inspired by natural selection, traditionally using random mutation and recombination operators. Recent machine learning approaches replace these random operators with optimization-guided ones that aim to improve solutions but are computationally more expensive. Query complexity measures the number of queries needed to solve a problem, and reinforcement learning provides a framework for analyzing sequential decision-making under uncertainty.

## [Embedding Model Routing Formalized as Low-Rank Contextual Bandits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14929) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.14929v2) formalizes embedding model routing as an adversarial contextual linear bandit with low-rank experts, where queries are contexts, items are actions, and embedding models are experts operating on low-rank latent spaces. It introduces a log-quadratic policy class and a Hypentropy Policy Gradient (HPG) algorithm that achieves O~(s√(MT)) linearized policy regret while adapting to unknown low-rank structure under bandit feedback. Embedding model routing is increasingly central to modern recommendation and retrieval systems that must dynamically dispatch diverse queries to multiple embedding models, yet it has lacked a rigorous theoretical treatment under realistic adversarial and bandit conditions. This work provides provable efficiency guarantees that avoid the curse of dimensionality, potentially guiding the design of more robust and scalable routing systems in industry. The paper first shows that standard regret notions suffer from structural misspecification or statistical intractability in this setting, motivating the log-quadratic policy class that is expressive enough for query-dependent routing yet structured enough for efficient learning. HPG is computationally efficient and parameter-free, with regret scaling in the intrinsic rank s of the experts, the number of models M, and the number of rounds T.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Contextual bandits are a sequential decision-making framework where a learner observes a context, chooses an action, and receives only feedback on the chosen action (bandit feedback), balancing exploration and exploitation. Embedding models map inputs such as text or images into dense vector representations used for similarity search and recommendations; routing means selecting which model to use for each query. Low-rank structure assumes that expert models operate in a lower-dimensional latent space, which can be exploited for statistical efficiency. Policy regret measures the gap between the learner's cumulative reward and that of the best policy in hindsight.

## [IRT Enables Efficient Safety Benchmarking for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20626) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.20626v2) applies Item Response Theory (IRT) to six widely used safety benchmarks, showing that adaptive item selection can approximate full-benchmark model rankings with Spearman's ρ > 0.90 while cutting evaluation cost by at least 80% on every benchmark where this threshold is attainable, and by up to 99.9% on AIR-Bench 2024. The paper also introduces a practical procedure for extracting a fixed, informative subset of items reusable across models, a static alternative with savings of 80–99.8%. Current safety benchmarks for language models are evaluated with static paradigms that treat all items as equally informative, which for adversarial, highly heterogeneous safety items would require on the order of 10^5 responses, most providing little ranking signal. By showing that psychometric methods can dramatically reduce evaluation costs while preserving model rankings, this work offers a practical path to more scalable and affordable AI safety evaluation across the pipeline. IRT recovers interpretable structure on safety benchmarks, with ability estimates resolving differences among models that cluster at the ceiling of raw safety metrics. Adaptive item selection dynamically chooses informative items for each model based on its responses, and the static fixed-subset alternative achieves savings of 80–99.8% across benchmarks; the 99.9% reduction was achieved on AIR-Bench 2024.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Item Response Theory (IRT) is a family of psychometric models that link a test taker's observed responses to items with an underlying latent trait, treating item difficulty and discrimination as information to be incorporated rather than assuming all items are equally informative. It is widely used in high-stakes assessments such as the GRE and GMAT. Adaptive item selection, related to computerized adaptive testing, dynamically chooses the most informative items for each examinee based on prior responses. Spearman's rank correlation coefficient (ρ) is a nonparametric measure of rank correlation ranging from −1 to 1, commonly used to compare rankings such as model leaderboards.

## [NMO Benchmark Uses Quantum Simulations for Nanotech Molecular Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30170) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced the Nanotechnology Molecular Optimization (NMO) Benchmark, which replaces proxy oracles with quantum simulations to evaluate generative molecular design against real nanotechnology targets. The suite imposes hard structural constraints and rugged fitness landscapes, and notably, advanced molecular optimization methods underperform simpler approaches on these tasks. This benchmark addresses the limitations of drug-discovery-centric molecular optimization by bridging machine learning and quantum materials science, potentially driving genuine scientific discovery in nanotechnology. It challenges the ML community to develop models that transfer to structurally distinct domains rather than overfitting to pharmaceutical benchmarks. The NMO tasks use physics-based quantum simulations to calculate real molecular properties, creating extremely rugged fitness landscapes and hard constraints due to molecule binding. The authors also developed a new baseline method with a novel representation for structural constraints and a domain-agnostic pretraining strategy to eliminate pharmaceutical dataset bias, surpassing state-of-the-art physical properties and revealing previously unknown structural motifs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Generative molecular design typically relies on proxy oracles—computationally cheap but approximate property predictors—and models pretrained on large pharmaceutical datasets, which can lead to benchmark overfitting and poor transferability to other domains. The NMO Benchmark replaces these proxies with rigorous quantum simulations, which are more accurate but computationally expensive, and introduces strict protocols to prioritize scientific utility over leaderboard performance. This shift requires generative models to handle hard structural constraints and rugged fitness landscapes, which are common in real-world molecular design but underrepresented in drug-discovery benchmarks.

## [CoFL-S: Language-Conditioned Sector Flow Fields for Local Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02222) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoFL-S is a low-level vision-language-action framework that predicts a language-conditioned flow field over the robot's local visible sector and generates continuous trajectories by rolling out that field. The authors also introduce a continuous-time Habitat benchmark that isolates low-level action interfaces and executes all methods through a shared velocity-command controller. Vision-Language Navigation research has largely focused on high-level instruction reasoning, memory, and global map construction, leaving low-level action representation comparatively underexplored. CoFL-S targets this gap and, under matched encoders and training settings, consistently outperforms baselines across planner frequencies, with zero-shot real-world closed-loop deployment showing advantages beyond simulation. To train the low-level representation, the authors convert each VLN-CE episode, originally a whole-episode instruction paired with an action sequence, into frame-level local supervision with aligned sub-instructions and matched action, trajectory, and dense flow-field targets. The continuous-time Habitat benchmark enables decomposition-independent closed-loop comparison across different planner frequencies rather than fixed discrete forward-and-turn transitions in VLN-CE.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Navigation (VLN) is a task where agents use natural language instructions and visual observations to navigate unfamiliar 3D environments, typically predicting a goal, waypoint, or action representation. Habitat is a widely used platform for embodied AI research that allows training and benchmarking embodied agents in simulated 3D scenes. Flow fields represent motion as a vector field over space, and in robotics they can encode dense directional guidance for continuous trajectory generation rather than discrete action choices.

## [CheapCT Probe Predicts Expensive 3D CT Encoder Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22771) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces CheapCT, a cheap probing method that forecasts the downstream performance of fine-tuned 3D CT encoders for text generation, validated on report generation and a newly built MeasureVQA dataset. The rank agreement between CheapCT and full fine-tuning stays high across every capability, with Spearman rho between 0.90 and 0.97, and the forecast survives changes to both the probe readout and the language-model backbone. Selecting a 3D CT image encoder currently requires fine-tuning every candidate through the full language model, an enormously expensive search that bottlenecks medical vision-language model development. CheapCT can pick an encoder nearly as good as the best while fine-tuning only a single candidate at orders of magnitude less compute, making efficient model selection practical for medical imaging researchers. MeasureVQA is a new VQA dataset that scores one capability at a time, with answers measured from segmentation masks and Hounsfield units, whereas report generation scores the whole report at once and mostly reflects disease. The code and MeasureVQA are released at https://github.com/renjie-liang/CheapCT.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: A 3D CT vision-language model typically pairs a 3D image encoder with a language model so that the system can generate text, such as radiology reports, from CT volumes. Probing is a technique that trains a small, cheap classifier on an encoder's frozen representations to test what information they contain, and Hounsfield units are the standardized radiodensity scale used in CT imaging. This paper asks whether such a cheap probe can reliably forecast the much more expensive outcome of fine-tuning the entire model.

## [Recover-and-Reguard Defense Closes the Decode Gap in VLM Jailbreaks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26574) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.26574v3) builds a preprocessor that recovers and decodes encoded inputs before they reach a vision-language model's safety guard, raising block rates on image-rendered jailbreaks from exactly zero to 67-90% against an ensemble of eleven encoding attacks. The authors also introduce a reguard step that re-screens the recovered pre-decode surface, which is the only intervention that consistently lowers ensemble attack success across all ten guard-target pairs. Safety classifiers are the dominant black-box defense for vision-language models, yet they judge surface form rather than meaning, so harmful requests re-encoded as set theory, formal logic, classical languages, code, or text inside images slip past them. This work quantifies that 'decode gap' and shows that recovery-based preprocessing can meaningfully improve coverage, which is likely to influence how future VLM guardrails are designed. The benign-traffic cost of recovery is set by the guard rather than the mechanism: one guard pays 9 benign blocking points for the same 70-point gain another pays 69 for. Critically, the defense does not make the system safer against an attacker free to choose among eleven encodings, since closing one channel relocates success rather than removing it, and no configuration in the full guard x target x condition factorial reaches an ensemble attack-success rate at or below 40% while holding benign over-refusal under 70%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) process both images and text, and their safety is typically enforced by a separate classifier or 'guard' that screens inputs before the model sees them. Jailbreak attacks exploit the fact that these guards evaluate the literal surface form of an input, so a harmful request expressed in an unusual encoding — such as formal logic notation or text embedded in an image — may pass through even though the underlying meaning is unchanged. This paper formalizes that mismatch as the 'decode gap' and tests a recover-and-reguard pipeline against a broad ensemble of encoding attacks.

## [Code-Encoding Best-of-N Jailbreak Breaks SAGE Defense 9-75x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26639) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.26639v2) shows that moving best-of-N jailbreaking variance from surface-level character scrambling into a structural code-completion encoding achieves 67%, 22%, and 15% attack success rates on three open-weight targets against the SAGE self-check defense, versus at most 4.7% for the encoding alone and 3.0% for the published character search at full budget — a 9 to 75x amplification. The findings reveal that SAGE's protection does not survive composition, and that fusing safety screening with response generation into a single pass is architecturally fragile, since every attacker draw redraws the safety decision; this has direct implications for how LLM defenses should be designed and red-teamed. At the actionable severity threshold, the cells read 24, 8, and 1 behaviors (95% CI [13,28], [3,13], [0,3]), and a 2x2 ablation shows transform defenses are broken by encoding depth (7→67 behaviors at fixed variation) while gate defenses are broken by variation breadth (13→57 at fixed encoding); repeated sampling also inflates apparent robustness because attackers experience the maximum over draws while safety is reported as means.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Best-of-N (BoN) jailbreaking spends a query budget generating many surface-level variations of a harmful request — scrambling and recasing text — until one bypasses the model's safety filter. SAGE is described as the strongest published self-check defense, where the model screens its own output before answering. Code-completion encoding (from Ren et al. 2024) rewrites a harmful request as a code task, routing the model into its code-completion mode, which alone barely dents SAGE but becomes far more effective when combined with BoN search.

## [TSDS: Calibrated Early Stopping and Cloud Deferral for Edge LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26865) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose TSDS (Think Short, Defer Smart), a framework that combines a lightweight convergence probe to halt on-device reasoning early with a perplexity-based deferral rule that escalates uncertain actions to a cloud model. Both mechanisms are jointly calibrated via a multi-objective Learn-Then-Test procedure, yielding finite-sample guarantees on expected episode reward and cloud-call rate, and TSDS cuts per-episode thinking compute by 43%-65% over deferral-only baselines on HotpotQA, MBPP, and a household robot task. Deploying LLM agents on resource-constrained edge devices requires balancing limited compute budgets against reliability, and TSDS offers a principled way to do so with statistical guarantees rather than ad-hoc thresholds. This could make multi-step ReAct agents more practical for on-device applications such as robotics and mobile assistants, where cloud calls are costly or latency-sensitive. The framework is evaluated on four ReAct benchmarks—GSM8K (arithmetic reasoning), HotpotQA (multi-hop QA), MBPP (code generation), and a household robot embodied planning task—and compared against thought-calibration-only and calibrated-deferral-only baselines. The joint calibration uses a multi-objective Learn-Then-Test procedure to simultaneously certify expected episode reward and cloud-call rate, though the reported compute savings are not observed on GSM8K.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: ReAct is a paradigm in which LLM agents interleave reasoning traces with actions to solve multi-step tasks, but each reasoning step consumes compute that is scarce on edge devices. Learn-Then-Test (LTT) is a statistical framework that calibrates machine learning models so their predictions satisfy explicit finite-sample guarantees regardless of the underlying model or unknown data distribution, typically by framing threshold selection as multiple hypothesis testing. Perplexity, a measure of how uncertain a language model is about its next token, is used here as a signal for when a local model should defer to a more capable cloud model.

## [LoRD Calibrates Language Model Log Anomaly Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17965) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.17965v4) shows that language model-based log anomaly detectors are persistently miscalibrated, assigning excessive confidence to incorrect predictions even when conventional calibration metrics look good. To fix this, the authors propose LoRD (Log Reconstruction and Distance), a lightweight post-hoc calibration framework that learns prediction-route-specific reliability models and selectively recalibrates high-risk predictions. Overconfident errors in operational monitoring can cause operators to miss real anomalies or waste time chasing false alarms, so improving confidence reliability directly affects the trustworthiness of AI-driven systems monitoring. The work is relevant to AI/ML reliability and large-scale systems operations, though it is an incremental research contribution rather than a paradigm shift. LoRD learns reliability models from latent representations of correctly classified validation samples and estimates reliability via route-wise reconstruction distances, then recalibrates only high-risk predictions to suppress overconfident errors while preserving reliable ones. Experiments on four large-scale log benchmark datasets and multiple language model-based detectors show consistent improvements in confidence reliability and substantially fewer overconfident anomaly-related errors without sacrificing detection performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Log anomaly detection is a core task in maintaining large-scale computing systems, and recent approaches use language models to flag unusual log entries. Model calibration refers to how well a model's predicted confidence matches its actual accuracy; a model can have high detection performance yet still be badly calibrated. Post-hoc calibration methods such as Platt scaling adjust a trained model's outputs using validation data, and LoRD extends this idea with a route-specific, reconstruction-based approach.

## [PolyChirp Brings Multiclass Birdsong Detection to NPU-Equipped Microcontrollers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23101) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PolyChirp, a new approach described in arXiv paper 2608.23101v2, combines biological domain expertise, automated dataset curation, neural architecture optimization, and NPU-accelerated hardware to perform multiclass bird species detection on low-power microcontrollers. It achieves robust classification of up to 10 species simultaneously with a macro F2 of up to 0.97, while matching or exceeding retrained TinyChirp single-species architectures, and a data-driven front-end redesign makes on-device mel feature extraction 7x to 11x cheaper. Prior TinyML acoustic monitoring on microcontrollers was limited to binary classification of a single species, but real wildlife deployments usually need to monitor many species at once. PolyChirp shows that multiclass bioacoustic detection can fit within the flash, latency, and energy budget of a battery-powered sensor, which could make long-term, low-cost ecological monitoring substantially more practical. The paper evaluates models on common microcontroller hardware, measuring flash footprint, latency, and energy consumption, and reports that PolyChirp still fits a low-power microcontroller sensor's budget. The key caveat is that the headline macro F2 of 0.97 applies to the up-to-10-species setting, and the work relies on newly designed tiny multiclass models plus an NPU rather than general-purpose microcontrollers.

## [Provably Safe Sim-to-Real Transfer via Reward-Free Safe RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01418) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.01418v2) formulates safe sim-to-real transfer as a reward-free safe reinforcement learning problem, in which real-world data is collected once and reused to plan for arbitrary reward functions. The authors develop a computationally efficient algorithm that identifies where simulator and real dynamics differ, uses certified simulator transitions where reliable, and estimates mismatched transitions from safely collected data, guaranteeing with high probability that every deployed policy is feasible. This work bridges two important trends—sim-to-real transfer and safe reinforcement learning—by providing provable safety guarantees during real-world data collection, which is critical for robotics and healthcare where unsafe exploration can cause physical harm. The reward-free formulation also means data collected once can serve many downstream tasks, potentially reducing the cost of deploying RL in the real world. The algorithm's sample complexity improves on the best-known online reward-free safe RL bound by a factor of Θ̃(H/ξ²) when the simulator is uninformative, and by Θ̃(H²|S||A|/(ξ²|B|)) when the simulator is accurate on most transitions, where |B| is the size of the sim-to-real mismatch region and ξ is the safety margin of a baseline policy. The guarantees hold with high probability, but the analysis assumes a finite state-action space and a known safety margin.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Sim-to-real transfer refers to training a reinforcement learning agent in a simulator and deploying it in the real world, which is attractive because simulators provide cheap data but suffer from mismatch between simulated and real dynamics. Safe reinforcement learning aims to ensure that an agent avoids catastrophic actions during learning, typically by incorporating constraints into the optimization. Reward-free reinforcement learning collects data without a specific reward function so that the same dataset can later be used to compute optimal policies for many different tasks.

## [New Bounds for Linear Accessibility in Feature Superposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09556) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper derives high-probability bounds for linear accessibility in feature superposition by framing it as a compressed sensing problem, proving that the sufficient dimension scales linearly as d = O_ε(k log m) rather than the prior worst-case quadratic limits. It also introduces IHT-SAE, a learned iterative refinement method that improves feature recovery beyond the limits of linear availability. This work quantifies the geometric constraints of the linear representation hypothesis, providing a rigorous framework for evaluating sparse autoencoders, compositional generalization, and neural interpretability. It could directly influence how researchers design and assess feature extraction methods in mechanistic interpretability. The analysis characterizes the asymmetry between active and inactive interference and the trade-off between interference and observation-noise budgets, validated through Gaussian-tail approximations. The IHT-SAE method uses learned iterative refinement to push feature recovery beyond the theoretical linear accessibility limits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Feature superposition is the phenomenon where neural networks encode more concepts than they have dimensions, which helps explain polysemantic neurons but introduces cross-feature interference. Compressed sensing is a framework for recovering sparse signals from fewer measurements than the signal dimension, and sparse autoencoders are a popular tool for extracting interpretable features from neural activations.

## [EGGROLL Analyzed: Low-Rank Evolution Strategies Theory and LOO-ROLL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10980) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper theoretically analyzes EGGROLL, the low-rank evolution strategy method for scaling ES to LLMs, characterizing its update mean field as a resolvent applied to the gradient of the perturbation-smoothed objective, showing that rank-one perturbations lie in a zero-volume subset of matrix space and can reverse local stability of an optimum. It also introduces LOO-ROLL, a leave-one-out estimator that replaces EGGROLL's two antithetic evaluations per direction with one, halving estimator MSE at equal evaluation cost and yielding matched-time gains of 2.9, 14.1, and 7.9 percentage points on GSM8K at 1.7B, 8B, and 14B parameters. This work matters because EGGROLL is one of the few practical ways to apply evolution strategies to LLM-scale training, and the paper both clarifies its theoretical limitations and offers a concrete improvement (LOO-ROLL) that delivers measurable gains on math benchmarks up to 14B parameters. It gives optimization and LLM-training researchers a more rigorous foundation for deciding when low-rank ES is safe and how to make it more sample-efficient. The analysis shows EGGROLL still recovers the gradient exactly on quadratic objectives at every rank and radius, and that the extra sampling variance of rank-one perturbations relative to dense Gaussian ES decays inversely with matrix width, being only 0.098% at width 4096. Across fourteen post-training settings up to 14B parameters, matched-time comparisons with EGGROLL yield eleven improvements in individual paired tests and no significant loss.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Evolution strategies (ES) are a class of black-box optimization methods that estimate gradients by sampling random perturbations of parameters and weighting them by the resulting objective values, avoiding backpropagation. Applying ES to large language models is challenging because dense Gaussian perturbations over billions of weights are prohibitively expensive, so EGGROLL instead uses low-rank Gaussian products, often rank one, to make the perturbations computationally tractable. This paper studies the geometry and stability of that low-rank choice and proposes a variance-reduced estimator on top of it.

A new arXiv paper introduces the concept of "second-order patterns"—latent patterns that neural networks learn when recognizing human-defined patterns—and proposes a new task called second-order pattern recognition. HCNA recognizes a second-order pattern as applicable to an unseen utterance when that utterance's representation falls within the cluster's extrapolation space, and experiments show this extrapolation space substantially improves task performance.

## [Internal Dual-Wiener Routing Reliability-Weights Distant Gradients in BPTT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12890) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Internal Dual-Wiener routing (Internal-DW), a backward-only intervention that reliability-weights internal gradient routes in backpropagation through time while preserving the full forward rollout and all step losses. On four history-dominated, weak-drive testbeds, Internal-DW reduces forecast error by 5.2%–13.8% relative to full BPTT and outperforms gradient clipping and Jacobian regularization on three testbeds. Long-horizon autoregressive forecasting relies on distant supervision, but repeated Jacobian products can let large distant gradients dominate updates even when their signal-to-noise ratio is low. This work shows that retaining long-horizon supervision does not require trusting every backward contribution equally, offering a practical alternative to gradient clipping and truncated BPTT for sequence models. At each residual block, Internal-DW derives bounded Wiener gains for the identity and nonlinear routes, estimated from route-level gradient statistics and an explicit noise model, to balance preserving predictable signal against suppressing unpredictable variation. The authors note a clear applicability boundary: benefits diminish or reverse when usable history is limited or when the selected sampler fails to represent dominant drive-dependent variation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Backpropagation through time (BPTT) is the standard method for training recurrent and autoregressive sequence models: it unrolls the network over time and propagates gradients from each step's loss back through many steps. Because each step multiplies by a Jacobian, distant gradients can grow or shrink dramatically, a long-standing issue related to the credit assignment problem—deciding which past steps deserve credit for current errors. The Wiener filter is a classic signal-processing tool for estimating a target signal from noisy observations by balancing signal preservation against noise suppression, and this paper adapts that idea to gradient routing.

<a id="item-257"></a>
## [State of Thought Enables Endogenous Reasoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16055) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes State of Thought (SoT), a new reasoning paradigm that extracts a compact dynamics-geometric state from a model's internal information transfer and uses a 582-parameter controller on frozen backbones to selectively activate historical reasoning support. Across 3 LLMs and 16 datasets, SoT improves mean-baseline accuracy while reducing generated tokens by 62.6% and end-to-end latency by 44.6%. SoT addresses the generalization and efficiency limits of existing test-time compute methods that rely on externally imposed control, such as fixed reasoning programs or costly search. If validated, this state-driven approach could make LLM reasoning cheaper and more generalizable, affecting how inference systems are designed for quantitative, symbolic, code, and long-context tasks. SoT reports speedups of 1.34x on quantitative, 1.62x on general, 1.76x on symbolic-and-code, and 2.51x on long-context reasoning, and across 2 VLM scales and 3 reasoning tasks it improves mean accuracy by 3.8 points with 74.9% fewer completion tokens and 73.5% lower latency than search-based methods. Under constrained access it retains 38.2%/36.5% mean accuracy gains in training-free/embedding-only settings, while trajectory-only judging reaches 84.1% agreement across 3 API models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time compute has become a major way to boost LLM capabilities, but most existing paradigms rely on externally imposed control, either through fixed reasoning programs or through costly expansion in constrained search spaces. SoT instead frames reasoning as a state-conditioned process over evidence rather than an externally prescribed token chain, with the model's internal reasoning state governing how reasoning unfolds. The approach builds on the idea that a compact internal state can capture the dynamics and geometry of information transfer within the model.
