<a id="item-258"></a>
## [ProtoLIP disentangles object-level visual evidence in vision-language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16284) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ProtoLIP introduces a lightweight prototype-mediated evidence layer that organizes reusable visual prototypes into text-derived semantic families and routes evidence coarse-to-fine, disentangling object-level visual evidence in query-conditioned vision-language models. Across four object- and phrase-level out-of-distribution benchmarks, it achieves average relative gains of 29% in Pointing and 43% in Energy, and its localization gains transfer to independently pretrained VLMs. This addresses a persistent interpretability problem in vision-language models, where sentence-level evidence does not cleanly decompose into object-specific contributions and object-level evidence remains entangled with co-occurring objects and scene context. By constructing image-text matching scores directly from localized prototype evidence, ProtoLIP enables exact decomposition without spatial annotations or backbone retraining, which could improve trust and debugging in multimodal systems. ProtoLIP uses coarse-to-fine evidence routing where semantic families constrain prototype eligibility and the complete query determines fine-grained prototype contributions. It improves image-text matching discrimination on the primary backbone while remaining competitive with a spatially supervised grounding model in object-level localization, and it requires no spatial annotations or backbone retraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Query-conditioned vision-language models aim to reveal which visual content supports a given textual query and how that evidence changes across queries, enabling fine-grained interpretation. However, prior work shows that exposed evidence maps do not necessarily correspond to the evidence that directly constitutes the model's prediction, and object-level evidence can remain entangled with co-occurring objects and surrounding scene context. ProtoLIP builds on this by introducing a prototype-mediated layer that organizes reusable visual prototypes into text-derived semantic families.

<a id="item-259"></a>
## [HOPE: Higher-Order Pruning of Experts in MoE Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18916) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced HOPE (Higher-Order Pruning of Experts), a second-order pruning objective for Mixture-of-Experts language models that provably minimizes an upper bound on pruning error by accounting for expert interactions. They show that the state-of-the-art first-order method REAP is a special case of HOPE where interaction terms are ignored, and across three frontier MoE models up to 122B parameters, HOPE achieved an average rank of 1.58 out of 5 methods at 50% pruning, versus 2.42 for REAP, with gains up to +6.1% on agentic coding. MoE models suffer from large parameter counts and memory bottlenecks, so better expert pruning directly enables more aggressive compression with less quality loss. Because HOPE's advantage is largest at high pruning rates and on complex agentic workloads, it could make large MoE models substantially cheaper to deploy without sacrificing performance on the hardest tasks. HOPE is a second-order objective that provably minimizes an upper bound on the error caused by pruning, and it was validated across three frontier MoE models (up to 122B parameters), two distinct calibration sets, and multiple benchmarks including math, instruction following, coding, and an agentic suite. Its advantage is most pronounced at high pruning rates and on challenging agentic workloads, where diverse expert combinations are invoked over long sequences.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) language models increase model capacity by sparsely activating only a subset of parameters, called experts, for each token, which keeps computation manageable but leaves a very large total parameter count and a memory bottleneck. Expert pruning removes less important experts to shrink the model, but existing methods decide each expert's fate independently and assume experts contribute additively. In reality, experts in MoE models are used cooperatively, so ignoring their interactions can lead to worse pruning decisions.

<a id="item-260"></a>
## [Matrix AdaGrad Adds Row-wise and Column-wise Adaptive Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21815) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21815v2) develops an Online Mirror Descent framework with adaptive proximal functions for matrix-valued parameters, deriving two concrete algorithms: Row-wise Matrix AdaGrad and Column-wise Matrix AdaGrad. The framework shows that row-wise and column-wise adaptive scaling emerge naturally from the corresponding proximal geometries, with regret guarantees strictly tighter than entry-wise AdaGrad under row-sparse or column-sparse gradient structures. Most widely used adaptive optimizers such as AdaGrad and Adam were designed for vector-valued parameters and ignore the matrix structure of weight matrices in neural networks. This work provides a principled theoretical route to matrix-aware adaptivity, which could improve optimization performance and training stability for deep learning practitioners, especially at larger learning rates and greater network depths. The framework reveals a trade-off in adaptive scaling: increasing scaling factors reduces the gradient-dependent dual norm term but raises the cost of evolving the proximal geometry. Under diagonal parameterization the row-wise trade-off becomes separable, allowing each row's scaling to be derived independently by minimizing its row-wise regret bound, and the column-wise version follows by applying the row-wise construction to the transposed matrix.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: AdaGrad is a classic adaptive subgradient method that scales each parameter's learning rate by the accumulated squared gradients, and it is grounded in online learning and regret minimization. Online Mirror Descent is a general algorithmic template that generalizes gradient descent by choosing a proximal function that defines the optimization geometry. This paper extends that template to matrix-valued parameters by introducing row-wise and column-wise matrix proximal functions, so that adaptive scaling respects the two-dimensional structure of weight matrices rather than treating every entry independently.

## [MM-ContextFold: Training-Free Context Folding for Multimodal Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23121) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose MM-ContextFold, a training-free framework that keeps a persistent text-only main context for high-level planning and spawns ephemeral branch contexts only when images are needed, folding results back as concise textual summaries. Backed by an empirical study of roughly 10,000 trajectories, it improves average accuracy by 6.3 percentage points over ReAct while cutting working context length by 27.5% across seven benchmarks and five backbone models. Multimodal agentic retrieval systems suffer from context explosion because raw images and interaction history accumulate in a single ever-growing context, and this work offers a practical, training-free way to manage token-intensive visual content. It could directly benefit researchers and engineers building multimodal RAG and agent pipelines who need to control cost and latency without retraining models. The empirical study shows that as visual cues are progressively extracted by external tools and textualized, raw images become increasingly redundant, and continued image retention is associated with higher output entropy and can even degrade task accuracy. The framework therefore discards images and branch traces after each subtask, keeping only a concise textual summary in the main context.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Agentic Retrieval (MAR) requires agents to solve complex information-seeking tasks by iteratively invoking external tools, and typical frameworks such as ReAct maintain raw multimodal inputs and the accumulating interaction history in a single, ever-growing context. This leads to the context explosion problem, where token usage balloons and reasoning quality suffers. While prior work compresses redundant text, strategies for managing token-intensive visual content remain largely underexplored.

## [Lifted Bellman Linear Programming Reformulates Offline RL Without Bootstrapped Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24489) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.24489v2) introduces Lifted Bellman Linear Programming (LBLP), which imposes in-sample Bellman optimality on the critic through inequality constraints in the joint (Q,V) space rather than through bootstrapped regression. The authors also propose ALBUM, a neural implementation that detaches K-step rollout targets via stop-gradient and requires no target networks or EMA updates, matching FQL's average performance on OGBench with the fewest parameters and least peak GPU memory among compared methods. This offers a fresh theoretical perspective on offline RL by replacing the standard bootstrapped regression paradigm with a linear programming characterization of Bellman optimality, potentially eliminating the need for target networks and off-policy correction. If the approach scales, it could simplify offline RL pipelines and reduce the computational overhead that has made these methods costly to train. The LBLP's unique minimizer is the in-sample optimal pair, and constraints along K-step trajectory segments leave this minimizer unchanged for any rollout policy and horizon; under deterministic dynamics, the minimizer lies between the best dataset return and the optimal value. Relaxing constraints into hinge penalties recovers the same solution above a finite penalty coefficient in the tabular case, and the detached update has the LBLP solution as a stationary point under a coefficient condition independent of gamma and K.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning aims to learn policies from a fixed dataset without interacting with the environment. Standard methods train a critic by minimizing regression loss against bootstrapped value targets, which are stabilized by target networks with exponential moving average (EMA) updates; multi-step targets that incorporate behavior-policy actions require off-policy correction. Bellman optimality is classically characterized as a linear program, and this paper lifts that characterization to the joint (Q,V) space so every constraint involves only state-action pairs present in the dataset.

## [Quality-Constrained Image Coding Boosts Machine Vision Bitrate Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25108) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a quality-constrained image coding method for machines that caps human-perceived quality at a desired level and allocates the remaining bits to machine task performance. It introduces two penalty function variants—an absolute function and a bilinear function with a steeper slope beyond the target—achieving BD-rates of -22.82% and -29.81% versus unconstrained joint optimization and a rate-distortion baseline, respectively. As visual data is increasingly consumed by machine-vision systems rather than humans, this approach enables significant bitrate savings while maintaining task performance and acceptable human-validatable quality. It could impact multimedia systems, autonomous perception, and any pipeline where humans only need to inspect or validate machine decisions. The codec meets the target visual quality with reasonable error and adds no complexity overhead, using joint compression-segmentation training recast as a constrained optimization problem. The bilinear penalty applies a steeper slope once the target quality is exceeded, guiding quality more aggressively toward the constraint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Image Coding for Machines (ICM) compresses images assuming the primary observer is a computer vision application, unlike traditional codecs such as JPEG or VVC that target human viewers. Just-noticeable distortion (JND) refers to the maximum distortion the human visual system cannot perceive, and this work uses it as inspiration to cap human-observed quality. Constrained optimization with penalty functions is a standard technique for enforcing constraints by augmenting the objective with a penalty for violations.

The ArGuard shared task released its evaluation report on harmful content detection in Arabic memes and LLM prompts, with 58 registered teams, 35 final participants, and 27 system-description papers. Best systems reached macro-F1 of 0.823 on A1, 0.419 on A2, 0.984 on B1, and 0.790 on B2, using models such as AraBERT, Jais, and Qwen3-VL. This is one of the first community-scale benchmarks targeting harmful content detection in Arabic, a relatively low-resource language for AI safety research, and it covers both multimodal memes and LLM prompt safety. The large gap between prompt detection (0.984) and fine-grained meme classification (0.419) highlights concrete open challenges for Arabic multimodal hate speech detection and LLM safety evaluation. Track A covers multimodal hate detection in Arabic memes with subtasks A1 and A2, while Track B addresses harmful prompt detection for Arabic LLM safety with subtasks B1 and B2. The A2 fine-grained meme classification was the hardest setting, partly due to sparse labels and train-test distribution shifts.

## [TGL-NSGA-II: Teacher-Guided Fitness Approximation for TinyML Architecture Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30553) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces TGL-NSGA-II, a teacher-guided low-fidelity framework for constrained TinyML neural architecture search that uses a pretrained teacher to stratify samples by difficulty and class, applies a capped knowledge-distillation procedure (KD-Lite) to each candidate, and fuses the resulting teacher-guided score with a Gaussian-process surrogate to decide which candidates merit full evaluation. On keyword spotting and bird-call classification, measured Kendall-τ values reach 0.74 and 0.62, exceeding predicted lower bounds of 0.60 and 0.46, while joint stratification cuts proxy-score variance by 41% and the method runs 2.2x faster than full NSGA-II. TinyML architecture search is bottlenecked by the cost of fully training and evaluating every candidate on constrained hardware, so a reliable low-fidelity ranking method could substantially cut search budgets for edge-AI deployment. The paper also provides theoretical analysis of evaluation variance, rank inversion, and hypervolume perturbation, offering guarantees at the population level that may inform how surrogate-assisted evolutionary search is designed more broadly. The framework derives a variance-aware fusion weight and a capacity-adaptive distillation rule, and the authors note that selective teacher mismatch increases differential bias and drops Kendall-τ to 0.41. The guarantees apply only to population-level low-fidelity evaluation and do not establish convergence of the complete evolutionary trajectory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: NSGA-II is a widely used non-dominated sorting multi-objective evolutionary algorithm that uses elitism and crowding distance to approximate a Pareto-optimal set. TinyML neural architecture search aims to find the smallest, fastest models that fit microcontrollers and other low-power edge devices, but evaluating each candidate is expensive. ## [BAER routes evidence adaptively to improve pairwise LLM judging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30751) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Backbone-Adaptive Evidence Routing (BAER), a method that selects among three symmetric expert heads—evidence stacking, reliability-based expert routing, and candidate-blind reference verification—for each benchmark-backbone condition, with the choice frozen before testing. Across four benchmarks and two 8B judge backbones, BAER achieves the highest test accuracy in all eight conditions, with gains of 0.87–7.32 points over the strongest external baseline and full prediction coverage. Pairwise LLM judges are widely used for evaluating model outputs, but no single evidence-gathering protocol works best across all benchmarks and judge backbones. BAER shows that adapting how evidence is gathered—rather than fixing one protocol—can consistently improve judge accuracy, which matters for the reliability of LLM-as-a-judge evaluation pipelines. BAER preserves candidate symmetry by separating each expert's signed preference from candidate-invariant reliability, ensuring that swapping the two responses may reverse the preference but cannot change its strength. The development data select one head per benchmark-backbone condition, and that choice is frozen before testing, which avoids test-time leakage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Pairwise LLM judging presents a judge model with a question and two candidate answers and asks which is better, and it is a common approach in LLM-as-a-judge evaluation. Judges can gather evidence through direct comparison, reasoning, or reference-based verification, but prior work typically fixes one protocol. BAER builds on this by routing among multiple evidence mechanisms while preserving symmetry, so that the judge's preference strength is invariant to the order of the candidates.

## [ACV-Gate Cuts Encoder Compute for Visual Token Communication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30756) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose ACV-Gate, an adaptive candidate evaluation framework that learns to approximate full-budget counterfactual evaluation and selectively assigns exact evaluations only to the most informative visual tokens. On CIFAR-10 at 0.20 bpp, the primary adaptive configuration improves PSNR over LocalMDL by 0.636 dB while using only 2.13 candidate evaluations per image, about 27.60% of the calls required by the Exact-Full expert. Generative image communication must pick which semantic tokens to transmit under a strict packet budget, and accurately valuing each candidate token normally requires repeated receiver-side reconstruction that is expensive on the encoder. By learning terminal-value rankings and refining only a bounded candidate set, ACV-Gate offers a controllable way to trade encoder computation for reconstruction quality, which matters for bandwidth-constrained and low-bit-rate image transmission systems. The framework trains a set-aware student using terminal advantages and regrets to predict candidate rankings directly, while a selective refinement mechanism evaluates only a bounded set containing Local-MDL and direct actions, with cost-based thresholds controlling average evaluation workload. Matched-candidate comparisons, synchronized GPU measurements, and transfer tests on STL-10 and at 384x384 scale show consistent quality-computation trade-offs, with gains most pronounced at low bit rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Generative image communication encodes an image into compact semantic tokens that are transmitted under a limited packet budget and then decoded by a generative receiver; which tokens are selected strongly affects the final reconstruction quality. Counterfactual evaluation means estimating the terminal value of each candidate token by simulating the full-budget decoding outcome, which is accurate but computationally heavy because it requires repeated receiver-side reconstruction. ACV-Gate amortizes this cost by learning to approximate those evaluations and spending exact evaluations only where they matter most.

## [HasMem Adaptively Resizes Continuous Memory for Frozen LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30797) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HasMem (Hard-Origin Adaptively Softened Memory) introduces a controller-based method that adaptively resizes and re-encodes continuous memory entries for a frozen LLM, using frozen hard-prompt embeddings as a verifiable initial state. On a 535-question reconstruction probe derived from the Multi-Session Chat (MSC) development split, its main configuration reaches lexical F1 of 95.3 (+4.4 points) at 93.6% of the hard reference's framed memory positions, and on all 500 LongMemEval-S questions local lexical F1 rises from 3.4 to 8.9 while answer NLL falls from 12.257 to 5.274. Long-term LLM agents must reuse past interactions, but growing context windows make text-based memory and context compression costly, and resizing continuous memory couples capacity allocation with readout. HasMem shows that a learned controller plus Writer/Reader adaptation can beat hard-prompt and rule-based re-encoding baselines, suggesting a practical path to more efficient memory management for frozen models without retraining the base LLM. The method uses a controller to adjust memory widths, a Writer to re-encode resized entries, and Reader and Global components for readout adaptation and cross-turn state; with approximately matched per-question target body budgets, six configurations at mean per-entry retention around 0.83–0.91 exceed rule-based re-encoding by 8.0–23.6 exact-match (EM) percentage points. Notably, F1 gains accompany lower EM on both evaluations, and the Global EM gain passes a user-level exact paired test with Bonferroni correction over eight comparisons only at a fixed rule target width ratio of 0.75.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents accumulate information across sessions, but a model's context window is finite, so systems externalize memory as text or as compressed continuous vectors. Soft context compression condenses context into a few memory tokens that a frozen LLM consumes in place of raw text, but existing compressors typically fix the compression ratio at training and inference, requiring a separately trained model for each deployed ratio. HasMem addresses this by starting from frozen hard-prompt embeddings and letting a controller adaptively resize continuous memory while the base LLM stays frozen.

## [JevSoup: Training-Free LoRA Routing via Orthogonal Projection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30922) ⭐️ 6.0/10

JevSoup introduces a training-free framework that separates expert routing (System One) from execution (System Two), selecting two LoRA experts using only the input and expert descriptions. It retains the leading expert's update, projects the second onto the orthogonal complement of the first update's row space, and combines them with equal weights, achieving absolute gains of up to 1.19% in task-macro and 1.21% in sample-micro accuracy across 14 PorTAL tasks and three Qwen3 scales. This work offers a lightweight, training-free approach to composing multiple LoRA adapters, which could simplify deployment of modular expert capabilities without auxiliary data or autoregressive decoding. It matters for practitioners seeking efficient multi-task adaptation of large language models, though the modest gains suggest incremental rather than transformative progress. The method relies on structured probabilities from expert descriptions to select two experts, then uses orthogonal projection to minimize interference between their updates. Gains are measured against the strongest evaluated external baselines on 14 PorTAL tasks across three Qwen3 model scales, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained model weights and injects small trainable low-rank matrices into each Transformer layer, enabling rapid customization with far fewer parameters. Combining multiple LoRA experts allows a single base model to handle diverse tasks, but routing among them typically requires auxiliary data, additional training, or autoregressive decoding. Orthogonal projection is a mathematical operation that removes components of one vector that lie along another, used here to reduce interference between combined expert updates. Qwen3 is a family of large language models available at multiple scales.

## [Purin Adds Biology-Inspired Synaptic Modulation to CNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31235) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.31235) proposes Purin, a biology-inspired mechanism that adds short- and long-term synaptic efficacy modulation to conventional convolutional neural networks. Purin uses a time-interval-based abstraction of neural activity, avoiding discrete time-steps, and reports improved classification accuracy on AlexNet, VGG11, and GoogLeNet after removing confounding factors. Most ANNs use fixed weights within a training batch, ignoring rapid synaptic changes that real neurons exhibit, so Purin offers a lightweight way to bring short-term plasticity into mainstream CNN architectures. If validated, this could inspire new bio-inspired designs that improve accuracy without requiring recurrent or spiking temporal machinery. Purin introduces a bounded factor for temporary synaptic efficacy changes plus two weight matrices representing input-side and output-side efficacy, updated by backpropagation and interpreted as long-term synaptic changes. The reported gains are modest and come from a preprint that has not been peer-reviewed, with experiments limited to three older CNN architectures and unspecified datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Artificial neural networks typically model synaptic transmission as fixed trainable weights during a training batch, which omits short-term changes in synaptic efficacy that biological neurons show. In neuroscience, short-term synaptic plasticity refers to activity-history-dependent changes in synaptic strength, while long-term changes are often associated with learning and memory. Purin aims to bridge these ideas by abstracting neural activity into time intervals rather than discrete simulation steps, making the mechanism compatible with standard convolutional architectures.

## [G2MAF: Test-Time Gradient Guidance for Multi-Agent Flow Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31286) ⭐️ 6.0/10

G2MAF is a test-time refinement framework for offline multi-agent reinforcement learning that applies a single globally normalized, projected critic gradient to guide and coordinate all agents' corrections while keeping the joint action feasible and close to the frozen policy proposal. Across 24 MPE and SMAC settings, its canonical variant improves 20 frozen settings, with mean relative gains of 9.2% on MPE and 8.9% on SMAC, while adding only about 6% inference latency. The work shows that a frozen offline multi-agent policy need not commit to a single one-shot joint action, since lightweight test-time gradient guidance can recover better nearby alternatives without any further environment interaction. This matters for deploying offline MARL in cooperative control settings where retraining or online exploration is expensive or unsafe. The method keeps the frozen policy intact and only refines its proposal at deployment, using one globally normalized projected critic gradient to coordinate all agents rather than per-agent updates. Gains are reported as mean relative improvements over 24 MPE and SMAC settings, with the added cost limited to roughly 6% inference latency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Offline multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) trains cooperative policies from fixed datasets without further environment interaction, and the learned policy is typically frozen at deployment. MPE (Multi-Agent Particle Environment) and SMAC (StarCraft Multi-Agent Challenge) are two widely used benchmark suites for evaluating cooperative MARL algorithms. Flow policies are expressive generative action models that produce actions by integrating a learned velocity field, and test-time gradient guidance uses critic gradients to steer such generative policies during sampling rather than during training.

## [ReVerPi Study: Completed Pairs Can Hide Capped Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31381) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv case study of ReVerPi, a Pi extension that replaces older tool observations with compact addressable excerpts, reports that analyzing only the 15 completed pairs (12/15 success per arm) hides 12 boundary runs where the runner suppressed the companion arm whenever the first arm failed to complete. Restoring all 27 boundary runs bounds projected-minus-full success between -9 and +1 tasks, and within the 11 jointly correct pairs projection cuts aggregate logical tokens by 25% while raising the median pair's tokens by 29% and total suffix requests from 35 to 55. The paper shows that evaluation methodology itself can manufacture apparent wins: stopping rules, unexecuted companion arms, and resource aggregation choices can make context projection look better than it is. This matters for anyone benchmarking LLM agent memory or context-compression techniques, since selective reporting of completed pairs may overstate token savings and hide bounded failures. The study is a methodological case study of one recorded 86-run campaign with 641 model requests, not a population-level claim of noninferiority or superiority over unrestricted Pi; one omitted selector-chosen projected continuation successfully retrieved archive text but exhausted twelve requests, while its full counterpart answered in three. Separating fitting from evaluation also changed the selector's apparent tie: outside its four fitting pairs it incurred one extra failure and 8.6% more logical tokens over thirteen comparable runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Context projection is a technique in LLM agents where older tool observations are replaced with compact, addressable excerpts, reducing repeated input but potentially adding evidence-retrieval turns. ReVerPi is a standalone extension for Pi agents that keeps observations as exact, addressable evidence the agent can search and re-read, plus a local control-plane gateway. The paper connects stopping rules, known bounded failures, unexecuted companions, and resource aggregation, and recommends retaining every intervention boundary, executing both allocated arms independently, and reporting completion alongside interaction and token expenditure.

A new arXiv preprint (arXiv:2609.31460) introduces SeLATM, a framework that performs topic modeling at the segment level and refines topics through agentic feedback loops. Experiments across multiple datasets show it significantly reduces LLM resource consumption compared with topic-assignment-based methods while maintaining superior performance. LLM-based topic modeling has been limited by high token costs and coarse topic granularity, which are especially problematic for industrial-scale document analysis. By cutting resource usage while improving topic quality, SeLATM could make LLM-driven topic discovery practical for large corpora in text mining, market research, and enterprise data exploration. SeLATM replaces the conventional prompt-then-assign pipeline with segment-level topic generation plus an agentic feedback refinement loop, which also enables topic distributions over a document rather than a single hard assignment. The abstract reports reduced LLM resource use and better performance, but provides no specific numbers, model choices, or dataset names, and the work has not yet undergone peer review.

Recently, researchers have prompted large language models to generate and assign topics, producing more human-readable labels but at the cost of processing every document through the LLM. SeLATM sits in this newer line of work, aiming to keep the readability benefits of LLMs while avoiding the expense of assigning topics to every full document.

## [Diagnostic Framework Analyzes Generative AI's Cultural Impacts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31482) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31482) introduces a diagnostic framework that decomposes generative AI interventions into three coordinates: the epistemic site where a technology acts, the governing logic by which it organizes its object, and the technical mechanism through which that logic is instantiated. Applying this to information discovery and knowledge synthesis, the paper traces how shifts from indexicality to inference and from editorial authority to statistical consensus produce specific cultural effects. The framework offers policymakers and scholars a more precise way to distinguish structural cultural consequences of generative AI from contingent ones that remain open to design and institutional choice, potentially improving AI governance by targeting the right properties of the system. This matters as generative AI increasingly reshapes how knowledge is found, synthesized, and held accountable. The paper is conceptual and lacks empirical validation, and its abstract is somewhat repetitive. It argues that imprecise historical analogies—such as comparing generative AI to the printing press or electricity—lead to imprecise governance by designing interventions against the wrong property of the system.

**Background**: Generative AI refers to technology that creates new content by learning from vast datasets through machine learning algorithms, unlike traditional AI that mainly analyzes existing data. ## [Prompt Minimization: Cutting LLM Input Redundancy While Preserving Output Fidelity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31505) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv proposal (arXiv:2609.31505v1) introduces the concept of 'prompt minimization' — reducing prompts to their smallest, most information-dense form while preserving output fidelity — and proposes three variant frameworks to identify and evaluate minimal prompts. The author reports that minimal prompts often produce outputs comparable to those of their longer counterparts. Shorter prompts directly reduce computational overhead and inference latency, which matters most when large contexts such as entire documents or codebases are included unnecessarily. The work also suggests that longer prompts can damage LLM reasoning and accuracy, so prompt minimization could improve both efficiency and output quality for anyone deploying LLMs at scale. The paper is an early-stage proposal with a clear theoretical framing but no experimental results yet; it argues that the existence of multiple prompts yielding equivalent outputs implies a high degree of redundancy in the input space. It raises fundamental questions about what information is truly essential to elicit specific model behaviors, and frames prompt minimization as a new direction for efficient prompt engineering and input compression in LLMs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) such as GPT-4 and Claude generate text by conditioning on an input prompt, and prompt design today remains largely heuristic and ad hoc. Because inference cost and latency scale with input length, and because overly long prompts can degrade reasoning, researchers have growing interest in compressing or trimming prompts. 'Prompt minimization' extends this line of work by asking how small a prompt can become before output fidelity is lost.

## [DeepEdu-v1: Efficient Agentic LLMs for Vietnamese Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31568) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced DeepEdu-v1, an AI tutoring system for Vietnamese education built on the SCALE framework, which combines a long-context inference engine that amortizes token selection from per-sub-chunk to per-cluster granularity with a self-improving agentic layer that curates a verified playbook from past interactions instead of fine-tuning. In its deployed configuration, DeepEdu achieves nearly a 2x TTFT speedup over standard vLLM serving and lifts agentic accuracy from 70.0% to 79.5% on complex tasks, issuing 7.7x fewer retrieval calls than a state-of-the-art selective-attention baseline while cutting prefill latency by roughly 35%. This work targets a practical and region-specific problem: cloud assistants like ChatGPT route sensitive student data to foreign servers, violating data-sovereignty laws such as Vietnam's Decree 53, while self-hosted open models struggle with memory and latency on consumer GPUs. By addressing data sovereignty, curriculum alignment, and resource constraints simultaneously, DeepEdu-v1 offers a blueprint for deploying AI tutoring in developing regions where both cloud dependence and local hardware limits are real barriers. The system's long-context inference engine reduces retrieval calls by 7.7x and prefill latency (TTFT) by roughly 35% compared to a state-of-the-art selective-attention baseline, while matching or improving task accuracy. The self-improving agentic layer is designed to progressively reduce reliance on dominant-language priors as trustworthy local knowledge accumulates, with the strongest per-track gains seen in financial-reasoning and interactive-agent benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training quantization techniques such as AWQ and GPTQ reduce the static weight footprint of large language models, but they do not solve the dynamic KV cache and prefill latency problems that cause out-of-memory failures and slow responses on consumer GPUs during long tutoring contexts. Vietnam's Decree 53, which took effect at the end of 2022, requires certain online service providers to store Vietnamese users' data inside Vietnam, making foreign cloud-based AI assistants legally problematic for sensitive student data. DeepEdu-v1 aims to keep data on-premise while aligning with the national textbook curriculum, addressing both legal and pedagogical gaps.

## [Advection-Aware GNNs Don't Beat Simpler Models for Solar Ramp Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30286) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A controlled study on a synthetic testbed with a known wind field shows that an explicit advection graph does not outperform a plain static or learned-adjacency spatiotemporal GNN when using a realistic cross-correlation cloud-motion vector (CMV) estimate. The authors also introduce a self-supervised cloud-motion estimator that recovers the true wind vector to 2-4 degrees median angular error, 2-4x better than the classical cross-correlation method, and feeding its vector to the forecaster closes about 60% of the oracle-CMV RMSE gap at moderate wind. This challenges a common assumption in spatiotemporal GNN forecasting that explicit advection graphs are necessary for propagating cloud-induced ramps, showing that most of the benefit comes simply from providing an accurate motion vector as an input feature rather than from graph structure. The finding is a useful, counterintuitive insight for practitioners in renewable energy forecasting and graph-based machine learning. Advection only helps when the advective displacement over the forecast horizon, v*H, fits inside the sensor network, and the study also reports a negative result for a spatially-coherent probabilistic head. All claims are established on a single synthetic simulator, and the authors discuss why real-network validation is the necessary next step.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Short-term forecasting of cloud-induced power ramps across distributed photovoltaic (PV) or irradiance sensors is a recognized pain point for grid operators. A natural idea is to make graph neural networks advection-aware by connecting each site to upwind sites with edge time-lags set by the cloud-motion vector, so that a ramp is propagated forward before it physically arrives. Cloud-motion vectors are typically estimated using classical cross-correlation methods on satellite or sky images.

A new arXiv survey (2609.30292) reviews 211 fake review detection studies published from 2018 to early 2026, organizing the field by evidence source and fusion level. It traces the evolution from traditional machine learning and deep learning to pre-trained language model (PLM) and large language model (LLM) based methods. Fake reviews distort ratings, recommendations, and consumer trust, and LLMs now make generating fluent deceptive reviews easier while also offering stronger detection representations. This survey gives researchers and platform operators a unified map of the field and highlights open problems in adversarial generation, cross-domain transfer, and trustworthy evaluation. The survey covers evidence sources including review text, sentiment, rating behavior, temporal metadata, user-product graphs, multimodal content, external knowledge, and LLM-generated signals, and analyzes performance trends on Amazon, Yelp, and OpSpam benchmarks. It cautions that reported results are hard to compare due to differing label construction procedures, data splits, and evaluation protocols.

## [SignTrace Uses LLMs to Find Signs from Movement Descriptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30295) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SignTrace is an LLM-powered system that lets users describe a sign's movement in natural language to find the matching entry in a Chinese sign-language dictionary of 6,699 entries. It achieves 94.0% Hit@1, 97.4% Hit@9, and an MRR of 0.9540 on a 500-query benchmark, and has been deployed for user trials. It tackles the longstanding reverse-lookup problem in sign-language learning, where a learner remembers a sign's movement but cannot find its meaning or formal feature codes. By connecting everyday movement descriptions to documented signs, it offers a practical accessibility tool for learners and could inspire similar natural-language retrieval interfaces for other structured dictionaries. The pipeline combines LLM-based dictionary enrichment, action extraction, dictionary-style rewriting, seven-channel retrieval, and candidate reranking; reranking alone raises Hit@1 from 71.8% to 94.0%. Median query-processing time is 13.37 seconds with six concurrent queries, and the authors note that dictionary-derived wording and prior selection in the benchmark limit generalization to descriptions independently produced by users.

Information-retrieval systems commonly use metrics such as Hit@K and mean reciprocal rank (MRR) to measure whether the correct answer appears near the top of a ranked list. Two-stage retrieval with reranking is a standard technique in which a fast first pass gathers candidates and a more precise model reorders them.

## [Cost-Aware Best-LLM Identification via Dueling Bandits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30360) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.30360) formulates a cost-aware dueling bandit problem for identifying the best LLM among candidates with heterogeneous querying costs, and proposes a Track-and-Stop style algorithm for best-arm identification with prescribed confidence. The authors prove the algorithm almost surely achieves asymptotically optimal cost as the error tends to zero, and validate it on synthetic and real-world datasets. LLM evaluation is increasingly expensive because querying frontier models costs far more than smaller ones, so a principled method for finding the best model under a budget could make benchmarking and model selection substantially cheaper. The work also extends the theory of cost-aware best-arm identification with dueling feedback, a setting relevant to any preference-based comparison system. The algorithm assumes the existence of a Condorcet winner — an arm that beats every other arm in pairwise comparison — a condition the authors empirically validate across multiple real-world datasets. It follows the Track-and-Stop paradigm, which tracks the optimal allocation of samples across arms and stops when a prescribed confidence level is reached, and the paper reports consistent improvements over cost-unaware algorithms and their cost-aware extensions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-armed bandit (MAB) is a classic online decision framework that balances exploration and exploitation when repeatedly choosing among options with uncertain rewards. In the dueling bandit variant, the learner does not observe numerical rewards but only the outcome of pairwise comparisons, which naturally models preference or ranking feedback. Best-arm identification is the fixed-confidence version of the problem, where the goal is to determine the single best option with high probability while minimizing the number of samples or total cost. Track-and-Stop is a well-known asymptotically optimal strategy for this task in the standard stochastic setting.

## [Contrastive Learning Decodes Climate Model Parameter Sensitivities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30420) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers developed an explainable contrastive learning model that maps five monthly cloud and radiation fields from two 100-member CAM6 perturbed parameter ensembles into a shared representation space, separating the KK2000 and TAU-ML warm rain microphysics schemes with over 94% linear classification accuracy. Integrated Gradients attributions further link model differences to specific regions, seasons, and physical parameters such as cloud microphysics, boundary layer turbulence, and deep convection. This work shows that explainable machine learning can turn complex, multivariate climate model ensembles into interpretable diagnostics, helping scientists understand how physics parameterizations affect simulations and how models compare with satellite observations. It could accelerate model calibration and evaluation, benefiting climate modeling and AI-for-science communities. The model is trained on 34 parameters across two PPEs that differ only in the warm rain microphysics scheme, and it preserves seasonal variability and ensemble spread. In the shared space, satellite observations lie on the same low-dimensional manifold but are most displaced during boreal spring and autumn, with the TAU-ML ensemble closer to observations than KK2000.

**Background**: Perturbed parameter ensembles (PPEs) are collections of climate simulations in which uncertain physics parameters are varied to reveal their effects on model output. The Community Atmosphere Model version 6 (CAM6) is a widely used atmospheric model, and microphysics schemes such as KK2000 and TAU-ML represent how cloud droplets form rain. Contrastive learning is a machine learning technique that learns representations by pulling similar samples together and pushing dissimilar ones apart, while Integrated Gradients is an attribution method that highlights which inputs most influence a model's output.

## [Proportional Representation in Temporal Voting with Ranked Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30555) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.30555) studies proportional representation in temporal voting where one candidate is selected per round and voters hold ranked preferences that may change over time. It defines proportionality relative to every admissible cutoff interpretation and establishes a hierarchy of axioms, showing that no version of extended justified representation (EJR) can be guaranteed, unlike with approval ballots. This is a solid theoretical contribution to computational social choice, extending proportional representation from approval ballots to ranked preferences in repeated temporal decisions. It matters for researchers designing multi-agent decision systems, participatory budgeting, and repeated elections, though it is niche for broader software engineering or AI/ML audiences. With a fixed common cutoff, JR, PJR, and PSC can be guaranteed only by rules that see all preferences in advance; once the cutoff varies across rounds, even such rules cannot guarantee JR or PSC for groups that agree in only some rounds. Under individual cutoffs, no version of JR or PJR can be guaranteed, yet serial dictatorship achieves PJR up to an additive loss that no rule can improve on, and checking the axioms is often coNP-complete.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Computational social choice studies how to aggregate individual preferences into collective decisions from a computational perspective. Justified representation (JR) and its stronger variants PJR, EJR, and PSC are fairness axioms from multiwinner approval voting that require sufficiently large cohesive voter groups to secure a fair share of approved candidates. Temporal voting extends this to settings where decisions are made repeatedly over a fixed horizon, and prior work focused on approval ballots rather than ranked preferences.

## [MedTokenBudget Routes Lesion-Enriched Tokens for Dermoscopy ViTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30613) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MedTokenBudget introduces a supervised post-backbone token routing framework that learns to build compact lesion-enriched representations for Vision Transformers when auxiliary lesion masks are available. Its Lesion-Aware Token Scoring (LATS) module fuses attention entropy, feature norm, and local feature contrast through a learned scorer, then routes the top-K patches under a target budget, and on ISIC 2019 it consistently outperforms Random and ToMe at headline budgets while retaining substantially more lesion patches. Existing token pruning methods reduce tokens using generic saliency or similarity signals but rarely verify whether the retained subset still contains the lesion, which risks discarding diagnostic evidence in medical imaging. By directly measuring lesion retention rate, this work points toward efficiency-oriented ViT pipelines that remain clinically trustworthy, potentially benefiting dermoscopy screening and other lesion-centric diagnostic tasks. LATS is trained with budget curriculum learning, diversity regularization, attention distillation, and lesion-mask supervision, and the trained router is evaluated with a lesion retention rate that directly measures how much ground-truth lesion evidence survives the token budget. The method assumes auxiliary lesion masks are available, and code plus complete tabulated results are provided for reproducibility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) split an image into patches, treat each patch as a token, and process all tokens uniformly through self-attention, which is computationally expensive. Token pruning methods such as ToMe (Token Merging) reduce this cost by dropping or merging uninformative tokens, but they typically rely on generic saliency or similarity signals rather than task-specific evidence. In dermoscopy, diagnostic evidence is concentrated in the lesion region, so a pruning method that ignores lesion location may remove the very patches a classifier needs.

## [Paper Warns of 'Authorship Hazard' When LLM Agents Govern Dataspaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30614) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.30614) identifies an 'authorship hazard' in which an LLM agent can simultaneously be a subject of a dataspace's governance policies and an author of those same policies, and proposes the principle that an agent should be a subject of the governance plane, never an author of it. On a frozen corpus of agent drafts, publishing without approval reversed 80 authorization decisions, mostly through drafts that changed only a field's sensitivity classification rather than any policy text. As enterprises deploy multi-agent systems that compose tool calls and spawn sub-agents, the boundary between who is governed and who writes the rules becomes a real security and compliance problem, not a theoretical one. The paper argues that existing research on agents generating governance artifacts only evaluates output quality, leaving the question of who may authorize an artifact for use unowned by either the agent literature or the governance literature. The authors propose closing the agent's authorization channel to publication by construction, treating its influence channel (drafting what humans approve) as an enforcement problem, and designing a registry-held classification mechanism that is modelled but not yet implemented in the prototype. At the execution boundary, protected fields reached the model in 105 of 105 cases under prompt-stated duties but in 0 of 105 when an ODRL duty was compiled into an invocation-time tool-call constraint, though the compiled condition still exposed values in 7 of 7 cases where the value was not confined to a named field.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Dataspaces are shared data ecosystems, often built on standards such as IDSA and ODRL, in which a connector decides whether a data transfer may occur rather than inspecting what the transferred value contains. This design works for contracted applications but breaks down for LLM agents that compose tool calls and spawn sub-agents, because a published policy is exactly what a dataspace's decision point enforces, making publication itself a governance event. The paper sits at the intersection of AI governance, policy enforcement, and multi-agent systems, where a separate governance plane is increasingly seen as distinct from the observability layer.

## [Causal Retention Framework for Interactive Agents via Causal Core](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30650) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a theoretical framework for causal retention in interactive agents, characterizing optimal probe error as a Bayes decision risk and showing it vanishes exactly when every learning-interface fiber lies within one probe-answer fiber. The authors implement these conditions in a system called Causal Core, which uses evidence-gated writing, readout filtering, temporal credit, hidden-context setup, and local diagnostic updates, and validate it on finite causal systems, continuous simulators, an official TD-MPC2 world model, and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct. This work distinguishes causal retention from task sufficiency and source-domain decodability, suggesting that agents can perform well while failing to retain the causal mechanisms they were trained on. It could influence how researchers evaluate and design interactive agents, particularly in reinforcement learning and causal inference, by providing formal conditions and a concrete architecture for preserving intervention mechanisms. A frozen Qwen last-layer probe reaches 0.958 balanced accuracy on source mechanisms but only 0.583 on changed delays, while the gated mechanism state reaches 1.000 and accepts only 0.056 of synchronized-readout candidates; in TD-MPC2, five target states per actuator recover effect-sign accuracy from 0.057 to 0.948 without degrading stable responses. The framework includes a posterior-coverage theorem for budgeted retesting and an exact edit decomposition showing the shifted set is the unique support of an error-free target update.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Structural causal models (SCMs) are mathematical frameworks that use directed graphs and structural equations to represent causal mechanisms in a system, allowing researchers to reason about interventions and counterfactuals. In reinforcement learning, interactive agents learn policies from experience, but it is often unclear whether their internal representations preserve the causal structure of the environment. Bayes decision risk is a decision-theoretic quantity that measures the minimum expected loss achievable by an optimal decision rule under uncertainty, and here it is used to characterize the best possible probe error for detecting retained causal mechanisms.

## [Threat-Aware Energy-Efficient Deployment for Dynamic UAV Networks via Multi-Agent RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30690) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.30690) proposes a three-stage framework that combines a threat-aware K-means (TAKM) algorithm, an optimal matching stage, and a threat-aware multi-agent MATD3 reinforcement learning algorithm to maximize global energy efficiency while guaranteeing safe operation of multi-UAV aerial base station networks. Simulations report zero observed safety violations, superior energy efficiency, faster convergence than other learning methods and non-clustering baselines, and generalization to unseen user distributions, larger UAV fleets, and different threat geometries. Multi-UAV aerial base stations are increasingly considered for flexible wireless coverage, but operating them in threat-prone environments while conserving onboard energy is a hard joint safety-and-efficiency problem. This work shows that embedding threat awareness directly into clustering and multi-agent reinforcement learning can deliver zero safety violations with lower online computational complexity than heuristic optimization such as GPSO, which matters for practical real-time UAV deployment. The framework first uses TAKM, a projection-based variant of K-means that forces UAV centroids to remain inside verified safe regions and determines the minimum number of UAVs needed; then an optimal matching stage assigns physical UAVs to centroids to minimize energy expenditure; finally a threat-aware MATD3 agent dynamically optimizes trajectories, transmit power, and user associations. The paper claims performance comparable to optimized PSO (OPSO) but with significantly lower online deployment complexity, though results are simulation-based and limited to the considered scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) can act as flying base stations to provide wireless coverage where terrestrial infrastructure is unavailable or damaged. Because UAVs run on limited battery power, energy efficiency is a central design goal, and when they operate in hostile or threat-prone areas, safety constraints become equally important. K-means clustering is commonly used to group users and place UAVs, while multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) lets multiple UAVs learn cooperative control policies; MATD3 is a multi-agent extension of the twin delayed deep deterministic policy gradient algorithm designed for continuous control. This paper combines these ideas so that clustering, assignment, and learned control all respect threat information.

## [SAGE: Unified Frequency-Equalized Framework for RGB-T Alignment and Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30703) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers proposed SAGE (Source-Anchored Guidance via Frequency Equalization), a unified framework that integrates frequency equalization, hierarchical alignment, and subband fusion for RGB-T image fusion. It uses invertible joint encoding and source-specific low-frequency modulation to derive structural and gain guidance, then performs hierarchical frequency collaborative alignment and guided subband fusion via inverse wavelet transform. RGB-T fusion is widely used in autonomous driving, surveillance, and robotics, where thermal and visible cameras must be combined despite spatial misregistration and cross-modal discrepancies. By unifying appearance adaptation, geometric alignment, and fusion into one framework, SAGE could reduce ghosting and structural blurring in weakly registered image pairs, potentially improving downstream tasks like detection and segmentation. SAGE estimates global affine geometry from low-frequency approximations and transfers geometric and contextual cues to high-frequency correlation reasoning for reliability-aware residual refinement. Experiments on RGB-T datasets with both real-world and synthetic misalignments show consistently competitive alignment and fusion performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: RGB-T image fusion combines visible (RGB) and thermal (T) images to produce a single image containing complementary information, which is useful when illumination is poor or when thermal cues matter. Spatial misregistration means the two images are not perfectly aligned pixel-by-pixel, and cross-modal discrepancies refer to differences in how the same scene appears in each modality. Frequency-domain methods, such as wavelet transforms, decompose images into low- and high-frequency subbands, where low frequencies capture global structure and high frequencies capture edges and details; histogram equalization is a related classical technique for balancing intensity distributions.

Researchers propose VLALight, a lightweight end-to-end vision-language-action framework that directly maps multi-view intersection observations and signal-phase information to discrete traffic signal actions using a compact 0.5B-parameter model, without intermediate image-to-text descriptions or handcrafted traffic-state representations. Experiments show it achieves the best emergency-vehicle service among compared methods, reducing pooled emergency waiting time by 21.1% over the cascaded VLMLight while running in real time on local hardware and generalizing to unseen intersection topologies and traffic-flow patterns. VLALight combines multiple directional camera views into a unified visual input and uses textual instructions to establish correspondence between views, traffic movements, and signal phases, enabling direct action prediction with a 0.5B-parameter model. The reported 21.1% reduction in pooled emergency waiting time is measured against the cascaded VLMLight baseline, and the system is claimed to run in real time on local hardware while generalizing to unseen intersection topologies and traffic-flow patterns.

## [Source Framing Beats Document Order in Gemma 4 Conflict Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30716) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.30716) reports a fully counterbalanced behavioral evaluation of Google's Gemma 4-e4b model across 13 items and 784 forward passes, testing how it prioritizes two conflicting documents. The study finds that source framing (e.g., 'official guideline' vs. 'fresh update') significantly outweighs presentation order, though a variable primacy effect favoring the first document remains. The results suggest that how a source is semantically framed may matter more than where it appears in the prompt, which has direct implications for RAG pipelines, prompt engineering, and any system that feeds conflicting documents to an LLM. It also adds evidence that positional bias is not a fixed mechanical artifact but is modulated by surface wording and structural repetition. The counterbalanced design mathematically isolates source-framing and reading-position effects while canceling out the model's natural vocabulary biases, but the sample is small (n=13 items) and limited to a single model in short, single-turn contexts. The primacy effect fluctuated by at least a factor of 5 based on surface wording, and it grew significantly when the two competing documents used word-for-word identical templates.

## [XPhysICS: Cross-Physical-Domain Threat Grounding for ICS Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30805) ⭐️ 6.0/10

XPhysICS introduces a provenance-aware, target-conditioned method that separates analyst-guided source abstraction from deterministic grounding into target-specific validation slices, evaluating candidate mappings against five eligibility criteria: role compatibility, implemented type compatibility, stage coherence, slice viability, and rule-surface applicability. The authors evaluate 83 structured source-threat abstractions across water treatment, water distribution, hydro/water-energy, and chemical-process targets, including SWaT-to-water-treatment and WADI-to-water-distribution groundings and nine Hydro/GRFICS cases. The work shows that reusing ICS threat intelligence across different physical domains requires more than semantic similarity, which could change how defenders adapt threat models from one plant to another. It matters for ICS security practitioners and researchers who currently rely on informal analogy when transferring threat knowledge between water, energy, and chemical facilities. XPhysICS keeps grounding acceptance, slice adequacy, dynamic realizability, consumer applicability, and consumer outcome as distinct evidence layers, and target contracts are schema-validated before grounding or execution. The evaluation also covers bounded predictive, state-aware, and phase-aware consumer lanes, the unmodified upstream GeCo implementation, and a paper-derived reproduction of a physics-guided search method over three frozen groundings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Industrial control systems (ICS) run physical processes such as water treatment and chemical production, and threats documented for one plant may produce cyber-physical effects relevant to another. Traditional threat modeling relies on semantic similarity between descriptions, but that does not prove an effect is structurally admissible or evaluable on a different target. XPhysICS addresses this gap by requiring traceable source semantics and explicit target-conditioned grounding criteria before any target-side evidence is considered.

## [Persistent Negatives Stabilize Adversarial Black-Box On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30864) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces persistent-negative adversarial distillation, a live-pool method that replaces a fraction of each discriminator batch with historical, prompt-matched teacher-student comparisons to counter the moving-target problem in black-box on-policy distillation. Under matched discriminator compute, it consistently improves performance across two student families, three judges, and four judged-chat benchmarks, while producing smoother fresh-policy discriminator trajectories with fewer below-chance dips. This work identifies the discriminator's negative distribution as an important design axis in black-box on-policy distillation, offering a practical fix for reward instability that could make adversarial distillation more reliable for training LLMs from proprietary teacher APIs. It matters to researchers working on LLM distillation, RLHF, and reinforcement learning with GRPO, where stable reward estimation is critical. The analysis identifies the Bayes-optimal reward as a teacher-to-negative log-density ratio and, under explicit assumptions, shows that persistent negatives anchor the discriminator and reduce reward-estimation MSE relative to fresh-negative training. GRPO remains on-policy with fresh student responses, while only the discriminator batch mixes in historical comparisons, keeping the policy update on-policy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Black-box on-policy distillation aims to improve a student model using its own generations when the teacher only provides sampled responses rather than token probabilities. Adversarial distillation learns a discriminator over prompt-matched teacher and student responses and uses its score as the policy reward, but sampling negatives from the latest student couples the reward to a distribution that changes after every policy update, creating a moving-target problem. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm designed for LLM alignment that reduces compute by comparing groups of responses.

## [SemISAC Unifies Semantic Communication and Sensing in One 6G Waveform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30891) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.30891) proposes SemISAC, a dual-function waveform that performs both semantic communication (SemCom) and semantic sensing within a single integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) signal. Using a joint deep learning encoder, it converts road-scene images into semantic symbols placed on OFDM data cells, while adaptively optimized pilot cells handle channel estimation and sensing; simulations in a vehicular scenario show segmentation accuracy close to a dedicated SemCom module while outperforming conventional and semantic baselines in target recognition and range estimation. This work is significant because it moves beyond applying semantic technology to only the communication or sensing module of ISAC, instead jointly optimizing both within one waveform, which could improve spectrum efficiency and enable richer environment-aware services in future 6G networks. It matters for researchers and engineers working on 6G, vehicular networks, and joint communication-sensing system design. The transmitter uses a deep learning encoder to map input road-scene images to semantic symbols on OFDM data cells, with remaining cells serving as pilots whose configuration is adaptively optimized based on channel conditions to balance communication and sensing. At the receiver, a deep learning model reconstructs pixel-wise segmentation, while the transmitting vehicle uses reflected waveforms and task-specific decoders for target recognition and range estimation; however, results are simulation-based and the paper is a non-peer-reviewed preprint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic communication is an emerging paradigm that transmits the meaning or semantics of data rather than raw bits, using deep learning to extract essential task-relevant information and reduce redundancy. Integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) allows the same radio signals and network infrastructure to both carry data and sense the physical environment, and it is considered a key technology for 6G. OFDM is a widely used multicarrier modulation scheme in which some subcarriers, called pilots, are reserved for channel state information estimation, a necessary step before demodulation in time-varying wireless channels.

<a id="item-308"></a>
## [Unified Protocol Tests Robust Counterfactual Explanations Across Model Changes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30918) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a unified cross-family evaluation protocol that holds factual instances and generated counterfactuals fixed while testing six robust counterfactual explanation methods and two standard baselines against the same eight types of model change on four tabular datasets. It reports that bounded parameter perturbations change only 0.95% of test predictions on average, compared with 4.9% for bootstrap retraining, and that methods with guarantees for one change type do not necessarily transfer to others. Robust counterfactual explanations are meant to give recourse that survives model updates, but existing methods are each evaluated against the single change type they were designed for, so reported robustness scores are not comparable. A common protocol that specifies model changes and measures their realized behavioral magnitude could make robustness claims meaningful and guide practitioners choosing explanation methods for deployed systems. The benchmark characterizes every changed classifier through its outputs and reports empirical robustness alongside coverage, base validity, and proximity, keeping generation performance separate from robustness. RobX transfers most consistently across change families in the experiments, though greater stability can require larger interventions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Counterfactual explanations are a type of explainable AI output that tells a user what minimal changes to their input would flip a model's decision, such as what a loan applicant would need to alter to be approved. Robust counterfactual explanations aim to remain valid after the underlying model changes, for example through parameter perturbation, retraining on new data, or a new architecture. Because these changes are different events, a method tuned for one may fail on another, which is the gap this paper addresses.

## [Spackle: Residual Gaussians Fix Capacity Competition in Single-Image NVS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30941) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.30941) introduces Spackle, a lightweight residual learning framework for single-image novel view synthesis (NVS) that mitigates the 'capacity competition' problem in hybrid 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) plus diffusion pipelines. Spackle works in three stages — predicting base 3DGS attributes from the input view, automatically identifying poorly reconstructed regions, and learning a residual 3DGS optimized only for those areas — then combines baseline and augmented Gaussians at inference, achieving state-of-the-art results on large-view-deviation cases. Single-image NVS is a key capability for turning a single photo into a navigable 3D scene, which matters for VR/AR, gaming, and content creation. By fixing the fidelity degradation that occurs when the target view deviates far from the input, Spackle makes hybrid 3DGS-diffusion systems more practical for large-view-deviation rendering without sacrificing inference efficiency. The core insight is that a fixed Gaussian budget forces resources to shift from visible to newly disoccluded areas, degrading fidelity of the original scene; Spackle instead adds a residual set of Gaussians only where reconstruction is poor. The framework is described as lightweight and efficiency-preserving, but the abstract does not report specific quantitative metrics, dataset names, or runtime numbers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Novel view synthesis (NVS) aims to render photorealistic images of a scene from viewpoints not present in the input images. 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) represents a scene as a cloud of 3D Gaussians that can be rendered in real time, while diffusion models can hallucinate plausible content in regions never observed. Hybrid decoupled frameworks combine both — 3DGS for geometry and diffusion for occluded regions — but with only a single input image, the number of Gaussians is limited, creating the capacity competition this paper targets.

## [PORL Combines Online Pretraining and Offline RL for Job Shop Scheduling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30948) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Pretrained Offline Reinforcement Learning (PORL), a hybrid method that first learns a general scheduling policy through online interaction in simulation and then adapts it offline to production-specific data using a KL-divergence-based policy constraint. Evaluated on Job Shop Scheduling Problem instances with distribution shift and datasets from heuristic, noisy-expert, and random behavioral policies, PORL consistently achieves lower optimality gaps than standalone offline RL and general scheduling baselines. The work targets the simulation-to-reality gap that plagues reinforcement learning for industrial scheduling, where direct online exploration is often impractical or costly. By showing that offline adaptation of a pretrained policy is robust even when offline data quality is poor, it offers a practical path for deploying RL in real production scheduling environments. The method uses a KL-divergence-based policy constraint to limit deviations from the pretrained policy during offline fine-tuning, and its advantage over standalone offline RL grows as dataset quality decreases, indicating reduced sensitivity to data quality and coverage. The evaluation covers JSSP instances with distribution shift and three types of behavioral policies: heuristic, noisy-expert, and random.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: The Job Shop Scheduling Problem (JSSP) is a classic combinatorial optimization problem in which a set of jobs, each consisting of ordered operations that must be processed on specific machines, are scheduled to minimize the total makespan. Offline reinforcement learning learns policies from a fixed, static dataset of previously collected transitions without interacting with the environment, but its performance depends heavily on dataset quality and coverage. Online RL, by contrast, can explore but typically requires a simulator, creating a simulation-to-reality gap when deployed in production.

## [FLIP: Final-Layer Inference-Time Probing for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30993) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce FLIP, a final-layer inference-time probe that applies elementwise flooring to the final normalized hidden state of an open-weight vision-language model before logit computation, leaving parameters, prompts, and decoding untouched. On a controlled detection/counting task, sweeping intervention strength reveals three regimes — negligible change, a bounded interior regime where detection recall at IoU 0.50 improves while tolerant counting error falls, and over-suppression — and the authors formalize a four-criterion probe-and-sweep protocol to discipline interpretation of these effects. This work offers a validation step for intervention-based mechanistic interpretability, helping researchers distinguish genuine task-linked computation from generic output instability or degradation when probing vision-language models. It matters for AI/ML researchers working on model interpretability and multimodal systems, though its immediate practical impact is moderate given its niche, diagnostic nature. The post-normalization state passed to the output head is the logit-facing instantiation of the test, and under a non-targeted flooring sweep it satisfies the full four-criterion protocol. Raw decoder-layer interventions, including the last-block output before final normalization, and the singleton-pair left/right control fail to reproduce the Final-site signature, while same-site operators and multiple VLMs replicate it; FLIP is thus a validation step for interpretability, not a steering method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that jointly interpret and generate information from both images and text, extending text-only large language models; examples include GPT-4V, Gemini, Claude 3 Opus, and open-source models such as LLaVA and InstructBLIP. Inference-time intervention is a family of techniques that modulate model activations during inference without altering core parameters, often used to improve reliability or elicit truthful answers. Mechanistic interpretability seeks to understand how internal computations produce behavior, but behavioral changes under internal intervention are mechanistically ambiguous — they may reflect better use of visual evidence, generic instability, or outright degradation, which is the problem FLIP addresses.
