## [Pocket-STVG: A Lightweight Cascade Architecture for Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31135) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Pocket-STVG (P-STVG) is a lightweight cascade architecture for spatio-temporal video grounding that combines efficient pre-trained components—a MobileViCLIP-based temporal-aware video encoder, an MDETR-derived spatial encoder-decoder, and a shared aligned text encoder—instead of large end-to-end models. It uses either a lightweight 1D U-Net or a simple thresholding strategy for temporal localization, allowing the same framework to operate in both weakly supervised and zero-shot settings with fewer than 90M parameters. This work shows that STVG can be performed with a fraction of the memory and computational cost of existing methods, achieving performance on par with weakly supervised approaches and improving on earlier zero-shot methods. It lowers the barrier for deploying video grounding on resource-constrained devices and for indexing large-scale video collections. The architecture precomputes video representations independently of the query, yielding an indexing-friendly pipeline for efficient inference. Despite using fewer than 90M parameters, P-STVG matches weakly supervised methods and improves on earlier zero-shot approaches, though the abstract lacks detailed benchmark comparisons and the work is an incremental improvement rather than a breakthrough.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding (STVG) is the task of localizing a spatio-temporal tube—a sequence of bounding boxes over time—in a video that corresponds to a natural language query. Recent methods achieve strong results in fully supervised, weakly supervised, and zero-shot settings, but often rely on computationally expensive architectures, complex training pipelines, or multimodal large language models. MobileViCLIP is an efficient video-text model designed for mobile devices, and MDETR is a modulated detection model for end-to-end multi-modal understanding.

## [FedHisto-PAST v2: Stain-Aware Federated Learning for Lung Histopathology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31150) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FedHisto-PAST v2 combines a frozen HIBOU-B pathology foundation model with parameter-efficient adaptation, stain-conditioned paired-view prediction, reliability-aware prototype learning, and adaptive federated aggregation for three-way classification of adenocarcinoma, Normal, and squamous cell carcinoma. On the external LungHist700 cohort it reached a Macro-F1 of 0.728560 and balanced accuracy of 0.730454 while updating only 1.253841% of model parameters. The work targets practical obstacles in cross-site medical AI—stain variation, non-IID client data, missing classes, and the high cost of adapting large pathology encoders—by showing that a stain-aware, parameter-efficient federated framework can generalize to an external cohort. This matters for hospitals and research groups that want to train on distributed, privacy-sensitive histopathology data without sharing raw slides or paying the full cost of fine-tuning billion-parameter foundation models. Internal evaluation on a five-client non-IID simulation hit near-ceiling performance, which limited discrimination on the fixed split, and only prediction-level consistency showed a clearly supported independent contribution in the external ablation. The authors explicitly note that the results are exploratory and do not establish formal privacy, patient-level independence, prospective deployment, or clinical validation, and that higher Normal and SCC recognition came at the cost of lower ACA recall with imperfect calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Histopathology slides are stained with dyes such as hematoxylin and eosin, and differences in staining protocols and scanners across laboratories create color shifts that can degrade AI models trained at one site and deployed at another. Federated learning lets multiple institutions train a shared model without centralizing patient data, but client datasets are typically non-IID and may lack some diagnostic classes. Foundation models like HIBOU-B are large pretrained pathology encoders, and parameter-efficient fine-tuning adapts them by updating only a small fraction of weights, reducing communication and compute costs.

## [Teacher-Anchored Selection of Quantized Models Under Domain Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31155) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31155) studies label-free selection among post-training quantized model candidates when the target domain differs from the source, finding that minimum teacher distortion acts almost as a constant rule that always picks the same 8-bit per-channel unclipped configuration, while confidence-based estimators can order the family nearly backwards in an overconfident-collapse regime. Across 134 candidate families (one per independently trained CNN or Vision Transformer teacher), teacher anchoring reduced mean selection regret at the smallest label budget in every setting, though the advantage fades beyond 25 labels. Deploying compressed models under domain shift is common in practice, yet choosing which quantized candidate to ship usually assumes target labels that are unavailable or expensive. This work shows that a simple teacher-relative anchor is a stable and often better label-free criterion than confidence-based estimators, which matters for anyone doing post-training quantization in production where target data is unlabeled. The paper gives exact quadratic identities for a canonical quadratic analogue of the candidate family, and proves that under symmetric corruption the label-dependent part of any criterion linear in the label indicator is multiplied by one common factor when its coefficient sums are candidate-invariant — a class that includes teacher contrasts and accuracy but not cross-entropy. Notably, the analysis characterizes the score's components without bounding selection regret, and the diagnostics that identify the overconfident-collapse regime themselves require the labels the setting denies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training quantization compresses an already-trained model into lower-precision formats (e.g., 8-bit) to cut memory and latency, and a single trained model can yield a whole family of such deployment candidates. Domain shift means the target data distribution differs from the source data used in training, which can change which compressed candidate is actually best. Label-free model selection tries to rank candidates without target labels, typically using proxies such as agreement with a teacher model or prediction confidence.

## [ReG-SAM Uses Reference Graphs to Boost SAM Vessel Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31160) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose ReG-SAM, a SAM-based framework for 2D vessel segmentation that introduces graph prompt embeddings (GPEs) and vascular prototype embeddings (VPEs) derived from a modality-wise vascular database. Experiments across 19 datasets show it consistently outperforms existing baselines, including those using manual prompts, especially on thin vessels. Vessel segmentation is critical for clinical diagnosis and treatment planning, yet existing deep learning models rarely generalize across anatomies and imaging modalities. By adapting SAM with graph-driven representations, ReG-SAM moves toward a foundational vessel segmentor that could reduce the need for manual prompts and per-task retraining. GPEs encode global spatial features from graphs, while VPEs capture fine-grained modality-specific vessel characteristics from multi-scale feature maps and vascular masks. Because ground-truth masks are unavailable at inference, the authors build a modality-wise vascular database and two reference graph-guided learning schemes to estimate these embeddings from database samples instead.

**Background**: The Segment Anything Model (SAM) is a foundation model for image segmentation that has shown promise in medical imaging but struggles with fine-grained vascular structures because its original design does not fully exploit vascular morphology. Graphs naturally represent vascular morphology, making them a suitable basis for vessel segmentation prompts that carry both structural and modality-specific information.

A new arXiv preprint (arXiv:2609.31166v1) proposes AgentRecommender, a method that leverages the investigation capability and internal knowledge of LLM agents to let users build customizable recommender systems without requiring additional data. The approach aims to give users their own recommendation tools rather than relying on platform-controlled algorithms. Platform-centric recommender systems can promote clickbait, filter bubbles, and fake news because they optimize for platform lock-in rather than user interests. The method relies on the LLM agent's investigation capability and internal knowledge to flexibly construct a recommender without additional data, which addresses a key barrier in prior user-side recommender research. As an arXiv preprint, it has not yet undergone peer review, and its practical performance and scalability remain to be validated.

A new arXiv paper (2609.31169) introduces Metric-based Loss Weighting, a training method that increases the loss for tokens that benefit from accompanying images, identified via Point-wise Cross-mutual Information (PCXMI) and a newly proposed Congruency-based PCXMI metric. Fine-tuning three pretrained Multimodal Large Language Models on image-guided machine translation across three language directions improved accuracy on the CoMMuTE contrastive dataset by up to more than 7 percentage points over standard fine-tuning. Multimodal LLMs often ignore visual input even when it is available, so this work offers a concrete training-time technique to make models actually use images, which could improve translation of ambiguous text and inform broader multimodal alignment research. The gains on a contrastive benchmark suggest the approach is a practical complement to architectural fusion methods. The method combines two metrics: the original PCXMI, which compares output probabilities with and without visual context, and a new Congruency-based PCXMI; the paper reports that using both together yields the best results. Evaluation covers three pretrained MLLMs and three language directions, and the authors note that strong general translation performance is maintained alongside the contrastive accuracy gains.

**Background**: Multimodal Machine Translation (MMT) aims to use non-textual signals such as images to resolve ambiguities in translation, but models trained with multimodal fusion can learn to ignore the image and rely only on text. Visual grounding refers to the model's ability to actually attend to and use relevant visual information. Loss weighting is a common training technique in which different examples or tokens receive different weights in the loss function, and PCXMI is a metric that measures how much a token's probability changes when visual context is added.

Researchers propose UniAR, a unified framework that uses a large multimodal model to generate hierarchical diagnostic descriptions at the word, phrase, and sentence levels, compensating for scarce paired clinical reports in autism spectrum disorder (ASD) recognition. A Mixture-of-Experts-based Multi-Scale Alignment Module then aligns these generated semantics with vector-quantized visual prototypes, achieving average accuracies of 75.9% on brain MRI benchmarks and 91.6% on facial expression benchmarks. ASD diagnosis typically depends on scarce expert-annotated clinical text, so UniAR's ability to synthesize hierarchical diagnostic descriptions from a multimodal model could reduce reliance on expensive labeled reports. Its gains of 1.5 percentage points on MRI benchmarks and 1.2 percentage points on facial benchmarks over state-of-the-art baselines suggest a practical path toward more robust and interpretable ASD screening. The framework's Mixture-of-Experts module dynamically matches vector-quantized visual prototypes with semantic representations at corresponding granularities, and experiments span four benchmarks covering brain MRI and facial expression scenarios. The abstract does not report sensitivity, specificity, or cross-site generalization, so clinical robustness beyond these benchmarks remains unverified.

**Background**: Autism Spectrum Disorder is a complex neurodevelopmental disorder where early and accurate diagnosis improves long-term developmental outcomes, but existing automated recognition methods often rely mainly on visual analysis because paired diagnostic text is scarce. Multi-granularity prompt learning decomposes prompts into distinct levels such as word, phrase, and sentence, giving finer control over how a model adapts to a task. Vector quantization discretizes continuous visual features into compact prototype codes, a technique long used in bag-of-visual-words recognition systems and more recently in visual tokenizers for multimodal models.

## [VLA-Dreamer Concept Trains World Models on VLA Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31313) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv concept paper (2609.31313) proposes VLA-Dreamer, an architecture that trains a predictive world model directly on the embedding space of a Vision-Language-Action model's vision encoder rather than on pixels. The authors frame it as an investigation into whether VLA embeddings are action-relevant and sufficient for future prediction, with the trained world model also usable for short-term planning by sampling actions conditioned on goal images. VLA models currently demand massive amounts of high-quality imitation learning data and lack an explicit world model, which casts doubt on their control capabilities. If embeddings prove predictive, this approach could reduce data requirements and add planning ability at inference time, potentially influencing how future robot-learning systems are designed. Unlike standard world models whose loss is computed in pixel space, VLA-Dreamer computes its loss in the embedding space, similar to joint embedding predictive architectures (JEPA). The paper is explicitly a concept proposal with no empirical results yet, and its central hypothesis is that failure to predict the future from these embeddings would expose a key limitation of VLA architectures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models map camera images plus a language instruction directly to robot actions, unifying perception, language understanding, and control in one model. They are typically trained by imitation learning, which requires large volumes of synchronized vision-language-action trajectories. World models are learned simulators of environment dynamics that let an agent predict the consequences of actions, and prior work such as JEPA shows that predicting in a learned embedding space can be more efficient than predicting raw pixels.

## [ECO Postprocessing Layer Boosts Closed-Loop Driving Scores](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31383) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Endpoint-Constrained Optimization (ECO), a lightweight postprocessing layer that anchors predicted trajectories to the vehicle's executed history, preserves the policy's predicted endpoint, and reshapes intermediate waypoints to improve physical feasibility. Across two closed-loop simulators, ECO improved the aggregate closed-loop score of all six evaluated generative and regression-based driving policies, boosting VaVAM from 18.1 to 31.0 HD-Score (+71%) on HUGSIM and increasing scene scores of VaVAM and DiffusionDrive by 123% and 22% on AlpaSim. This work addresses a practical open-loop/closed-loop mismatch in end-to-end driving models, showing that repairing intermediate trajectory geometry without changing the predicted endpoint can substantially improve closed-loop performance. The approach is model-agnostic, requires no map, privileged simulator state, or additional training, and can be inserted between a broad range of waypoint-emitting policies and their controllers, making it directly applicable to deployed autonomous driving systems. ECO is a postprocessing layer that requires no map, privileged simulator state, or additional training, and its gains tend to increase with how often the base plans violate motion limits. The paper identifies that waypoint-based supervision and displacement metrics do not ensure the intermediate trajectory is physically coherent or easy for the controller to track, with inconsistencies concentrating primarily at intermediate waypoints while the predicted endpoint remains comparatively reliable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: End-to-end driving policies are commonly trained through open-loop behavior cloning, where the model learns to imitate expert trajectories from recorded data, but they must ultimately operate in closed-loop when deployed on a vehicle, creating a fundamental mismatch between training and execution. Beyond the commonly studied effects of covariate shift and causal confusion, this paper identifies a complementary factor for the open-loop/closed-loop gap: waypoint-based supervision and displacement metrics do not ensure that the intermediate trajectory is physically coherent or easy for the controller to track.

A new arXiv paper (2609.31397) introduces Intent2Tc, a closed-loop language-model framework that converts high-level traffic-shaping intents into validated, executable Linux tc configurations. Evaluated on 100 RFC 9315-compliant intents, Claude Sonnet-4.6 achieved 0.98 semantic similarity, 1.0 semantic unit coverage, and 0.045 normalized edit distance, while RAG let compact models like Phi-4-mini approach much larger models. Automating the translation from business-level intents to deployable network policies could significantly reduce the complexity and error-proneness of QoS enforcement in intent-based networking. If validated further, this approach could make network automation more accessible and reliable for operators who currently rely on manual, expert-driven tc configuration. The framework combines an Active Queue Management (AQM)-based digital twin semantic model, automated metadata extraction, critique-driven refinement, and Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) for knowledge reuse. RAG also reduced token consumption and inference latency, though the work remains an early-stage preprint without community validation.

## [Uncertainty vs. Loss Signals for Detecting Corruption in Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31454) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper compares input-conditional uncertainty and prediction-label loss as detection signals for two corruption modes in federated learning: additive image noise and persistent random label flips. On ResNet-20 with CIFAR-10 and SVHN under non-IID Dirichlet partitions, prediction-label loss achieves within-client per-sample AUC of 0.85 (CIFAR-10) and 0.95 (SVHN) for label flips, while all uncertainty estimators stay at chance (0.49–0.50); for image noise, expected-entropy uncertainty rises to 0.67 (CIFAR-10) and 0.66 (SVHN), with loss comparable at 0.64 on both. The findings suggest that robust federated learning data-quality assessment should match the detection signal to the corruption type rather than relying on uncertainty alone, which has direct implications for how practitioners design monitoring and filtering pipelines in privacy-preserving distributed training. This matters for anyone deploying FL systems where client data may be noisy or mislabeled, since choosing the wrong signal could leave a corruption mode undetected. The uncertainty signal is characterized using a learned aleatoric variance estimate together with Monte Carlo (MC) dropout variance and entropy measures, while the loss is computed against the supplied label. The pattern for label flips is consistent with the model remaining confident in the underlying image despite the supplied label being wrong, and the expected-entropy uncertainty advantage for image noise becomes more apparent as federation-wide corruption prevalence increases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning (FL) trains a shared model across many clients without centralizing their data, but client data can be corrupted either in inputs (e.g., image noise) or in labels (e.g., random flips). Non-IID data, often simulated with Dirichlet partitions, means different clients hold different data distributions, which complicates detection. Uncertainty estimation techniques such as MC dropout and aleatoric variance quantify how unsure a model is, while prediction-label loss measures disagreement between the model's prediction and the given label; this paper asks which signal better exposes which corruption.

## [First Systematic Evaluation of LLM Cultural Awareness in Haitian Creole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31506) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents the first systematic evaluation of cultural awareness in large language models for Haitian Creole, using a native-speaker-curated benchmark of culturally salient prompts in a text infilling setting. It assesses four dimensions—specificity, bias, diversity, and variation—and finds a clear gap versus higher-resource French, with recurring stereotypical portrayals of Haitian characters in story generation. This work highlights how LLMs fail not only in task performance but also in capturing the cultural norms of underrepresented communities, which is critical for fairness and low-resource language NLP. It provides a publicly available benchmark and evaluation framework that can guide more culturally aware model development for Haitian Creole and other marginalized languages. The benchmark uses culturally salient prompts curated by native speakers in a text infilling format, and results show Haitian Creole performance is more uneven across domains and more affected by French linguistic interference than French. Story generation reveals that even positive characterizations, such as hardship and resilience, can encode stereotypical narratives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Haitian Creole is spoken by millions but is severely underrepresented in digital resources, making it a low-resource language in NLP. Text infilling is a task where a model predicts missing spans of text consistent with surrounding context, and linguistic interference refers to the influence of one language on another in multilingual models.

## [PBOS: Preference-Based Opponent Shaping for Differentiable Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03072) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose Preference-based Opponent Shaping (PBOS), a method that introduces a preference parameter into each agent's loss function so agents can directly account for opponents' losses during strategy updates. The preference parameters are updated concurrently with strategy learning, allowing agents to adapt to both cooperative and competitive differentiable games. Existing opponent shaping methods rely on simple predictions of opponent strategy changes and lack modeling of behavioral preferences like cooperation and competition, limiting them to predefined scenarios. PBOS addresses this generalization gap, which could improve multi-agent reinforcement learning in mixed cooperative-competitive settings. The preference parameter is embedded in the agent's loss function and updated alongside strategy learning, and experiments across a variety of differentiable games show PBOS guides agents to learn appropriate preference parameters and achieve better reward distribution. The work is an incremental contribution to multi-agent reinforcement learning rather than a fundamental breakthrough.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-agent games, each agent's reward depends on the joint strategy of all agents, so greedy self-interested learning can get trapped in local optima. Opponent shaping methods address this by modeling and influencing other agents' learning processes, while differentiable games are settings where agents' objectives are differentiable and can be optimized via gradient-based methods. PBOS builds on these ideas by adding preference modeling to opponent shaping.

## [Sparse Sensing Reconstructs Urban Drainage Flow with 3 Sensors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.04556) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates a data-driven sparse sensing (DSS) workflow for optimal sensor placement and flow reconstruction in a 77-node urban drainage network, achieving accurate reconstruction with only 3 sensors (4% of nodes). Using a validated SWMM parameterization, SVD-based spatial basis construction, and pivoted QR for sensor selection, the 3-node layout achieved a median system-level Nash-Sutcliffe efficiency (NSE) of 0.791 across 225 held-out simulations, with all simulations exceeding NSE = 0.700. This work demonstrates that practical constraints in time, budget, and technology for dense monitoring of urban drainage networks can be overcome with a small number of optimally placed sensors, potentially enabling cost-effective real-time flow prediction across entire networks. It provides a benchmarked, non-iterative sensor placement method that matches the performance of Greedy D-optimal and genetic algorithm approaches, which could be valuable for environmental engineering and urban water management. The pivoted QR-selected sensor sets matched the performance of reference configurations from Greedy D-optimal and genetic algorithms without requiring iterative layout searches. The framework was robust to multiplicative Gaussian noise but sensitive to sensor failures depending on location; the sensitivity to monitored node loss was associated with energy-weighted modal exposure (leave-one-node-out Spearman correlation of 0.781), which correlated with the lost node's upstream drainage area, mean adjacent circular-conduit diameter, and nodal active flow fraction.

**Background**: Urban drainage networks are increasingly stressed by urbanization and intense storms, but dense monitoring is often impractical due to cost and technology constraints. Data-driven sparse sensing (DSS) leverages the low-dimensional structure of high-dimensional system states to reconstruct full fields from a few strategically placed sensors. SWMM (Storm Water Management Model) is a widely used free model developed by the US EPA for simulating urban drainage and sewer systems. This arXiv paper (2606.05828v2) introduces a lightweight local harness that strictly decouples statistical preference learning from semantic intent parsing, using on-device statistical results to modulate the remote LLM's skill selection decisions. Evaluations show the decoupled approach achieves the lowest cumulative regret and highest test accuracy, significantly outperforming traditional memory-augmented agents. As personal agents accumulate large skill libraries, learning implicit user preferences locally becomes critical, yet local deployment constraints rule out complex centralized selection algorithms. This work offers a practical, lightweight architecture that could make personalized skill selection feasible on edge devices without heavy training infrastructure. The architecture separates a bandit-style statistical preference tracker running on-device from the remote LLM's semantic intent parsing, so the local harness only modulates selection rather than performing full centralized optimization. The paper reports cumulative regret and test accuracy as its primary metrics, though it remains a specialized preprint without independent replication.

Cumulative regret is a standard metric from multi-armed bandit theory measuring the total loss from not always choosing the optimal skill, and statistical priors refer to learned distributions over user choices that guide decisions. Decoupling means the statistical preference module and the semantic understanding module operate independently, each handling what it does best.

## [Consist-Retinex enables one-step low-light image enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.08982) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Consist-Retinex introduces a one-step noise-emphasized consistency training method for Retinex-based low-light image enhancement, using a Retinex Transformer Decomposition Network (TDN) to produce paired reflectance and illumination maps, then training two conditional consistency models with a Retinex-aware dual objective and adaptive noise-emphasized fixed-point sampling. Experiments on paired and unpaired low-light benchmarks show it achieves the best VE-LOL-L scores among compared methods under one-step inference and remains competitive on LOL, with substantially reduced sampling and consistency-stage training cost. This work addresses the instability of directly adapting consistency models to Retinex-factorized enhancement, enabling fast one-step inference without iterative sampling, which is crucial for deployment under strict latency budgets. It could benefit real-time applications like mobile photography, autonomous driving, and surveillance where low-light enhancement must be both high-quality and computationally efficient. The method provides an endpoint error bound, an anchoring-propagation result, and a high-noise sample-allocation analysis to explain why endpoint supervision and temporal consistency are complementary. The dual objective combines trajectory consistency with paired ground-truth component alignment, while the sampling rule concentrates supervision near the inference endpoint without discarding full-range noise coverage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Retinex theory, inspired by human color vision, separates an image into reflectance and illumination components, which helps in low-light enhancement by adjusting illumination while preserving reflectance. Consistency models are a class of generative models that support fast one-step generation by learning to map noise directly to data, unlike diffusion models that require iterative sampling. The Retinex Transformer Decomposition Network (TDN) uses Transformer attention to efficiently decompose images into illumination and reflectance maps, as seen in prior work like Diff-Retinex.

## [PromptCCZSL: Prompt-Based Continual Compositional Zero-Shot Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09172) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes PromptCCZSL, the first prompt-based framework for Continual Compositional Zero-Shot Learning (CCZSL), which adapts a frozen vision-language model to new attributes, objects, and compositions across sessions without forgetting prior knowledge. It combines recency-weighted multi-teacher distillation, session-aware compositional prompts, a Cosine Anchor Loss (CAL), an Orthogonal Projection Loss (OPL), and an Intra-Session Diversity Loss (IDL), and reports substantial gains over prior VLM and non-VLM baselines on the UT-Zappos and C-GQA benchmarks. This work matters because it addresses a realistic and harder setting than standard continual learning: attributes and objects can reappear across sessions while compositions remain unique, so models must both retain old knowledge and generalize to unseen combinations. It also introduces a protocol that jointly measures catastrophic forgetting and compositional generalization, which could become a useful evaluation standard for researchers working on vision-language models and continual learning. The framework keeps the VLM backbone frozen and relies on prompt learning, with session-aware compositional prompts fusing multimodal features for new compositions while attribute and object prompts use session-agnostic fusion for global semantic consistency. The Cosine Anchor Loss aligns features with fixed unit-norm class anchors via cosine similarity, OPL keeps new attribute and object embeddings distinct from previous ones, and IDL encourages diversity within the current session; experiments are limited to closed-world settings on UT-Zappos and C-GQA.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Compositional Zero-Shot Learning (CZSL) aims to recognize novel attribute-object combinations, such as 'red apple,' that never appeared together during training, by reusing learned representations of individual attributes and objects. Continual learning, by contrast, studies how models can learn new tasks sequentially without catastrophically forgetting earlier ones, and prompt learning adapts large frozen vision-language models by learning small input prompts instead of fine-tuning all parameters. Multi-teacher distillation lets one student model learn from several teacher models, and the Cosine Anchor Loss is a geometric loss that pulls features toward fixed unit-norm class anchors using cosine similarity.

<a id="item-333"></a>
## [Dynamic Pooled Testing Welfare Bound Proved](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22419) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proves that the optimal dynamic policy for budget-constrained welfare-maximizing pooled testing is worth at most twice the optimal static overlapping allocation, for any population, budget, and pool-size cap. The proof uses path sampling, random thinning, a Boolean-cube argument, and complementary-profile coupling, and also shows that Exact-Joint Greedy and Finster et al.'s non-overlapping Greedy are each a 2(e+1)-approximation to the optimal dynamic policy. This provides a tight theoretical benchmark for how much adaptivity can improve pooled testing outcomes, which matters for designing efficient testing programs in public health and other resource-constrained screening settings. It also connects pooled testing to the approximation-algorithms literature by giving a non-trivial factor-two guarantee and new approximation bounds for existing greedy heuristics. The paper identifies regimes where adaptivity has no value, shows that strict adaptive gains require re-pooling agents after positive tests, and gives a three-agent instance where adaptation is strictly beneficial. It also includes a reproducible exact small-instance study comparing dynamic and static benchmarks and greedy policies, with exploratory appendix results for Gibbs-marginal and reinforcement-learning approaches at larger scales.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Pooled testing (also called group testing) uses a single test on a combined sample to certify multiple individuals as healthy when the result is negative, a technique first studied by Robert Dorfman in 1943 and widely used in screening programs such as during the COVID-19 pandemic. Schemes where later tests depend on earlier outcomes are called adaptive, while those fixing all tests in advance are non-adaptive; this paper compares the welfare achievable by these two approaches under a budget constraint. Approximation algorithms are efficient algorithms that find provably near-optimal solutions to hard optimization problems, characterized by a multiplicative approximation ratio.

## [Spectral-Sphere-Constrained Hyper-Connections Improve Training Stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20896) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2603.20896v2) proposes Spectral-Sphere-Constrained Hyper-Connections (s²HC), which replaces the doubly stochastic constraints used in Manifold-Constrained Hyper-Connections (mHC) with a spectral norm sphere constraint on residual matrices. The authors identify three limitations of the doubly stochastic approach—identity degeneration, an expressivity bottleneck, and parameterization inefficiencies—and show that s²HC eliminates unstable Sinkhorn-Knopp iterations and factorial-scaling permutation parameterizations. Hyper-Connections and their constrained variants are an emerging technique for improving deep network training stability, and this work addresses concrete failure modes that could limit their practical adoption. By restoring free control over the subdominant spectrum while preserving stability, s²HC could enable more expressive residual mixing in large-scale models without the computational overhead of prior methods. The doubly stochastic constraint is shown to cause learned matrices to collapse around identity initialization, restrict the subdominant spectrum, and incur either unstable Sinkhorn-Knopp iterations or factorial-scaling overhead from permutation-based parameterizations. The proposed s²HC confines residual matrices to a spectral norm sphere, which the authors claim enables expressive, non-degenerate residual matrices while maintaining training stability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Residual connections, which add a layer's input to its output, are fundamental to training very deep networks because they preserve an identity mapping that helps gradients flow. Hyper-Connections (HC) generalize this idea by extending residuals into multiple parallel streams and using residual matrices to mix information across streams, increasing model expressivity. However, unconstrained mixing can break the identity mapping property and destabilize training, so Manifold-Constrained Hyper-Connections (mHC) restrict the mixing matrices to be doubly stochastic—matrices whose rows and columns each sum to one—typically enforced via the Sinkhorn-Knopp algorithm or permutation-based parameterizations.

## [Memory-Constrained Routing Games and the Recall Braess Paradox](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11733) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a design theory for routing games where travelers optimize over remembered or surfaced routes rather than a fixed exogenous action set, defining a stationary Forgetful Wardrop Equilibrium (FWE) with existence and uniqueness results. It also defines a Recall Braess Paradox, showing that improving recall can increase equilibrium delay without changing physical capacity, and proves this can arise on every two-terminal network with at least two distinct s-t paths. This work connects memory and interface design to equilibrium outcomes in transportation and communication networks, showing that how information is surfaced can be as important as physical capacity. It offers a tractable optimization layer for designing surfacing policies, which could inform navigation apps, traffic management, and network routing systems. The micro model uses finite memory states, logit choice, and LRU-style updates, while the design layer uses salience-weighted stochastic user equilibrium as the unique minimizer of a strictly convex potential. The bridge between layers is exact for last-choice memory (B=1), and for larger memories the authors develop an LRU-to-TTL-to-salience approximation pipeline with contraction-based error bounds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Routing games model how self-interested travelers choose paths in a congested network, with Wardrop equilibrium being the standard solution concept where no traveler can improve by unilaterally changing route. Classical models assume travelers know all routes, but in reality they rely on memory and interfaces that surface only a subset of alternatives. LRU (Least Recently Used) is a common cache replacement policy that evicts the least recently accessed item, and here it is adapted to model how travelers forget routes.

## [Topology-Driven MARL Framework Prevents Soft Robot Entanglement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05236) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.05236v2) proposes a Topology-Driven Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (TD-MARL) framework that coordinates multiple soft robots to avoid entanglement in highly constrained environments. The framework combines centralized learning with distributed execution and adds a topological safety layer using topological invariants to assess and mitigate entanglement risk. Entanglement is a persistent failure mode for soft robots operating in dense, cluttered spaces, and existing distributed training frameworks struggle with observability and instability in such settings. This work could improve reliability for precision manufacturing and other constrained-environment applications where multiple compliant robots must cooperate. The critical network uses centralized learning so each agent can perceive other agents' policies through shared topological states, while distributed execution removes the need for inter-robot communication. Simulation experiments reportedly show better convergence and anti-entanglement performance than current state-of-the-art deep reinforcement learning (DRL) methods.

Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) extends reinforcement learning to systems where multiple agents share an environment and must coordinate, often facing challenges like partial observability and unstable training. Topological invariants are mathematical properties preserved under continuous deformation, used here to quantify when robots risk becoming tangled.

## [ADAS: Attention-Discounted Reranking Boosts Masked Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10829) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose ADAS, a training-free reranking rule for masked diffusion language models that discounts each token's confidence score based on its attention to already-selected positions, weighted by their prediction uncertainty. Plugged into Top-k, Fast-dLLM, and EB-Sampler, ADAS improves low-NFE performance by 9.11 and 10.46 percentage points on average on reasoning (GSM8K, MATH500) and code (HumanEval, MBPP) benchmarks across LLaDA-8B-Base and Dream-7B-Base, with only 3.1% per-forward runtime overhead. Masked diffusion language models promise faster inference by revealing multiple tokens per denoising step, but naive parallel decoding can commit mutually incompatible tokens and degrade quality. ADAS addresses this coupling problem without retraining or changing the base sampler's stopping rule, making it a drop-in enhancement that could make diffusion-based text generation more practical for reasoning and code tasks. ADAS is training-free and leaves the base sampler's stopping rule unchanged, instead greedily discounting token-wise confidence by attention to already-selected positions weighted by their prediction uncertainty. The reported gains are measured at matched denoiser evaluations (low NFE), and the method adds only 3.1% per-forward runtime overhead; code is available at https://github.com/yusufsahin99/ADAS.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Masked diffusion language models (MDLMs) generate text by iteratively reversing a noising process over token masks, rather than producing tokens strictly left-to-right like autoregressive models such as GPT. This bidirectional denoising lets them reveal several tokens per iteration, reducing the number of function evaluations (NFE) needed at inference. LLaDA-8B-Base and Dream-7B-Base are two representative open masked diffusion language models at the 7-8B parameter scale. Existing training-free samplers like Top-k, Fast-dLLM, and EB-Sampler mainly decide how many tokens to reveal, often ranking candidates by token-wise scores that ignore interactions among the selected set.

## [InMyStyle: LoRA Adapters Let Small Qwen2.5 Models Rewrite Text in a User's Style](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29238) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.29238v2) introduces InMyStyle, a privacy-first single-user system that fine-tunes rank-8 LoRA adapters on Qwen2.5 models from 0.5B to 7B parameters to rewrite AI-edited text into an individual's writing style without an inference-time instruction prompt. In a case study of 219 evaluation pairs from one author's scientific writing, the automatic composite score plateaued across model sizes (Q=0.689–0.695), showing that models as small as 0.5B perform comparably to larger ones for this task. This work reinforces the trend that parameter-efficient LoRA adaptation can make personalization practical on small, locally runnable models, which matters for privacy-conscious users who cannot send their documents to cloud APIs. It also suggests that for style rewriting, scaling model size yields diminishing returns, shifting the focus to efficiency trade-offs rather than raw quality. All adapters used the same rank-8, three-epoch recipe, and length-aware generation budgets with automatic chunking handled varying input lengths. The gains favored content-preserving naturalization over recovery of personal style, with authorship probabilities staying near the classifier's decision boundary (0.51–0.53) and stylometric improvement near zero; a secondary evaluation of 400 LLM-judge ratings gave InMyStyle outputs a mean perceived AI-ness score over 20% lower than their helper-generated inputs, though the study does not establish generalization across users.

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes a pre-trained model's weights and injects small trainable rank-decomposition matrices into each Transformer layer, drastically reducing trainable parameters and memory overhead. Qwen2.5 is Alibaba's family of open foundation models spanning roughly 0.5B to hundreds of billions of parameters, commonly used for local deployment. Text style transfer aims to rewrite content while preserving meaning but changing stylistic attributes, and personalization here means adapting to one specific author rather than a general style.

## [PILOT Framework Decouples Intention from Trajectory in World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06994) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.06994v2) proposes PILOT (Physical Inference for Latent Optimized Trajectories), a Representational Deduction (RD) framework that integrates motion chain-of-thought (CoT) guidance as a native capability of World Action Models (WAMs). RD encourages the action branch to explicitly model potential state transition tokens, which are retained as CoT in the reasoning space to guide fine-grained motion trajectory generation. Existing WAMs suffer from representational entanglement between high-level physical state evolution and low-level action trajectory generation, which acts as a structural bottleneck. By decoupling intention from trajectory, PILOT reportedly improves success rate and generalization in complex robotic manipulation tasks, and its abundant state transition supervision could serve as an efficient few-shot real-robot fine-tuning strategy for mainstream WAM architectures. The RD mechanism introduces state transition supervision signals that alleviate the sparse supervision problem in action generation, and the paper claims enhanced physical interpretability by separating high-level motion semantics from low-level trajectory details. However, the provided abstract lacks quantitative experimental results, and as an arXiv preprint it has not undergone peer review.

Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning, popularized in large language models, encourages models to generate intermediate reasoning steps before producing a final answer. PILOT applies this idea to robotics by having the model reason over latent state transitions before emitting motion trajectories.

A new arXiv paper presents a progressive three-phase study of convolutional encoders and value-estimation designs within the Parallelized Q-Network (PQN) framework, resulting in the Aftab architecture. Aftab achieves an interquartile mean human-normalized score of 6.592 on Atari-57, compared to 2.715 for a rerun PQN reference, with a game-level Probability of Improvement of 0.86. This work shows that architectural choices in visual encoders and value estimation can substantially improve replay-free Q-learning, which avoids large replay buffers and target networks. The open-sourced Aftab framework and strong Atari-57 results provide a practical reference for reinforcement learning practitioners seeking efficient, stable training. The study first compares eight convolutional encoder topologies on Atari-57, then integrates Hadamax-style multiplicative feature interactions and explicit pooling, and finally compares categorical-dueling, ensemble-dueling, and categorical ensemble-dueling value-estimation configurations. On Procgen Hard, Aftab reaches a terminal IQM normalized score of 0.418 versus 0.382 for PQN and raises the normalized area under the learning curve from 0.216 to 0.541, though terminal performance remains heterogeneous across environments.

## [Unified Dynamic Face Landmark Detection via FPALPs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10346) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.10346v2) proposes Face Part-Anchored Landmark Positions (FPALPs), which represent each landmark as a progression value between 0 and 1 along a face part's contour, allowing all N-point face landmark datasets to be merged into one. Using FPALP-based queries refined by a cross-modality decoder, the resulting Unified Dynamic FLD model can train on any number of N-point datasets and output any number of landmarks by loading designated queries at runtime. Face landmark detection has long been fragmented, requiring a separate model per annotation scheme and preventing reuse across datasets; this unification could reduce training cost and enable a single deployable model to serve applications with different landmark counts. It may also make it easier to pool heterogeneous datasets for better generalization. The method expresses every landmark in FPALP format regardless of source dataset, represents each landmark with an FPALP-based query, and iteratively refines queries and coordinates through a cross-modality decoder block. Experiments on multiple benchmark datasets show it remains competitive with, and in several cases outperforms, existing state-of-the-art methods.

**Background**: Face landmark detection (FLD) predicts the coordinates of predefined facial landmarks in images, and different datasets define different numbers of landmarks and annotation schemes, such as the 468-point MediaPipe Face Mesh. Because these schemes are incompatible, models are typically trained separately for each N-point benchmark and can only output the N landmarks they were trained on. FPALPs address this by describing landmarks relative to their position along a face part's contour, making annotations comparable across datasets.

## [VolRouter Recasts Volatility Control as State-Conditioned Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10375) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.10375v2) introduces VolRouter, a modular framework that formulates volatility control as state-conditioned routing over estimator-controller pairs, using three stages: state inference, switch review, and pair selection. Evaluated across S&P 500, Multi-Asset, Bitcoin, and USDT settings, VolRouter achieves the highest Sharpe ratio in three of four cases, improving S&P 500 Sharpe from 0.952 to 1.222 while cutting maximum drawdown from 15.10% to 12.58% and daily CVaR from 1.76% to 1.32%. This work suggests that volatility control can be treated as a policy-selection problem rather than relying on a single fixed estimator or control rule, which could improve risk-adjusted returns for quantitative portfolio managers operating across heterogeneous market regimes. It also opens a path for LLM-based decision modules to be plugged into risk management pipelines while keeping portfolio actions governed by predefined control policies. Ablation and sensitivity analyses show that gains come from relative policy evaluation and selective persistent switching rather than simply expanding the policy library, and the USDT setting serves as a boundary case where simpler state-aware selectors remain competitive. The Router can be implemented with rule-based, learnable, or LLM-based decision modules, while portfolio actions remain generated by predefined control policies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Volatility control is a core technique in dynamic portfolio management that converts risk estimates into portfolio exposure, typically by scaling positions inversely to predicted volatility. Traditional implementations rely on a fixed volatility estimator (such as realized volatility) paired with a pre-defined control rule, which may fail to adapt when market conditions change. The Sharpe ratio is a standard measure of risk-adjusted return, calculated as excess portfolio return divided by portfolio standard deviation, while CVaR (conditional value at risk) measures expected loss in the worst tail of the return distribution. Routing, in machine learning, refers to dynamically selecting among multiple modules or paths based on the current state rather than committing to a single static configuration.

A new arXiv paper applies Single-Linkage Clustering (SLINK) to speaker embeddings and proposes Hierarchical Cluster-Class Matching (HCCM) to interpret the resulting hierarchical organization from an explainable AI perspective. A new arXiv paper (2609.26342v2) proposes a geometry-aware hyperbolic residual-quantized variational autoencoder that fixes two geometric inconsistencies in naive hyperbolic extensions of residual vector quantization: non-associative hyperbolic addition that prevents consistent residual aggregation, and a standard straight-through estimator that ignores latent-space geometry. The method introduces Hyperbolic Residual Aggregation in the forward pass to restore telescoping behavior on the Poincaré ball, and a discounted Hyperbolic Straight-Through Estimator in the backward pass that routes reconstruction gradients through the quantizer as a single geometric block. This work matters because hyperbolic geometry is a natural fit for hierarchically organized data, yet naive hyperbolic quantization has been unstable; the proposed fixes could make hyperbolic discrete latent spaces practical for hierarchical prediction, recommendation, image tokenization, and neural audio coding. It also clarifies a structure-compression trade-off, showing that Euclidean residual quantization remains preferable for pure compression while geometry-aware hyperbolic quantization excels for hierarchically organized discrete latent spaces. The forward pass restores the telescoping behavior of residual quantization on the Poincaré ball, while the backward pass avoids unstable recursive gradient transport across residual stages by treating the quantizer as a single geometric block. Evaluations across hierarchical prediction, recommendation, image tokenization, and neural audio coding show improved stability and structural organization of hyperbolic residual codes over naive hyperbolic baselines, though the authors note a clear structure-compression trade-off.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Residual Vector Quantization (RVQ) turns continuous representations into discrete, multi-level token sequences by sequentially summing codewords from multiple codebooks, and it is widely used in neural audio codecs. Hyperbolic geometry, which describes spaces of constant negative curvature such as the Poincaré ball, is often used in machine learning to represent hierarchical or tree-like data more efficiently than Euclidean space. However, extending RVQ to hyperbolic space is tricky because hyperbolic addition is non-associative, so residual aggregation is not consistent, and because the straight-through estimator (STE) commonly used to backpropagate through non-differentiable quantization ignores the geometry of the latent space.

## [Hierarchical GNNs with Kron Transports Improve Power Flow Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26603) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces hierarchical latent communication into the GENCO graph neural network for power flow, replacing two local correction steps with two reduced graphs that exchange information via Kron-derived transports. Trained with roughly 100x fewer optimizer updates than GENCO's reference protocol, the Kron variant achieves a macro family-balanced voltage error of 0.851±0.110, 51.3% below a per-bus mean baseline and 85.0% below the flat GENCO architecture. Power flow modeling underpins grid planning, contingency analysis, and real-time operation, so a model that generalizes across operating scenarios with one shared parameter set and far less training could make AI-based grid tools more practical. The result also shows that embedding physics-derived structure (Kron reduction) into GNN hierarchies is a promising direction for physics-informed machine learning in power systems. The study compares Kron-derived transports against a same-anchor Quotient construction and the flat GENCO baseline under one protocol: 200 epochs on three grid topologies, fewer than 1,900 training scenarios per grid, and three initialization seeds, evaluated on 200 fresh preselected scenarios per grid. Kron beats the fitted reference on 98.5% of 600 fresh scenarios and is 31.0% below Quotient, but on two topologies unseen in training the current models do not yet outperform the fitted reference in calibrated transfer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Graph neural networks (GNNs) learn on graph-structured data such as power grids, where buses are nodes and transmission lines are edges, and hierarchical GNNs add multi-scale representations by grouping nodes into coarser graphs. Kron reduction is a classical technique that eliminates internal nodes from an electrical network while preserving its terminal behavior, producing a smaller equivalent graph. GENCO is a GNN architecture for power flow that applies local correction steps; this paper replaces some of those steps with hierarchical communication over Kron-reduced graphs to improve generalization to new operating scenarios.

## [Gödel's and Scott's Ontological Argument Ported to Lean 4 and TPTP THF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26806) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper presents a complete, structure-preserving port to Lean 4 of the Isabelle/HOL dataset for Gödel's ontological argument and Scott's variant, covering 30 modules and 548 statements certified identical by a comparison tool. It re-proves every named result, including the inconsistency of Gödel's 1970 axioms, modal collapse, monotheism, and the ultrafilter property of positive properties, and also renders the dataset in TPTP THF and SMT-LIB. This work provides a cross-system, machine-checked bridge between Isabelle/HOL, Lean 4, and the TPTP/SMT automated theorem proving ecosystem for a well-known philosophical argument, making the formalization more accessible and independently verifiable. It also demonstrates how Lean 4's explicit proof terms and #print axioms can yield precise upper bounds on the modal logic required by each result. Because Lean 4 lacks a sledgehammer and model finder, automated proofs become explicit proof terms and the 65 Nitpick invocations are documentation; the 45 statements refuted or left open are anonymous sorrys nothing depends on. Six provers (E, Vampire, Zipperposition, cvc5, Leo-II, Leo-III) prove 227 of the 294 theorems within ten seconds on one core and 231 within sixty, with Leo-II repaired and released as 2.2.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 28, 04:00

**Background**: Lean 4 is a proof assistant and functional programming language based on the Calculus of Inductive Constructions, while Isabelle/HOL is a higher-order logic proof assistant with tools like the sledgehammer proof search and the Nitpick counterexample finder. TPTP THF is a higher-order logic format for automated theorem provers, and it was the format in which Gödel's argument was first mechanised. Gödel's ontological argument and Scott's variant are formal modal-logic proofs of God's existence, and Benzmüller and Scott previously studied them in Isabelle/HOL.

Ozon Tech held its E-CODE conference for Programmer's Day, and the ML/DS track recordings are now available, covering Ozon's recommendation system and a computer vision pipeline for measuring product dimensions. The company is gradually migrating different pipeline stages, from candidate selection to final ranking, to neural network models, and it also detailed a Large User Model architecture that aggregates user signals including negative feedback. The talks offer a rare look at how a large e-commerce marketplace productionizes ML across both recommendation and physical logistics, showing that neural approaches are being adopted end-to-end rather than in isolated experiments. This is relevant to ML engineers and data scientists building real-world pipelines where accuracy, cost, and throughput must be balanced simultaneously. The recommendation system must jointly account for sales, advertising, engagement, and LTV, while the dimensioning pipeline uses a stereo camera, segmentation, and 3D reconstruction on a dataset of over 100,000 images, with Grounding DINO and SAM2 used for semi-automatic labeling. Products move at up to 1 m/s, so processing speed matters as much as accuracy, and the team compared ML approaches against classical and point-cloud-based methods on quality, cost, and performance.

**Background**: Recommendation systems use machine learning to predict user preferences from past behavior such as clicks, views, and purchases, and modern systems increasingly use neural models across multiple stages. Together they enable semi-automatic labeling of large image datasets without fully manual annotation.
